## What was wrong and what was done

### Corollary 3.19 — corrected

- **As printed.** \label{cor:normality} Suppose that the assumptions of \cref{thm:tikhonv_rate} hold for both $h_0$ and $q_0$ with (potentially different) source conditions $\beta_h,\beta_q>0$. Choose a deterministic sequence $\xi_n\downarrow0$, and run the primal and dual tuning rules at confidence levels $\xi_n/2$. Let a common deterministic sequence $\delta_n$ satisfy \eqref{eq:delta_trae} for both problems at these confidence levels. Assume that, for some $\gamma_h,\gamma_q>0$, the score maps satisfy \begin{align*} \mathbb{E}\left[\left(\tilde{m}( W;h_{1}) -\tilde{m}( W;h_{2})\right)^{2}\right] & \leqslant C\| h_{1} -h_{2} \| ^{\gamma_h },\\ \mathbb{E}\left[\left(m( W;q_{1}) -m( W;q_{2})\right)^{2}\right] & \leqslant C\| q_{1} -q_{2} \| ^{\gamma_q } \end{align*} for all $h_1,h_2\in\mathcal H$ and $q_1,q_2\in\mathcal Q$. Let $\beta_m=\max\{\beta_h,\beta_q\}$ and assume that $\delta_n$ satisfies \[ \delta _{n} =o\left( n^{-\alpha _{\mathrm{dr}}}\right) ,\qquad \alpha _{\mathrm{dr}} :=\frac{1+\min\{\beta _{m} ,1\}}{2+4\min\{\beta _{m} ,1\}} . \] Let $\hat h,\hat q$ be the adaptive adversarial Tikhonov estimators, trained and tuned independently of the evaluation sample used in $\hat\theta(\hat h,\hat q)$. Finally, assume that $0<\sigma_0^2:=\mathbb E[\psi_0(W)^2]<\infty$. Then, \[ \sqrt{n}(\hat{\theta }(\hat{h},\hat{q}) -\theta _{0}) =\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum _{i=1}^{n} \psi _{0}( W_i) +o_{p}( 1) , \] where $\psi _{0}( W) =\tilde{m}( W;h_{0}) +m( W;q_{0}) -q_{0}( Z) h_{0}( X) -\theta _{0}$. Consequently, $\sqrt n(\hat\theta(\hat h,\hat q)-\theta_0)\rightsquigarrow N(0,\sigma_0^2)$.
- **What is wrong.** ``For completeness, define the uncentered score \[ S(W;h,q)=\tilde m(W;h)+m(W;q)-h(X)q(Z), \qquad d_n(W)=S(W;\hat h,\hat q)-S(W;h_0,q_0).\mbox{''} \] Defining the fitted score difference sectionwise does not ensure a jointly measurable version coherent with both its conditional population integrals and its evaluations on the finite evaluation sample.
- **Counterexample.** Let $U$ and $V$ be independent uniform random variables on $[0,1]$, realized on the uncompleted Borel product probability space, with $U$ representing training information and $V$ a fresh observation. Let $A\subset[0,1]$ be a non-Borel set. Consider the sectionwise representatives $g(u,v)=\mathbf 1_A(u)\mathbf 1_{\{0\}}(v)$ and $\bar g(u,v)=0$, which represent the same zero population-$L_2$ function for every fixed $u$. Every fixed-training section of $g$ is measurable and has zero population-$L_2$ norm, but $g(U,V)$ is not measurable on this product space because its nonzero set is $A\times\{0\}$; choosing the coherent measurable version $\bar g$ resolves this version ambiguity.
- **Correction.** Suppose that the assumptions of \cref{thm:tikhonv_rate} hold for both $h_0$ and $q_0$ with (potentially different) source conditions $\beta_h,\beta_q>0$. Choose a deterministic sequence $\xi_n\downarrow0$, and run the primal and dual tuning rules at confidence levels $\xi_n/2$. Let a common deterministic sequence $\delta_n$ satisfy \eqref{eq:delta_trae} for both problems at these confidence levels. Assume that, for some $\gamma_h,\gamma_q>0$, the score maps satisfy \begin{align*} \mathbb{E}\left[\left(\tilde{m}( W;h_{1}) -\tilde{m}( W;h_{2})\right)^{2}\right] & \leqslant C\| h_{1} -h_{2} \| ^{\gamma_h },\\ \mathbb{E}\left[\left(m( W;q_{1}) -m( W;q_{2})\right)^{2}\right] & \leqslant C\| q_{1} -q_{2} \| ^{\gamma_q } \end{align*} for all $h_1,h_2\in\mathcal H$ and $q_1,q_2\in\mathcal Q$. Let $\beta_m=\max\{\beta_h,\beta_q\}$ and assume that $\delta_n$ satisfies \[ \delta _{n} =o\left( n^{-\alpha _{\mathrm{dr}}}\right) ,\qquad \alpha _{\mathrm{dr}} :=\frac{1+\min\{\beta _{m} ,1\}}{2+4\min\{\beta _{m} ,1\}} . \] Let $\hat h,\hat q$ be the adaptive adversarial Tikhonov estimators, trained and tuned independently of the evaluation sample used in $\hat\theta(\hat h,\hat q)$. For each $n$, write $\mathcal G_n$ for the full nuisance-training and tuning sigma-field. Take jointly measurable versions of the fitted nuisance evaluations, representing the fitted functions for almost every training realization and agreeing almost surely with their evaluations at every observation in the finite evaluation sample. Write \[ S(w;h,q)=\tilde m(w;h)+m(w;q)-h(X(w))q(Z(w)),\qquad d_n(w)=S(w;\hat h,\hat q)-S(w;h_0,q_0). \] Suppose that, for each $n$, $d_n$ admits a version jointly measurable in $\mathcal G_n$ and a fresh observation, agreeing with $d_n$ for almost every training realization and almost every fresh observation, and agreeing almost surely with $d_n(W_i)$ simultaneously for $i=1,\ldots,n$ in the evaluation sample. Finally, assume that $0<\sigma_0^2:=\mathbb E[\psi_0(W)^2]<\infty$. Then, \[ \sqrt{n}(\hat{\theta }(\hat{h},\hat{q}) -\theta _{0}) =\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum _{i=1}^{n} \psi _{0}( W_i) +o_{p}( 1) , \] where $\psi _{0}( W) =\tilde{m}( W;h_{0}) +m( W;q_{0}) -q_{0}( Z) h_{0}( X) -\theta _{0}$. Consequently, $\sqrt n(\hat\theta(\hat h,\hat q)-\theta_0)\rightsquigarrow N(0,\sigma_0^2)$.
- **Proof.** Apply \cref{thm:tikhonv_rate} to both nuisance estimators at the prescribed confidence levels $\xi_n/2$. Their rate bounds then hold on a common event with probability at least $1-\xi_n\to1$. Because $\beta_h,\beta_q>0$ and $\delta_n\to0$, these bounds give \begin{equation}\label{eq:conv_hq} \| \hat{h} -h_{0} \| =o_{p}( 1) ,\qquad \| \hat{q} -q_{0}\| =o_{p}( 1) . \end{equation} The remaining bias condition is \begin{equation}\label{eq:square_root} \sqrt{n}\mathbb{E}[(\hat{q}( Z) -q_{0}( Z))(\hat{h}( X) -h_{0}( X))] =o_{p}( 1) . \end{equation} Suppose first that $\beta_m=\beta_h\geqslant\beta_q$. ### Lemma B.1 — statement unchanged; proof corrected

- **As printed.** \label{lemma:t_err_dp} Under \cref{asp:re_cd,asp:cr_deepiv}, with probability at least $1-\xi$, \begin{align} \mathbb E_Z\!\left[H^2\{\hat g(\cdot\mid Z),g_0(\cdot\mid Z)\}\right] &\leqslant C\delta_{n,\mathcal G}^2, \label{eq:rdiv_mle_hellinger}\\ \|\hat r-r_0\|_{L_2(Z)}&\leqslant \delta_{n,r},\nonumber\\ \sup_{h\in\mathcal H} \|(\hat{\mathcal T}-\mathcal T)h\|_{L_2(Z)} &\leqslant C\delta_{n,\mathcal G},\label{eq:rdiv_op_global}\\ \|(\hat{\mathcal T}-\mathcal T)(h-h')\|_{L_1(Z)} &\leqslant C\delta_{n,\mathcal G}\|h-h'\|, \qquad h,h'\in\mathcal H.\label{eq:rdiv_op_relative} \end{align} Moreover, if \[ \widehat L(h):=\mathbb E[(Y-\hat{\mathcal T}h(Z))^2\mid I_{\mathrm{pre}}], \] then, uniformly over $h,h'\in\mathcal H$, \begin{equation}\label{eq:rdiv_first_stage_loss} |\{\widehat L(h)-\widehat L(h')\}-\{L(h)-L(h')\}| \leqslant C\delta_{n,\mathcal G}\|h-h'\|. \end{equation}
- **What is wrong.** ``\dots applied with $g_0\in\mathcal G$ and the critical radius of $\mathcal G_0$, gives \eqref{eq:rdiv_mle_hellinger} with probability at least $1-\xi/2$.'' This step uses the conditional-MLE bound of \citet[Theorem~24]{li2024regularized}, which the paper assumes only for the density lower bound and the conditions of the source's referenced RDIV theorem, including its source condition, but applies it to a model satisfying only Assumptions~\ref{asp:re_cd} and~\ref{asp:cr_deepiv}. In the step quoted in (a): The cited theorem states an exponential confidence formula without a negative exponent, and its proof gives $1-\exp(n\delta_n^2)$, which is nonpositive and does not imply the asserted probability $1-\xi/2$.
- **Counterexample.** Let $\mu$ be Lebesgue measure on $[0,1]$, let $Z$ be uniform on $[0,1]$, and let $\phi_j$ be the mean-zero orthonormal Rademacher functions. Set $\sigma_j=2^{-j^2-2}$ and $g_0(x\mid z)=1+\sum_{j\geqslant1}\sigma_j\phi_j(x)\phi_j(z)$; both marginals are uniform, and $1/2\leqslant g_0\leqslant2$. Take $\tilde{\mathcal H}=\overline{\operatorname{span}}\{1,\phi_j:j\geqslant1\}\subseteq L_2(X)$ and $\tilde{\mathcal Q}=L_2(Z)$, so that $\mathcal T\phi_j=\sigma_j\phi_j$ and $\mathcal T^*\mathcal T\phi_j=\sigma_j^2\phi_j$. Set $h_0=\sum_{j\geqslant1}j^{-2}\phi_j$, $r_0=\mathcal T h_0$, $Y=r_0(Z)$, and $\hat r=r_0$. Take $\mathcal H=\{c h_0:1/2\leqslant c\leqslant3/2\}$, $\mathcal G=\{g_0,1\}$, $B=3$, $c_0=1/2$, and $C_G=2$. Choose $\delta_{n,r}=0$ and deterministic, $\beta$-independent critical-radius upper bounds satisfying the logarithmic floors in Assumption~\ref{asp:cr_deepiv}; these exist because the density class is finite and the primal and density-induced classes are bounded scalar intervals. Fix $n\geqslant1$ and $\xi\in(0,1)$. These objects satisfy Assumptions~\ref{asp:re_cd} and~\ref{asp:cr_deepiv}, but $\sum_{j\geqslant1}j^{-4}\sigma_j^{-2\beta}=\infty$ for every $\beta>0$, so the cited source condition fails; moreover, $1-\exp(n\delta_{n,\mathcal G}^2)\leqslant0<1-\xi/2$, so the reported confidence formula supplies no asserted positive probability guarantee even in this model.
- **Correction.** The statement stands as printed; its proof is corrected.
- **Proof.** Allocate failure probability $\xi/2$ to the conditional-density estimator and $\xi/2$ to the pilot regression. As elsewhere, evaluate the corresponding deterministic accuracy bounds at these confidence levels and relabel them $\delta_{n,\mathcal G}$ and $\delta_{n,r}$. We establish the density bound directly using the Localized Concentration lemma of \citet{li2024regularized}. Write $P=\mathbb E_{X,Z}$ and $P_n=\mathbb E_{n,\mathrm{pre}}$, and define \[ f_g(x,z)=\sqrt{\frac{g(x\mid z)+g_0(x\mid z)}{2g_0(x\mid z)}},\qquad \mathcal U=\{f_g-1:g\in\mathcal G\}. \] Realizability gives $f_{g_0}=1$, so $0\in\mathcal U$. The density bounds give \[ 2^{-1/2}\leqslant f_g\leqslant M,\qquad … (the corrected paper gives it in full)
- **Conditions.** The corrected statement states the conditions it uses itself; it no longer refers to 1 condition(s) of the paper it used as printed.

### Lemma C.2 — statement unchanged; proof corrected

- **As printed.** Under the assumptions of \cref{thm:iter_trae_rate}, there is a nondecreasing deterministic sequence with $b_0=0$ and $b_j\geqslant2$, $j=1,\ldots,t$, where each $b_j$ depends only on $j$ and the prescribed boundedness and mean-square-continuity constants, independently of $\beta$ and $\|w_0\|$. This sequence may be calibrated so that, on $\mathcal E_{n,t}^{\mathrm{IT}}$, simultaneously for $\lambda\in\Lambda_n\cap[\lambda_{\mathrm{IT}}^\circ,2]$, \begin{align} \|\hat h_{j,\lambda}-h_{j,\lambda}^*\|^2 &\leqslant C\frac{b_j\delta_n^2}{\lambda^2}, \qquad j=1,\ldots,t, \label{eq:iter_trae_strong_propagation}\\ L(\hat h_{t,\lambda}) &\leqslant C\left[ \delta_n^2+\min\left\{\lambda, \frac{b_t\delta_n^2}{\lambda}\right\} +\|w_0\|^2\lambda^{q_t} \right]. \label{eq:iter_trae_weak_propagation} \end{align}
- **What is wrong.** ``\dots applied at this fixed iteration--grid pair, gives \[ L(\hat h_{j,\lambda})-L(h_{j,\lambda}^*) \leqslant \lambda I_{n,j} +C\left\{ \delta_ne_j\|f_j^*\|_\infty +\delta_nu_j+\delta_n^2 \right\}.\mbox{''} \] This step uses the excess-loss comparison of \citet[Lemma~11]{bennett2023source}, which the paper assumes only for the source's iterative-theorem conditions, including $\mathbb E[h_0(X)-h(X)\mid Z=\cdot]\in\mathcal F$ for every $h\in\mathcal H$, but applies it to a projected operator for which only $\mathcal T(h_0-h)\in\mathcal F$ is assumed. The step quoted in (a) uses the excess-loss comparison of \citet[Lemma~11]{bennett2023source}, which the paper assumes only for the source's standing Realizability and Closedness assumption, which requires convexity of $\mathcal H$, but applies it to the general hypothesis class $\mathcal H$, which need not be convex. ``For the weak bound, the ordinary excess-loss comparison \citep[Lemma~10]{bennett2023source} retains the population bias.'' This step uses the excess-loss comparison of \citet[Lemma~10]{bennett2023source}, which the paper assumes only for the source's one-step-theorem conditions, including $\mathbb E[h_0(X)-h(X)\mid Z=\cdot]\in\mathcal F$ for every $h\in\mathcal H$, but applies it to a projected operator for which only $\mathcal T(h_0-h)\in\mathcal F$ is assumed. The step quoted in (c) uses the excess-loss comparison of \citet[Lemma~10]{bennett2023source}, which the paper assumes only for the source's standing Realizability and Closedness assumption, which requires convexity of $\mathcal H$, but applies it to the general hypothesis class $\mathcal H$, which need not be convex. ``The localized squared-norm comparison for $\operatorname{star}(\mathcal H-\mathcal H)$ then yields \[ I_{n,j}-I_j \leqslant C\delta_n(e_j+e_{j-1})+C\delta_n^2+2Ce_je_{j-1}.\mbox{''} \] The localized concentration application for squares centered at $h_{j-1,\lambda}^*$ or $h_{k,\lambda}^*$ requires measurable localized loss suprema, which do not follow from measurability for unshifted squares centered at $h_0$ or for the specified zero-comparator adversarial process.
- **Counterexample.** For the citation-hypothesis obstruction, let $V$ and $\varepsilon$ be independent Rademacher variables, and set $X=Z=V$, $Y=1/2+\varepsilon/4$, and $W=(Y,X,Z)$. Take $\widetilde{\mathcal H}=\operatorname{span}\{1,x\}$, $\widetilde{\mathcal Q}=\operatorname{span}\{1\}$, and $m(W;q)=Yq(Z)$. Let $\mathcal H=\{x\mapsto a+bx:0\leqslant a\leqslant1/2,\ b\in\{-1/4,0,1/4\}\}$ and $\mathcal F=\{z\mapsto d:|d|\leqslant1\}$. Take $\beta=1$ and $w_0=h_0=1/2$; then $\mathcal T(a+bx)=a$ and $h_{j,\lambda}^*=\{1-(\lambda/(1+\lambda))^j\}/2\in\mathcal H$ for every $j\geqslant1$ and $\lambda>0$. Use independent copies of $W$, fix $t\geqslant1$ and $\xi\in(0,1)$, choose sufficiently large deterministic critical-radius bounds and calibrated scales of order $\sqrt{\{\log\log(en)+\log(t(K_n+1)/\xi)+1\}/n}$, and select measurable empirical minimizers over the compact class $\mathcal H$. For a separate instance of the squared-loss measurability obstruction, let $A\subset(0,1/2)$ be non-Lebesgue-measurable and let $W=X=Z$ have the completed uniform law on $[0,1]$. Take $\widetilde{\mathcal H}=L_2(X)$, $\widetilde{\mathcal Q}=\operatorname{span}\{1\}$, $\mathcal T h=\mathbb E[h(X)]$, $r_0=h_0=w_0=1$, $\beta=1$, $m(W;f)=f$, and $\mathcal F$ equal to the constant functions with values in $[0,1]$. Using the supplied representatives, take $\mathcal H=\{x\mapsto c:0\leqslant c\leqslant1\}\cup\{1-\mathbf1_{\{a\}}:a\in[0,1]\}\cup\{1-\tfrac12\mathbf1_{\{a\}}:a\in A\}$, set $t=2$ and $\rho=1/2$, and choose the constant empirical minimizers $\hat h_{j,\lambda}=h_{j,\lambda}^*=1-\{\lambda/(1+\lambda)\}^j$. Use $B=1$ and deterministic critical-radius bounds of order $\sqrt{\{\log\log(en)+\log(t(K_n+1)/\xi)+1\}/n}$; the localized linear Rademacher suprema are measurable with bounds $O(r/\sqrt n+1/n)$, and the optional singleton increments are dominated by the singleton increments indexed by $[0,1]$ for both the stipulated unshifted squared-loss and zero-comparator adversarial suprema. In the first instance, for $h(x)=x/4$ the unprojected residual $1/2-z/4$ is outside $\mathcal F$, and $0,x/4\in\mathcal H$ while their midpoint $x/8\notin\mathcal H$, so both excess-loss citations lack their required hypotheses; in the separate uniform-law instance, for $\lambda=1$ and sufficiently large $n$, the localized centered squared-loss supremum at $h_{1,1}^*=1/2$ with radius $1/2$ equals $(4n)^{-1}\mathbf1_{\{\exists i:X_i\in A\}}$ off the measurable null set of sample ties and is not measurable under the fitting-sample law.
- **Correction.** The statement stands as printed; its proof is corrected.
- **Proof.** We adapt the iterative argument of \citet[Theorem~6]{bennett2023source} to the finite grid and give the recursive comparison explicitly. Fix $j$ and $\lambda$, and set \[ e_j=\|\hat h_{j,\lambda}-h_{j,\lambda}^*\|, \qquad u_j=\|\mathcal T(\hat h_{j,\lambda}-h_{j,\lambda}^*)\|, \qquad f_j^*=\mathcal T(h_0-h_{j,\lambda}^*). \] Also define \[ M_{\leqslant j} =\max_{1\leqslant\ell\leqslant j} \left\{\lambda,\|f_\ell^*\|_\infty^2\right\}. \] By \cref{asp:iter_realizability_trae,asp:closeness}, $f_\ell^*\in\mathcal F$. The boundedness part of \cref{asp:critical_radius} therefore gives $M_{\leqslant j}\leqslant\max\{2,B^2\}$ for every grid point under consideration. To track the random penalty … (the corrected paper gives it in full)

### Conditions and definitions restated

Conditions or definitions the paper states too loosely for the results that use them; the corrected results now state what they use.

- Assumption~\ref{asp:critical_radius} supplies bounded classes for the localized concentration arguments used in one-step and iterative TRAE. ``For a fixed $B<\infty$, suppose that $|m(W;f)|$, $|h(X)|$, and $|f(Z)|$ are a.s. bounded by $B$, uniformly over $ h\in \mathcal{H} ,f\in \mathcal{F}$.'' Uniform boundedness does not ensure measurability of the localized empirical and Rademacher loss suprema, and measurability for the $h_0$-centered squares and zero-comparator adversarial loss does not ensure it for squares centered at $h_{j-1,\lambda}^*$ or $h_{j,\lambda}^*$. Corrected text: For a fixed $B<\infty$, suppose that $|m(W;f)|$, $|h(X)|$, and $|f(Z)|$ are a.s. bounded by $B$, uniformly over $ h\in \mathcal{H} ,f\in \mathcal{F}$. Suppose in addition that, for every integer $n\geqslant1$ and every $r>0$, the localized squared-loss suprema \[ \sup_{\substack{h\in\mathcal H\\ \|h-h_0\|\leqslant r}} \left|(\mathbb E_n-\mathbb E)[h(X)^2-h_0(X)^2]\right| \quad\text{and}\quad \sup_{\substack{h\in\mathcal H\\ \|h-h_0\|\leqslant r}} \left|\frac1n\sum_{i=1}^n\epsilon_i\{h(X_i)^2-h_0(X_i)^2\}\right| \] are measurable random variables under the fitting-sample law and its product with the independent Rademacher law, respectively. Regard the functions in $\mathcal H\cdotp\mathcal F$ and $\mathcal F$ as functions of $W$ through $(X,Z)$ and $Z$, respectively, and put \[ \mathcal U=\operatorname{star}(m\circ\mathcal F) \times\operatorname{star}(\mathcal H\cdotp\mathcal F) \times\operatorname{star}(\mathcal F), \qquad \|u\|_{L_2(W;\mathbb R^3)}^2 =\mathbb E\!\left[\sum_{j=1}^3u_j(W)^2\right]. \] Define the loss with zero comparator by \[ \operatorname{clip}_B(a)=\max\{-B,\min\{a,B\}\}, \qquad \ell_B(u)=2u_1-2u_2-\operatorname{clip}_B(u_3)^2, \qquad \ell_B(0)=0. \] Suppose also that, for every integer $n\geqslant1$ and every $r>0$, the localized loss suprema \[ \sup_{\substack{u\in\mathcal U\\ \|u\|_{L_2(W;\mathbb R^3)}\leqslant r}} \left|(\mathbb E_n-\mathbb E)[\ell_B(u(W))-\ell_B(0)]\right| \quad\text{and}\quad \sup_{\substack{u\in\mathcal U\\ \|u\|_{L_2(W;\mathbb R^3)}\leqslant r}} \left|\frac1n\sum_{i=1}^n\epsilon_i\{\ell_B(u(W_i))-\ell_B(0)\}\right| \] are measurable random variables under the fitting-sample law and its product with the independent Rademacher law, respectively. Whenever the primal and dual roles are exchanged, suppose that these same two localized loss suprema for the exchanged problem are measurable under its fitting-sample law and its product with the independent Rademacher law, respectively. For iterative TRAE, suppose in addition that the following fixed-population-center squared-loss processes are measurable. For every integer $n\geqslant1$, prescribed integer $t\geqslant1$, grid point $\lambda\in\Lambda_n\cap[\lambda_{\mathrm{IT}}^\circ,2]$ for which $\lambda_{\mathrm{IT}}^\circ$ is defined, radius $r>0$, and $j=1,\ldots,t$, put \[ a_{j,\lambda}(h;X) =\{h(X)-h_{j-1,\lambda}^*(X)\}^2 -\{h_{j,\lambda}^*(X)-h_{j-1,\lambda}^*(X)\}^2, \qquad d_{j,\lambda}(h;X)=\{h(X)-h_{j,\lambda}^*(X)\}^2, \] where the population iterates are defined by \eqref{eq:iter_population}. For each $v\in\{a_{j,\lambda},d_{j,\lambda}\}$, suppose that both \[ \sup_{\substack{h\in\mathcal H\\\|h-h_{j,\lambda}^*\|\leqslant r}} \left|(\mathbb E_n-\mathbb E)v(h;X)\right| \quad\text{and}\quad \sup_{\substack{h\in\mathcal H\\\|h-h_{j,\lambda}^*\|\leqslant r}} \left|\frac1n\sum_{i=1}^n\epsilon_i v(h;X_i)\right| \] are measurable under the fitting-sample law and its product with the independent Rademacher law, respectively, using the same function representatives as the empirical penalties. The zero-iterate comparison uses $\hat h_{0,\lambda}=h_{0,\lambda}^*=0$.
- The localized squared-norm inequality holds uniformly over $h\in\mathcal H$ under the stated TRAE assumptions. ``\dots uniformly over $h\in\mathcal H$, \[ \left|\{\|h\|_{2,n}^2-\|h_0\|_{2,n}^2\} -\{\|h\|^2-\|h_0\|^2\}\right| \leqslant C\{\delta_n\|h-h_0\|+\delta_n^2\}.\mbox{''} \] The concentration argument requires measurable localized squared-loss suprema, which do not follow from measurability of the empirical loss and its selected minimizer. Add measurability of the localized squared-loss empirical and Rademacher suprema to Assumption~\ref{asp:critical_radius}, and explicitly use this condition in the cited step of Lemma~\ref{lemma:strong_convexity}; this supplies the missing regularity while protecting Theorem~\ref{thm:tikhonv_rate}, Theorem~\ref{thm:iter_trae_rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:normality}, and Corollary~\ref{cor:trae_discrepancy_bound} at their stated rates.

## Clarifications (not errors)

Nothing in this section is an error of the paper. Each item is something the paper determines but does not say, made explicit so that the statements can be read, and formalized, in one way.

**How a statement is read.**

- **Theorem 3.1.** On $\mathcal E_n$, the algorithm terminates through its acceptance branch at an index $k\leqslant k^\circ$, where $\lambda^\circ=2\rho^{k^\circ}$ and $k^\circ\leqslant K_n$; acceptance at $k=K_n$ is allowed, and both error bounds hold on this event.
- **Proposition 3.5.** For $\mathcal H$ and, for each fixed $g\in\mathcal G$, $\mathcal T_g\mathcal H$, the empirical-process suprema used in the critical-radius analysis are measurable under their respective input distributions.
- **Proposition 3.5.** For every $\lambda\in\Lambda_n$, the empirical RDIV minimum is attained and $\hat h_\lambda$ is a measurable selected minimizer of $L_n(h)+\lambda\mathbb E_n[h(X)^2]$ over $h\in\mathcal H$.
- **Proposition 3.5.** For each fixed $n\geqslant N_{\mathrm R}(B)$, all probabilistic conclusions, including initial acceptance when $h_0=0$, hold on the same event $\mathcal E_n^{\mathrm R}$ of probability at least $1-\xi$.
- **Proposition 3.5.** The assumptions imported from Theorem~\ref{thm:deepiv_rate} are the general hypotheses of Proposition~\ref{prop:exist_lambda_deepiv}, without imposing $h_0=0$.
- **Proposition 3.5.** For every preliminary sample size $n\geqslant1$, the empirical log likelihood $g\mapsto\mathbb E_{n,\mathrm{pre}}[\log g(X\mid Z)]$ attains its maximum over $\mathcal G$ almost surely, and $\hat g\in\mathcal G$ is a measurable maximizer selected using only $I_{\mathrm{pre}}$.
- **Theorem 3.6.** For each fixed $n\geqslant N_{\mathrm R}(B)$, the displayed error bounds and, when $h_0=0$, both sharper $O(\delta_n^2)$ bounds hold jointly with probability at least $1-\xi$ over the preliminary, fitting, and calibration samples.
- **Theorem 3.6.** The assumptions of \cref{thm:deepiv_rate} mean its standing hypotheses inherited from \cref{prop:exist_lambda_deepiv}; the bounds here hold for arbitrary $h_0$, without imposing $h_0=0$.
- **Proposition 3.8.** The displayed discrepancy inequality holds on $\mathcal E_{n,t}^{\mathrm{IR}}$, with $\mathbb P(\mathcal E_{n,t}^{\mathrm{IR}})\geqslant1-\xi$, simultaneously for every $\lambda\in\Lambda_n\cap[\lambda_{\mathrm{IR}}^\circ,2]$.
- **Proposition 3.8.** For each fixed $n\geqslant N_{\mathrm R}(B)$ and prescribed $t$, there is an event of probability at least $1-\xi$ on which the rule accepts a candidate and satisfies the stated output alternatives, including acceptance of $\lambda_0=2$ when $h_0=0$.
- **Proposition 3.13.** The empirical loss $L_n$ is measurable, and the asserted attainment and measurable selection concern the minimum of $h\mapsto L_n(h)+\lambda\mathbb E_n[h(X)^2]$ over $h\in\mathcal H$.
- **Proposition 3.13.** For a fixed constant $C_{\mathrm T}>0$, both displayed inequalities hold on $\mathcal E_n$, simultaneously for all $\lambda\in\Lambda_n$, under the stated calibration convention.
- **Proposition 3.13.** For each fixed $n\geqslant N_{\mathrm T}(B)$, acceptance within the stated number of optimization problems and the stated alternative for the returned parameter hold jointly on $\mathcal E_n$, with probability at least $1-\xi$ over the fitting sample.
- **Proposition 3.13.** There exists a fixed $C<\infty$, independent of $f$, $n$, and $\lambda$, such that $\mathbb E[m(W;f)^2]\leqslant C\|f\|^2$ for every $f\in\mathcal F$.
- **Proposition 3.13.** The lower bound denoted by $\Omega$ means that there exists a constant $c>0$, independent of $n$ and $\xi$, such that for every integer $n\geqslant1$ and every $\xi\in(0,1)$, $\tilde\delta_n(\xi)\geqslant c\sqrt{\{\log\log(en)+\log(1/\xi)\}/n}$.
- **Theorem 3.14.** With $\beta$ and $\xi$ fixed as $n\to\infty$, for each $n\geqslant N_{\mathrm T}(B)$ the displayed strong- and weak-error bounds hold jointly on a fitting-sample event of probability at least $1-\xi$.
- **Proposition 3.17.** For each fixed $n\geqslant N_{\mathrm{IT}}(B)$ and prescribed $t$, acceptance within the stated number of minimax problems and the stated alternative for the returned parameter hold jointly on $\mathcal E_{n,t}^{\mathrm{IT}}$, with probability at least $1-\xi$ over the fitting sample.
- **Theorem 3.18.** Under the assumptions of Theorem~\ref{thm:iter_trae_rate}, the displayed inequality holds simultaneously for all $\lambda\in\Lambda_n\cap[\lambda_{\mathrm{IT}}^\circ,2]$ on $\mathcal E_{n,t}^{\mathrm{IT}}$, where $\mathbb P(\mathcal E_{n,t}^{\mathrm{IT}})\geqslant1-\xi$.
- **Theorem 3.18.** The constant $C_t$ may depend on the prescribed $t$ and fixed population quantities, including $\beta$ and $\|w_0\|$, but is independent of $n$, $\lambda$, and the realized fitting observations.
- **Theorem 3.18.** The constants hidden by $O_t(\cdot)$ may depend on the prescribed $t$, fixed population quantities including $\beta$ and $\|w_0\|$, and the fixed grid ratio $\rho$, but are independent of $n$ and the candidate $\lambda$.

**Side conditions the proofs use without stating them.**

- **Theorem 3.18.** With $\mathcal U$ and $\ell_B$ as in Assumption~\ref{asp:critical_radius}, for every fixed iteration--grid pair $(j,\lambda)$ under consideration and every $r>0$, both $\sup_{u\in\mathcal U,\,\|u-u_{j,\lambda}^*\|_{L_2(W;\mathbb R^3)}\leqslant r}|(\mathbb E_n-\mathbb E)\{\ell_B(u(W))-\ell_B(u_{j,\lambda}^*(W))\}|$ and $\sup_{u\in\mathcal U,\,\|u-u_{j,\lambda}^*\|_{L_2(W;\mathbb R^3)}\leqslant r}|n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^n\epsilon_i\{\ell_B(u(W_i))-\ell_B(u_{j,\lambda}^*(W_i))\}|$ are measurable under the fitting-sample law and its product with the independent Rademacher law, respectively, where $f_j^*=\mathcal T(h_0-h_{j,\lambda}^*)$ and $u_{j,\lambda}^*(W)=(m(W;f_j^*),h_{j,\lambda}^*(X)f_j^*(Z),f_j^*(Z))$.

**Notation.**

- $r_0$: $r_0\in\tilde{\mathcal Q}$ is the Riesz representer of the continuous linear functional $q\mapsto\mathbb E[m(W;q)]$, satisfying $\mathbb E[m(W;q)]=\mathbb E[r_0(Z)q(Z)]$ for every $q\in\tilde{\mathcal Q}$. In Proposition~\ref{prop:exist_lambda_deepiv}, Theorem~\ref{thm:deepiv_rate}, Proposition~\ref{prop:exist_lambda_iter_rdiv}, Theorem~\ref{thm:iter_rdiv_rate}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:t_err_dp}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:err_l_dp}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:err_r_dp}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:str_dp}, Corollary~\ref{cor:rdiv_discrepancy_bound}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:rdiv_accepted_bounds}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:iter_rdiv_stability}, Corollary~\ref{cor:iter_rdiv_discrepancy_bound}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:iter_rdiv_accepted_bounds}, \cite[Lemma 14]{foster2023orthogonal}, used in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lemma:err_l_dp}: $\tilde{\mathcal Q}=L_2(Z)$ and $r_0(z)=\mathbb E[Y\mid Z=z]$.
- $q$: The letter $q$ is locally reused for function-valued arguments in $\tilde{\mathcal Q}$ and for a deterministic scalar bias exponent in $(1,\infty)$; these uses denote distinct objects. In Theorem~\ref{thm:common_dp}: In $B_n(\lambda)=C_b\lambda^q$, $q>1$ is a fixed scalar exponent. In Theorem~\ref{thm:deepiv_rate}, Theorem~\ref{thm:tikhonv_rate}: In the application of the generic discrepancy guarantee, the scalar exponent is $q=p=\min\{\beta+1,2\}$. In Theorem~\ref{thm:iter_rdiv_rate}, Theorem~\ref{thm:iter_trae_rate}: In the proof's scalar abbreviation and application of the generic discrepancy guarantee, $q=q_t=\min\{\beta+1,2t\}$. In Corollary~\ref{cor:normality}: In the dual minimization, $q\in\mathcal Q$ is the candidate function of $Z$; in $\hat\theta(h,q)$ it is the dual nuisance argument.
- $Y$: $Y$ is the scalar outcome, with $r_0(z)=\mathbb E[Y\mid Z=z]$ in the IV setting. In Proposition~\ref{prop:exist_lambda_deepiv}, Theorem~\ref{thm:deepiv_rate}, Proposition~\ref{prop:exist_lambda_iter_rdiv}, Theorem~\ref{thm:iter_rdiv_rate}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:t_err_dp}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:err_l_dp}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:err_r_dp}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:str_dp}, Corollary~\ref{cor:rdiv_discrepancy_bound}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:rdiv_accepted_bounds}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:iter_rdiv_stability}, Corollary~\ref{cor:iter_rdiv_discrepancy_bound}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:iter_rdiv_accepted_bounds}, \cite[Lemma 14]{foster2023orthogonal}, used in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lemma:err_l_dp}: $|Y|\leqslant B$ almost surely for a fixed $B<\infty$.
- $\mathcal H$: $\mathcal H$ denotes the primal hypothesis class of functions of $X$. In Proposition~\ref{prop:rkhs_qp}: $\mathcal H$ is the full RKHS generated by $k_{\mathcal H}$, before restriction to the retained numerical spectral span; its RKHS norm specifies the minimum-norm representative among functions with the same fitting-sample evaluations.
- $\mathcal F$: $\mathcal F$ is a function class on the input domain used in the surrounding complexity or loss definition. In Corollary~\ref{cor:normality}: For the primal application, $\mathcal F\subset\tilde{\mathcal Q}$ is the adversarial test class; for the dual application the corresponding test class is $\mathcal S\subseteq\tilde{\mathcal H}$, with the primal and dual spaces exchanged. In Proposition~\ref{prop:rkhs_qp}: $\mathcal F$ is the full RKHS generated by $k_{\mathcal F}$, and its empirical supremum includes the penalty $-\eta\|f\|_{\mathcal F}^2$ with $\eta>0$. In Proposition~\ref{prop:exist_lambda}, Theorem~\ref{thm:tikhonv_rate}, Proposition~\ref{prop:exist_lambda_iter_trae}, Theorem~\ref{thm:iter_trae_rate}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:concentrate_l}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:strong_convexity}, Corollary~\ref{cor:trae_discrepancy_bound}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:trae_accepted_bounds}, Lemma~\ref{eq:iter_trae_strong_propagation}, Corollary~\ref{cor:iter_trae_discrepancy_bound}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:iter_trae_accepted_stability}, \cite[Lemma 9]{bennett2023source}, used in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lemma:concentrate_l}, \cite[Lemma 14]{foster2023orthogonal}, used in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lemma:strong_convexity}: $\mathcal F\subset\tilde{\mathcal Q}$ is the test class in $L_n(h)=\sup_{f\in\mathcal F}\mathbb E_n[2m(W;f)-2h(X)f(Z)-f(Z)^2]$ and its population counterpart; it contains $\mathcal T(h_0-h)$ for every $h\in\mathcal H$ under \cref{asp:closeness}. In \cite[Lemma 9 (lem:concentration)]{bennett2023source}, used in the proof of Lemma~\ref{eq:iter_trae_strong_propagation}: $\mathcal F\subset\tilde{\mathcal Q}$ is the test class in $L_n(h)=\sup_{f\in\mathcal F}\mathbb E_n[2m(W;f)-2h(X)f(Z)-f(Z)^2]$ and its population counterpart; it contains $\mathcal T(h_0-h)$ for every $h\in\mathcal H$ under \cref{asp:closeness}.
- $\mathcal S$: $\mathcal S$ is a fixed population function class whose local definition specifies its role and whether its functions have argument $X$ or $(X,Z)$. In Corollary~\ref{cor:normality}: $\mathcal S$ is the dual test class appearing in $L_n^{\mathrm{dual}}(q)=\sup_{s\in\mathcal S}\mathbb E_n[2\tilde m(W;s)-2q(Z)s(X)-s(X)^2]$. In Lemma~\ref{lemma:strong_convexity}, \cite[Lemma 14]{foster2023orthogonal}, used in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lemma:strong_convexity}: $\mathcal S=\operatorname{star}(\mathcal H-\mathcal H)$ is the star-shaped difference class used to verify the anchored critical-radius condition. In Lemma~\ref{lemma:t_err_dp}: In the likelihood concentration argument, $\mathcal S=\operatorname{star}(\mathcal G_0-\mathcal G_0)$ is the fixed star-shaped difference class of likelihood-transform functions on $(X,Z)$, with norm $\|\cdot\|_{L_2(X,Z)}$. In \cite[Localized Concentration (source label lem:local-concen)]{li2024regularized}, used in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lemma:t_err_dp}: In the likelihood concentration argument, $\mathcal S=\operatorname{star}(\mathcal G_0-\mathcal G_0)$ is the fixed star-shaped difference class of likelihood-transform functions on $(X,Z)$, with norm $\|\cdot\|_{L_2(X,Z)}$.
- $a_0$: The notation $a_0$ is local to either the dual inverse problem or the scalar recursion for $(a_j)_{j\geqslant0}$. In Corollary~\ref{cor:normality}: $a_0\in\tilde{\mathcal H}$ is the Riesz representer satisfying $\mathbb E[\tilde m(W;h)]=\mathbb E[a_0(X)h(X)]$ for every $h\in\tilde{\mathcal H}$, and is the right-hand side of $\mathcal T^*q_0=a_0$. In Lemma~\ref{lemma:iter_rdiv_stability}: In the proof's recursion $a_j=C(1+a_{j-1})$, $a_0=0$ is a scalar initial value, distinct from the Riesz representer in the dual inverse problem.
- $\hat\theta$: $\hat\theta$ denotes an empirical estimate of $\theta_0$, with its nuisance arguments displayed when the doubly robust score functional is intended. In Corollary~\ref{cor:normality}: $\hat\theta(h,q)=\mathbb E_{n,\mathrm{eval}}[\tilde m(W;h)+m(W;q)-h(X)q(Z)]$, and the estimator in the conclusion is its evaluation at $(h,q)=(\hat h,\hat q)$; here $q\in\mathcal Q$ is a nuisance-function argument.
- $\hat h$: $\hat h$ denotes an estimated primal solution, with $\hat h_\lambda$ denoting a candidate at penalty $\lambda$, $\hat h_{j,\lambda}$ denoting an empirical iterate, and $\hat h_{\lambda_{\mathrm{dp}}}$ or $\hat h_{t,\lambda_{\mathrm{dp}}}$ denoting the corresponding discrepancy-selected estimate. In Proposition~\ref{prop:exist_lambda_iter_rdiv}, Theorem~\ref{thm:iter_rdiv_rate}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:iter_rdiv_stability}, Corollary~\ref{cor:iter_rdiv_discrepancy_bound}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:iter_rdiv_accepted_bounds}: $\hat h_{0,\lambda}=0$ and $\hat h_{j,\lambda}=\arg\min_{h\in\mathcal H}\{L_n(h)+\lambda\|h-\hat h_{j-1,\lambda}\|_{2,n}^2\}$ for $j=1,\ldots,t$, with the RDIV squared loss; the candidate used for discrepancy selection is $\hat h_{t,\lambda}$. In Corollary~\ref{cor:normality}: $\hat h=\hat h_{\lambda_{\mathrm{dp}}}$ is the one-step adaptive adversarial Tikhonov estimate, with the primal tuning rule run at confidence level $\xi_n/2$ and with the common deterministic sequence $\delta_n$ specified for both nuisance problems. Its evaluations use jointly measurable versions relative to the full nuisance-training and tuning sigma-field $\mathcal G_n$ and the observation, representing the fitted function for almost every training realization and agreeing almost surely with its evaluations at every observation in the finite evaluation sample. In Proposition~\ref{prop:exist_lambda_deepiv}: $\hat h_\lambda=\arg\min_{h\in\mathcal H}\{L_n(h)+\lambda\mathbb E_n[h(X)^2]\}$ with $L_n(h)=\mathbb E_{n,\mathrm{fit}}[(Y-(\hat{\mathcal T}h)(Z))^2]$. In Proposition~\ref{prop:exist_lambda}: $\hat h_\lambda=\arg\min_{h\in\mathcal H}\{L_n(h)+\lambda\mathbb E_n[h(X)^2]\}$, where $L_n(h)=\sup_{f\in\mathcal F}\mathbb E_n[2m(W;f)-2h(X)f(Z)-f(Z)^2]$. In Proposition~\ref{prop:exist_lambda_iter_trae}, Theorem~\ref{thm:iter_trae_rate}, Lemma~\ref{eq:iter_trae_strong_propagation}, Corollary~\ref{cor:iter_trae_discrepancy_bound}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:iter_trae_accepted_stability}: $\hat h_{0,\lambda}=0$ and $\hat h_{j,\lambda}=\arg\min_{h\in\mathcal H}\{L_n(h)+\lambda\|h-\hat h_{j-1,\lambda}\|_{2,n}^2\}$ for $j=1,\ldots,t$, with the adversarial loss; the candidate used for discrepancy selection is $\hat h_{t,\lambda}$.
- $\hat q$: $\hat q$ denotes the dual empirical adversarial Tikhonov fit at the discrepancy-selected dual penalty. In Corollary~\ref{cor:normality}: The dual discrepancy rule is run at confidence level $\xi_n/2$, using the common deterministic sequence $\delta_n$ that satisfies \eqref{eq:delta_trae} for both nuisance problems. Take jointly measurable fitted evaluations of $\hat q$ representing the fitted function for almost every nuisance-training and tuning realization and agreeing almost surely with $\hat q(Z(W_i))$ simultaneously for $i=1,\ldots,n$ in the evaluation sample.
- $\beta$: $\beta>0$ is a source exponent satisfying $h_0=(\mathcal T^*\mathcal T)^{\beta/2}w_0$ for some $w_0\in\widetilde{\mathcal H}$. In Corollary~\ref{cor:normality}: The two applications use potentially different source exponents: $h_0=(\mathcal T^*\mathcal T)^{\beta_h/2}w_h$ and $q_0=(\mathcal T\mathcal T^*)^{\beta_q/2}w_q$, with source elements in their respective Hilbert spaces; $\beta_m=\max\{\beta_h,\beta_q\}$.
- $w_0$: $w_0\in\tilde{\mathcal H}$ is a chosen source witness satisfying $h_0=(\mathcal T^*\mathcal T)^{\beta/2}w_0$, with no uniqueness or unit-norm requirement. In Proposition~\ref{prop:exist_lambda_deepiv}, Proposition~\ref{prop:exist_lambda_iter_rdiv}: When $h_0=0$, take $w_0=0$ in the initial-acceptance argument.
- $\lambda$: $\lambda$ is the scalar regularization parameter. In the classical Hilbert-space construction \eqref{eq:tikh_sol} and Definition~\ref{def:dp}, finite penalties satisfy $0<\lambda<\infty$, with $\lambda=\infty$ denoting the zero-estimator convention. The value $\lambda=0$ is admitted only for the separate finite-dimensional computational convention of Section~\ref{sec:rkhs_computation}, which selects a minimum-Euclidean-norm solution on the retained spectral subspace; it does not define an unregularized solution of \eqref{eq:tikh_sol} for arbitrary $r_\delta$. In Theorem~\ref{thm:common_dp}, Proposition~\ref{prop:exist_lambda_deepiv}, Theorem~\ref{thm:deepiv_rate}, Proposition~\ref{prop:exist_lambda_iter_rdiv}, Theorem~\ref{thm:iter_rdiv_rate}, Proposition~\ref{prop:exist_lambda}, Theorem~\ref{thm:tikhonv_rate}, Proposition~\ref{prop:exist_lambda_iter_trae}, Theorem~\ref{thm:iter_trae_rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:normality}: The tuning rule searches the deterministic finite grid $\Lambda_n=\{2\rho^k:k=0,\ldots,K_n\}$, where $1/2\leqslant\rho<1$ and $K_n=\lceil\log(2n^2)/\log(1/\rho)\rceil$; its selected value is denoted $\lambda_{\mathrm{dp}}$. In Proposition~\ref{prop:rkhs_qp}: $\lambda>0$ is a candidate penalty for the unrestricted quadratic program, before the numerical spectral restriction. In Lemma~\ref{eq:iter_trae_strong_propagation}: In each concentration application in the proof, $\lambda\in\Lambda_n\cap[\lambda_{\mathrm{IT}}^\circ,2]$ is a fixed candidate penalty.
- $r_\delta$: $r_\delta\in\tilde{\mathcal Q}$ is a perturbed right-hand side satisfying $\|r_\delta-r_0\|\leqslant\delta$.
- $\delta$: $\delta\geqslant0$ denotes the prescribed bound in $\|r_\delta-r_0\|\leqslant\delta$ in the classical inverse problem, and the radius of the population-norm restriction $\|f\|\leqslant\delta$ when used as the argument of $\mathfrak R_n(\delta;\mathcal F)$.
- $h_\lambda^\delta$: $h_\lambda^\delta$ is the minimizer over $h\in\tilde{\mathcal H}$ of $\|\mathcal T h-r_\delta\|^2+\lambda\|h\|^2$ for $0<\lambda<\infty$, with $h_\infty^\delta=0$ under the discrepancy rule.
- $\ell$: $\ell$ takes its meaning from its local binding: a prescribed residual-bracketing factor, an integer iteration index, or a real-valued loss function on $\mathbb R$. In Lemma~\ref{eq:iter_trae_strong_propagation}: In $M_{\leqslant j}=\max_{1\leqslant\ell\leqslant j}\{\lambda,\|f_\ell^*\|_\infty^2\}$ and the subsequent references to $f_\ell^*$, $\ell$ ranges over the integers $1,\ldots,j$. In \cite[Lemma 14]{foster2023orthogonal}, used in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lemma:strong_convexity}: Put $M=\max\{1,B\}$. Here $\ell:\mathbb R\to\mathbb R$ is the globally Lipschitz loss $\ell(v)=M^2\bigl(\max\{-1,\min\{v,1\}\}\bigr)^2$, with Lipschitz constant $2M^2$; boundedness gives $\ell(h(X)/M)=h(X)^2$ for $h\in\mathcal H$. In Lemma~\ref{lemma:t_err_dp}: In the likelihood concentration argument, $\ell:\mathbb R\to\mathbb R$ is the fixed loss $\ell(v)=-\log\bigl(\max\{1+v,2^{-1/2}\}\bigr)$; it is $\sqrt2$-Lipschitz, satisfies $\ell(0)=0$, and obeys $\ell(f_g-1)=-\log f_g$. In Lemma~\ref{lemma:strong_convexity}: Put $M=\max\{1,B\}$. In the normalized squared-loss argument, $\ell:\mathbb R\to\mathbb R$ is the loss $\ell(u)=M^2\bigl(\max\{-1,\min\{u,1\}\}\bigr)^2$, which is $2M^2$-Lipschitz and satisfies $\ell(h(X)/M)=h(X)^2$ for $h\in\mathcal H$. In \cite[Localized Concentration (source label lem:local-concen)]{li2024regularized}, used in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lemma:t_err_dp}: In the likelihood concentration argument, $\ell:\mathbb R\to\mathbb R$ is the fixed loss $\ell(v)=-\log\bigl(\max\{1+v,2^{-1/2}\}\bigr)$; it is $\sqrt2$-Lipschitz, satisfies $\ell(0)=0$, and obeys $\ell(f_g-1)=-\log f_g$.
- $\xi$: $\xi\in(0,1)$ is a prescribed failure-probability level, with fixed numerical rescalings absorbed into the rate bounds and the level relabelled after intersecting finitely many event families. In Corollary~\ref{cor:normality}: The confidence level used in each primal and dual tuning rule is $\xi_n/2$, where $\xi_n\downarrow0$ is deterministic; the simultaneous nuisance bounds consequently have failure probability at most $\xi_n$.
- $\rho$: In the discrepancy grid, $\rho\in[1/2,1)$ is the fixed search factor, independent of $n$; the notation $\rho$ in the experimental designs of Section~\ref{sec:experiments} instead denotes their instrument-strength parameter, equal to the correlation of $(X,Z)$ in the linear Gaussian design.
- $\hat h_\lambda$: $\hat h_\lambda$ denotes the candidate solution at penalty $\lambda$, with the discrepancy search using the family indexed by $\lambda\in\Lambda_n$ and the candidate construction specified by the estimation method. In Proposition~\ref{prop:exist_lambda}, Theorem~\ref{thm:tikhonv_rate}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:concentrate_l}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:strong_convexity}, Corollary~\ref{cor:trae_discrepancy_bound}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:trae_accepted_bounds}, \cite[Lemma 9]{bennett2023source}, used in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lemma:concentrate_l}: $\hat h_\lambda$ is a measurable selected minimizer of $L_n(h)+\lambda\mathbb E_n[h(X)^2]$ over $h\in\mathcal H$, where $L_n(h)=\sup_{f\in\mathcal F}\mathbb E_n[2m(W;f)-2h(X)f(Z)-f(Z)^2]$. In Proposition~\ref{prop:exist_lambda_deepiv}, Theorem~\ref{thm:deepiv_rate}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:err_l_dp}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:err_r_dp}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:str_dp}, Corollary~\ref{cor:rdiv_discrepancy_bound}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:rdiv_accepted_bounds}, \cite[Lemma 14]{foster2023orthogonal}, used in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lemma:err_l_dp}: $\hat h_\lambda$ is a measurable selected minimizer over $h\in\mathcal H$ of $\mathbb E_{n,\mathrm{fit}}[(Y-\hat{\mathcal T}h(Z))^2]+\lambda\mathbb E_{n,\mathrm{fit}}[h(X)^2]$. In Proposition~\ref{prop:exist_lambda_iter_rdiv}, Theorem~\ref{thm:iter_rdiv_rate}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:iter_rdiv_stability}: The candidate supplied to the search is $\hat h_{t,\lambda}$, with $\hat h_{0,\lambda}=0$ and measurable selected minimizers $\hat h_{j,\lambda}\in\arg\min_{h\in\mathcal H}\{L_n(h)+\lambda\|h-\hat h_{j-1,\lambda}\|_{2,n}^2\}$ for $j=1,\ldots,t$, using the RDIV fitting loss. In Corollary~\ref{cor:normality}: The primal candidate is the TRAE measurable selected minimizer of $L_n(h)+\lambda\mathbb E_n[h(X)^2]$ over $\mathcal H$; the corresponding dual construction exchanges the primal and dual spaces and operators. For the selected primal fit $\hat h$, take jointly measurable fitted evaluations representing the fitted function for almost every nuisance-training and tuning realization and agreeing almost surely with $\hat h(X(W_i))$ simultaneously for $i=1,\ldots,n$ in the evaluation sample. In Proposition~\ref{prop:rkhs_qp}: $\widehat h_\lambda$ minimizes $L_{N,\eta}(h)+\lambda\mathbb E_N[h(X)^2]$ over the full RKHS $\mathcal H$, taking the minimum-RKHS-norm representative among functions with the same fitting-sample evaluations; it has the form $\widehat h_\lambda(\cdot)=\sum_{j=1}^N\widehat\alpha_{\lambda,j}k_{\mathcal H}(X_j,\cdot)$ with coefficients solving \eqref{eq:rkhs_qp}. In Proposition~\ref{prop:exist_lambda_iter_trae}, Theorem~\ref{thm:iter_trae_rate}, Lemma~\ref{eq:iter_trae_strong_propagation}, Corollary~\ref{cor:iter_trae_discrepancy_bound}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:iter_trae_accepted_stability}: The candidate supplied to the search is $\hat h_{t,\lambda}$, with $\hat h_{0,\lambda}=0$ and measurable selected minimizers $\hat h_{j,\lambda}\in\arg\min_{h\in\mathcal H}\{L_n(h)+\lambda\|h-\hat h_{j-1,\lambda}\|_{2,n}^2\}$ for $j=1,\ldots,t$, using the TRAE adversarial loss.
- $D_n$: $D_n$ is the nonnegative discrepancy functional supplied to the search, evaluated at the candidate for each $\lambda\in\Lambda_n$, with its functional definition specified by the estimation method. In Proposition~\ref{prop:exist_lambda_deepiv}, Theorem~\ref{thm:deepiv_rate}, Proposition~\ref{prop:exist_lambda_iter_rdiv}, Theorem~\ref{thm:iter_rdiv_rate}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:err_l_dp}, Corollary~\ref{cor:rdiv_discrepancy_bound}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:rdiv_accepted_bounds}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:iter_rdiv_stability}, Corollary~\ref{cor:iter_rdiv_discrepancy_bound}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:iter_rdiv_accepted_bounds}: $D_n(h)=\mathbb E_{n,\mathrm{cal}}[(\hat r(Z)-\hat{\mathcal T}h(Z))^2]$, the projected calibration residual; the same functional is evaluated at $\hat h_{t,\lambda}$ for iterative candidates. In Corollary~\ref{cor:normality}: For each nuisance problem, the discrepancy is its empirical adversarial loss: $D_n=L_n$ for the primal problem, with the corresponding loss after exchanging the primal and dual spaces and operators for the dual problem. In Proposition~\ref{prop:exist_lambda}, Theorem~\ref{thm:tikhonv_rate}, Proposition~\ref{prop:exist_lambda_iter_trae}, Theorem~\ref{thm:iter_trae_rate}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:trae_accepted_bounds}, Corollary~\ref{cor:iter_trae_discrepancy_bound}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:iter_trae_accepted_stability}, \cite[Lemma 9]{bennett2023source}, used in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lemma:concentrate_l}: $D_n(h)=L_n(h)=\sup_{f\in\mathcal F}\mathbb E_n[2m(W;f)-2h(X)f(Z)-f(Z)^2]$, with the same loss evaluated at the final iterate for iterative candidates.
- $A_n$: $A_n:\Lambda_n\to[0,\infty)$ supplies the noise level in the acceptance threshold $\tfrac32A_n(\lambda)$, with prescribed statistical-error bounds in the theoretical constructions and data-dependent noise-floor estimates in the experimental construction. In Theorem~\ref{thm:common_dp}: $A_n$ is prescribed, and is required to be nonincreasing only over $\Lambda_n\cap[\lambda^\circ,2]$. In Proposition~\ref{prop:exist_lambda_deepiv}, Theorem~\ref{thm:deepiv_rate}: $A_n=A_n^{\mathrm R}$, where $A_n^{\mathrm R}(\lambda)=\delta_n^2/\lambda+\nu_n^2$ for $\lambda\in\Lambda_n$. In Proposition~\ref{prop:exist_lambda_iter_rdiv}, Theorem~\ref{thm:iter_rdiv_rate}: $A_n=A_{n,t}^{\mathrm R}$, where $A_{n,t}^{\mathrm R}(\lambda)=s_{n,t}^{\mathrm R}/\lambda+\nu_n^2$ and $s_{n,t}^{\mathrm R}=a_t\delta_n^2$. In Proposition~\ref{prop:exist_lambda}, Theorem~\ref{thm:tikhonv_rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:normality}: $A_n=A_n^{\mathrm T}$, where $A_n^{\mathrm T}(\lambda)=\delta_n^2$ for $\lambda\in\Lambda_n$, using the TRAE accuracy scale for the relevant problem. In Proposition~\ref{prop:exist_lambda_iter_trae}, Theorem~\ref{thm:iter_trae_rate}: $A_n=A_{n,t}^{\mathrm T}$, where $A_{n,t}^{\mathrm T}(\lambda)=\delta_n^2+\min\{\lambda,s_{n,t}^{\mathrm T}/\lambda\}$ and $s_{n,t}^{\mathrm T}=b_t\delta_n^2$; on the oracle segment $[\lambda_{\mathrm{IT}}^\circ,2]$ this equals $\delta_n^2+s_{n,t}^{\mathrm T}/\lambda$.
- $\mathcal S_n$: $\mathcal S_n\subseteq(0,2]$ is the prescribed stability domain for the generic comparisons, which are required at grid points in $\Lambda_n\cap\mathcal S_n$. In Theorem~\ref{thm:deepiv_rate}, Theorem~\ref{thm:tikhonv_rate}: $\mathcal S_n=(0,2]$. In Theorem~\ref{thm:iter_rdiv_rate}: $\mathcal S_n=[\lambda_{\mathrm{IR}}^\circ,2]$ in the application of the generic comparison argument. In Theorem~\ref{thm:iter_trae_rate}: $\mathcal S_n=[\lambda_{\mathrm{IT}}^\circ,2]$ in the application of the generic comparison argument.
- $\mathcal E_n$: $\mathcal E_n$ is a common event of probability at least $1-\xi$ on which the relevant comparisons hold simultaneously over their stated function and grid domains. In Proposition~\ref{prop:exist_lambda}, Theorem~\ref{thm:tikhonv_rate}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:concentrate_l}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:strong_convexity}, Corollary~\ref{cor:trae_discrepancy_bound}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:trae_accepted_bounds}: $\mathcal E_n$ is the common TRAE concentration event formed using $\eta_n=\xi/(K_n+1)$ and a union bound over $\Lambda_n$; it includes the uniform loss comparison, the localized squared-norm comparison, and $L_n(h_0)\leqslant C\delta_n^2$.
- $\gamma$: $\gamma$ is locally bound to its construction, with $\gamma\in[0,1]$ denoting the scalar multiplier in $\operatorname{star}(\mathcal F)=\{\gamma f:\gamma\in[0,1],f\in\mathcal F\}$. In Theorem~\ref{thm:common_dp}: $\gamma\in(0,1]$ is a fixed exponent in the strong-error comparison and the resulting oracle bound. In Theorem~\ref{thm:deepiv_rate}, Theorem~\ref{thm:tikhonv_rate}: When applying the oracle guarantee, the exponent is $\gamma=\min\{\beta,1\}/(1+\min\{\beta,1\})$. In Theorem~\ref{thm:iter_rdiv_rate}, Theorem~\ref{thm:iter_trae_rate}: When applying the oracle guarantee, the exponent is $\gamma=\beta/(\beta+1)$. In Lemma~\ref{lemma:iter_bias}: In the spectral bound, $\gamma>0$ is an arbitrary real exponent in $\sup_{0\leqslant u\leqslant1}u^\gamma\{\lambda/(u+\lambda)\}^{2j}\leqslant C_{\gamma,j}\lambda^{\min\{\gamma,2j\}}$; it is then specialized to $\beta$ and $\beta+1$. In Proposition~\ref{prop:rkhs_qp}: $\gamma\in\mathbb R^N$ is the critic coefficient vector in $f_\gamma(\cdot)=\sum_{j=1}^N\gamma_j k_{\mathcal F}(Z_j,\cdot)$, over which the finite-dimensional critic objective is maximized.
- $\lambda^\circ$: $\lambda^\circ\in\Lambda_n$ is any oracle comparison point satisfying $\Lambda_n\cap[\lambda^\circ,2]\subseteq\mathcal S_n$, nonincreasing $A_n$ on that grid segment, and $B_n(\lambda^\circ)\leqslant\tfrac12A_n(\lambda^\circ)$; no uniqueness or computable selection rule is required.
- $\lambda_{\mathrm{dp}}$: $\lambda_{\mathrm{dp}}=2\rho^{k_*}$, where $k_*=\min\{k\in\{0,\ldots,K_n\}:D_n(\hat h_{2\rho^k})\leqslant\tfrac32A_n(2\rho^k)\}$ if this set is nonempty, and $k_*=K_n$ otherwise, using the candidates, discrepancy, and noise level supplied to the rule. In Proposition~\ref{prop:exist_lambda_deepiv}, Theorem~\ref{thm:deepiv_rate}: The rule uses the one-step RDIV candidates $\hat h_\lambda$, the projected calibration discrepancy, and $A_n^{\mathrm R}(\lambda)=\delta_n^2/\lambda+\nu_n^2$. In Proposition~\ref{prop:exist_lambda}, Theorem~\ref{thm:tikhonv_rate}: The rule uses the one-step TRAE candidates, $D_n=L_n$, and $A_n^{\mathrm T}(\lambda)=\delta_n^2$. In Corollary~\ref{cor:normality}: The primal and dual TRAE problems are tuned separately at confidence levels $\xi_n/2$ using a common deterministic accuracy bound $\delta_n$; the dual output is $\lambda_{\mathrm{dp}}^{\text{dual}}$. In Proposition~\ref{prop:exist_lambda_iter_rdiv}: The rule uses the terminal iterative RDIV candidates $\hat h_{t,\lambda}$, the projected calibration discrepancy, and $A_{n,t}^{\mathrm R}(\lambda)=s_{n,t}^{\mathrm R}/\lambda+\nu_n^2$; its selected parameter is denoted $\lambda_{\mathrm{dp},t}^{\mathrm R}$. In Proposition~\ref{prop:exist_lambda_iter_trae}: The rule uses the terminal iterative TRAE candidates and $A_{n,t}^{\mathrm T}(\lambda)=\delta_n^2+\min\{\lambda,s_{n,t}^{\mathrm T}/\lambda\}$.
- $g_0$: $g_0(\cdot\mid z)\in\mathcal G$ is the true conditional density of $X$ given $Z=z$ with respect to $\mu$, normalized by $\int g_0(x\mid z)\,\mu(dx)=1$ for $P_Z$-almost every $z$, with $g_0(x\mid z)\geqslant c_0$ on the common conditional-density support.
- $\hat r$: $\hat r$ is the pilot regression estimate of $r_0(z)=\mathbb E[Y\mid Z=z]$ fitted from $Y$ on $Z$ using $I_{\mathrm{pre}}$.
- $I_{\mathrm{pre}}$: In conditioning expressions, $I_{\mathrm{pre}}$ denotes the observed preliminary sample, so conditioning on $I_{\mathrm{pre}}$ means conditioning on $\sigma(W_i:i\in I_{\mathrm{pre}})$.
- $I_{\mathrm{fit}}$: $I_{\mathrm{fit}}$ is the fitting block of the three-way RDIV split, with $|I_{\mathrm{fit}}|=n$, and $\mathbb E_n=\mathbb E_{n,\mathrm{fit}}$ in the RDIV analysis.
- $I_{\mathrm{cal}}$: $I_{\mathrm{cal}}$ is the calibration block of the three-way RDIV split, with $|I_{\mathrm{cal}}|=n$.
- $L_n$: $L_n$ denotes the empirical fitting loss on $\mathcal H$, with $\mathbb E_n$ taken over the fitting observations of the applicable procedure. In Proposition~\ref{prop:exist_lambda_deepiv}, Theorem~\ref{thm:deepiv_rate}, Proposition~\ref{prop:exist_lambda_iter_rdiv}, Theorem~\ref{thm:iter_rdiv_rate}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:err_l_dp}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:err_r_dp}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:str_dp}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:rdiv_accepted_bounds}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:iter_bias}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:iter_rdiv_stability}, Corollary~\ref{cor:iter_rdiv_discrepancy_bound}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:iter_rdiv_accepted_bounds}, \cite[Lemma 14]{foster2023orthogonal}, used in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lemma:err_l_dp}: $L_n(h)=\mathbb E_{n,\mathrm{fit}}[(Y-\hat{\mathcal T}h(Z))^2]$; the iterative procedure retains this loss and changes the penalty center. In Proposition~\ref{prop:exist_lambda}, Theorem~\ref{thm:tikhonv_rate}, Proposition~\ref{prop:exist_lambda_iter_trae}, Theorem~\ref{thm:iter_trae_rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:normality}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:concentrate_l}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:strong_convexity}, Corollary~\ref{cor:trae_discrepancy_bound}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:trae_accepted_bounds}, Lemma~\ref{eq:iter_trae_strong_propagation}, Corollary~\ref{cor:iter_trae_discrepancy_bound}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:iter_trae_accepted_stability}, \cite[Lemma 9]{bennett2023source}, used in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lemma:concentrate_l}: $L_n(h)=\sup_{f\in\mathcal F}\mathbb E_n[2m(W;f)-2h(X)f(Z)-f(Z)^2]$, with $\mathbb E_n$ the TRAE fitting-sample average; the iterative procedure retains this loss and changes the penalty center. In \cite[Lemma 14 (lem:lipschitz_wainwright); signed-tail evidence: lem:prob_localized and lem:peeling]{foster2023orthogonal}, used in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lemma:iter_rdiv_stability}, Lemma (Recursive RDIV stability): $L_n(h)=\mathbb E_{n,\mathrm{fit}}[(Y-\hat{\mathcal T}h(Z))^2]$; changing the penalty center in a concentration comparison does not change this fitting loss. In \cite[Lemma 9 (lem:concentration)]{bennett2023source}, used in the proof of Lemma~\ref{eq:iter_trae_strong_propagation}: $L_n(h)=\sup_{f\in\mathcal F}\mathbb E_n[2m(W;f)-2h(X)f(Z)-f(Z)^2]$, where $\mathbb E_n$ is the TRAE fitting-sample average; changing the penalty center in a concentration comparison does not change this fitting loss.
- $L$: $L$ denotes the method-specific population loss functional, defined using population expectations. In Lemma~\ref{lemma:t_err_dp}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:err_l_dp}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:err_r_dp}, \cite[Lemma 14]{foster2023orthogonal}, used in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lemma:err_l_dp}: $L(h)=\mathbb E[(Y-\mathcal T h(Z))^2]=\sigma^2_{Y\mid Z}+\|\mathcal T(h-h_0)\|^2$, where $\sigma^2_{Y\mid Z}=\mathbb E[(Y-r_0(Z))^2]$. In Proposition~\ref{prop:exist_lambda}, Theorem~\ref{thm:tikhonv_rate}, Proposition~\ref{prop:exist_lambda_iter_trae}, Theorem~\ref{thm:iter_trae_rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:normality}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:concentrate_l}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:strong_convexity}, Corollary~\ref{cor:trae_discrepancy_bound}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:trae_accepted_bounds}, Lemma~\ref{eq:iter_trae_strong_propagation}, Corollary~\ref{cor:iter_trae_discrepancy_bound}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:iter_trae_accepted_stability}, \cite[Lemma 9]{bennett2023source}, used in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lemma:concentrate_l}: $L(h)=\sup_{f\in\mathcal F}\mathbb E[2m(W;f)-2h(X)f(Z)-f(Z)^2]$; under the stated closeness assumption, $L(h)=\|\mathcal T(h-h_0)\|^2$ for $h\in\mathcal H$. In \cite[Lemma 14]{foster2023orthogonal}, used in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lemma:strong_convexity}: $L(h)=\sup_{f\in\mathcal F}\mathbb E[2m(W;f)-2h(X)f(Z)-f(Z)^2]$; under the stated closeness assumption, $L(h)=\|\mathcal T(h-h_0)\|^2$ for $h\in\mathcal H$. In \cite[Lemma 9 (lem:concentration)]{bennett2023source}, used in the proof of Lemma~\ref{eq:iter_trae_strong_propagation}: $L(h)=\sup_{f\in\mathcal F}\mathbb E[2m(W;f)-2h(X)f(Z)-f(Z)^2]$; under the stated closeness assumption, $L(h)=\|\mathcal T(h-h_0)\|^2$ for $h\in\mathcal H$.
- $B$: $B$ is a fixed finite nonnegative uniform envelope bound for the quantities specified in the applicable boundedness assumptions. In Proposition~\ref{prop:exist_lambda}, Theorem~\ref{thm:tikhonv_rate}, Proposition~\ref{prop:exist_lambda_iter_trae}, Theorem~\ref{thm:iter_trae_rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:normality}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:concentrate_l}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:strong_convexity}, Corollary~\ref{cor:trae_discrepancy_bound}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:trae_accepted_bounds}, Lemma~\ref{eq:iter_trae_strong_propagation}, Corollary~\ref{cor:iter_trae_discrepancy_bound}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:iter_trae_accepted_stability}, \cite[Lemma 9]{bennett2023source}, used in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lemma:concentrate_l}, \cite[Lemma 14]{foster2023orthogonal}, used in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lemma:strong_convexity}: $|m(W;f)|$, $|h(X)|$, and $|f(Z)|$ are almost surely bounded by $B$, uniformly over $h\in\mathcal H$ and $f\in\mathcal F$; for a dual fit the corresponding bound uses the exchanged primal and dual objects. In Proposition~\ref{prop:exist_lambda_deepiv}, Theorem~\ref{thm:deepiv_rate}, Proposition~\ref{prop:exist_lambda_iter_rdiv}, Theorem~\ref{thm:iter_rdiv_rate}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:t_err_dp}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:err_l_dp}, Corollary~\ref{cor:rdiv_discrepancy_bound}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:rdiv_accepted_bounds}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:iter_rdiv_stability}, Corollary~\ref{cor:iter_rdiv_discrepancy_bound}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:iter_rdiv_accepted_bounds}, \cite[Lemma 14]{foster2023orthogonal}, used in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lemma:err_l_dp}: $|Y|\leqslant B$ almost surely, $|h(x)|\leqslant B$ for every $h\in\mathcal H$ on the common support of the conditional densities, and $|\hat r(z)|\leqslant B$. In \cite[Lemma 9 (lem:concentration)]{bennett2023source}, used in the proof of Lemma~\ref{eq:iter_trae_strong_propagation}: $|m(W;f)|$, $|h(X)|$, and $|f(Z)|$ are almost surely bounded by $B$, uniformly over $h\in\mathcal H$ and $f\in\mathcal F$.
- $c_0$: $c_0>0$ is a fixed finite constant, independent of sample size, such that $g_0(x\mid z)\geqslant c_0$ on the common conditional-density support.
- $C_G$: $C_G>0$ is a fixed finite constant, independent of sample size, such that $\sup_{g\in\mathcal G}\|g\|_\infty\leqslant C_G$ on the common conditional-density support.
- $\mathcal T_g$: For every $g\in\mathcal G$, $\mathcal T_g$ maps $h\in\mathcal H$ to the $P_Z$-square-integrable function $(\mathcal T_g h)(z)=\int h(x)g(x\mid z)\,\mu(dx)$, with $z$ understood $P_Z$-almost everywhere.
- $\mathcal G_0$: $\mathcal G_0$ is the measurable class $\{(x,z)\mapsto\sqrt{\{g(x\mid z)+g_0(x\mid z)\}/\{2g_0(x\mid z)\}}:g\in\mathcal G\}$ on the common conditional-density support, with input distribution the true joint law of $(X,Z)$ and positive denominator there.
- $\eta_n$: $\eta_n=\xi/(K_n+1)$ is the deterministic grid confidence allocation at the prescribed failure level $\xi$. In Corollary~\ref{cor:normality}: For each of the primal and dual tuning rules, the prescribed failure level is $\xi_n/2$, so $\eta_n=\xi_n/\{2(K_n+1)\}$, where $\xi_n\downarrow0$ is deterministic.
- $\delta_{n,r}$: $\delta_{n,r}=\delta_{n,r}(\xi)\geqslant0$ is a prescribed deterministic, $\beta$-independent accuracy bound such that $\|\hat r-r_0\|_{L_2(Z)}\leqslant\delta_{n,r}$ with probability at least $1-\xi$ over the preliminary sample. In Lemma~\ref{lemma:t_err_dp}: The pilot-regression accuracy bound is evaluated at failure probability $\xi/2$ and relabelled $\delta_{n,r}$, so here $\delta_{n,r}$ denotes $\delta_{n,r}(\xi/2)$ and satisfies $\|\hat r-r_0\|_{L_2(Z)}\leqslant\delta_{n,r}$ with probability at least $1-\xi/2$ over the preliminary sample.
- $\delta_{n,\mathcal G}$: $\delta_{n,\mathcal G}$ is a prescribed deterministic, $\beta$-independent upper bound on the critical radius of $\mathcal G_0$ under the joint input distribution of $(X,Z)$, evaluated at the failure probability allocated to the conditional-density fit and satisfying $\delta_{n,\mathcal G}\geqslant C\sqrt{\{\log\log(en)+\log(1/\xi)\}/n}$. In Lemma~\ref{lemma:t_err_dp}: The conditional-density bound is evaluated at failure probability $\xi/2$ and relabelled $\delta_{n,\mathcal G}$, so here $\delta_{n,\mathcal G}$ denotes $\delta_{n,\mathcal G}(\xi/2)$.
- $\delta_{n,\mathcal H}$: $\delta_{n,\mathcal H}=\delta_{n,\mathcal H}(\eta_n)$ is a prescribed deterministic common upper bound on the critical radii of $\mathcal H$ under the distribution of $X$ and of $\mathcal T_g\mathcal H$ under the distribution of $Z$, uniformly for $g\in\mathcal G$, with $\eta_n=\xi/(K_n+1)$ and $\delta_{n,\mathcal H}\geqslant C\sqrt{\{\log\log(en)+\log(1/\eta_n)\}/n}$. In Proposition~\ref{prop:exist_lambda_iter_rdiv}, Theorem~\ref{thm:iter_rdiv_rate}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:iter_rdiv_stability}, Corollary~\ref{cor:iter_rdiv_discrepancy_bound}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:iter_rdiv_accepted_bounds}: The iterative concentration comparisons allocate failure probability $\eta_{n,t}=\xi/\{t(K_n+1)\}$ over iteration--grid pairs; the resulting confidence adjustment is absorbed into $\delta_n$ for fixed $t$.
- $\delta_n$: $\delta_n$ is a prescribed deterministic, method-specific statistical-error bound varying with $n$, calibrated before fitting by fixed multipliers independent of $n$, $\lambda$, and the unknown source parameter. In Proposition~\ref{prop:exist_lambda_iter_rdiv}, Theorem~\ref{thm:iter_rdiv_rate}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:iter_rdiv_stability}, Corollary~\ref{cor:iter_rdiv_discrepancy_bound}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:iter_rdiv_accepted_bounds}: The iteration--grid confidence allocation is $\eta_{n,t}=\xi/\{t(K_n+1)\}$; the resulting $\log t$ contribution is absorbed into a constant adjustment of $\delta_n$ for fixed $t$. In Proposition~\ref{prop:exist_lambda}, Theorem~\ref{thm:tikhonv_rate}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:concentrate_l}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:strong_convexity}, Corollary~\ref{cor:trae_discrepancy_bound}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:trae_accepted_bounds}, \cite[Lemma 9]{bennett2023source}, used in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lemma:concentrate_l}, \cite[Lemma 14]{foster2023orthogonal}, used in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lemma:strong_convexity}: $\delta_n$ is the TRAE scale, unrelated to the RDIV scales, satisfying $\delta_n\geqslant\max\{\tilde\delta_n(\eta_n),n^{-1/2}\}$ and $\delta_n=o(1)$, with $\eta_n=\xi/(K_n+1)$. In Corollary~\ref{cor:normality}: $\delta_n$ is a common deterministic TRAE bound for both primal and dual problems, each tuned at confidence level $\xi_n/2$ for a deterministic $\xi_n\downarrow0$, and additionally satisfies $\delta_n=o(n^{-\alpha_{\mathrm{dr}}})$, where $\alpha_{\mathrm{dr}}=(1+\min\{\beta_m,1\})/(2+4\min\{\beta_m,1\})$ and $\beta_m=\max\{\beta_h,\beta_q\}$. In Proposition~\ref{prop:exist_lambda_iter_trae}, Theorem~\ref{thm:iter_trae_rate}, Lemma~\ref{eq:iter_trae_strong_propagation}, Corollary~\ref{cor:iter_trae_discrepancy_bound}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:iter_trae_accepted_stability}: $\delta_n$ is the TRAE scale satisfying $\delta_n\geqslant\max\{\tilde\delta_n(\eta_{n,t}),n^{-1/2}\}$ and $\delta_n=o(1)$, with $\eta_{n,t}=\xi/\{t(K_n+1)\}$. In Proposition~\ref{prop:exist_lambda_deepiv}, Theorem~\ref{thm:deepiv_rate}, Proposition~\ref{prop:exist_lambda_iter_rdiv}, Theorem~\ref{thm:iter_rdiv_rate}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:t_err_dp}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:err_l_dp}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:err_r_dp}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:str_dp}, Corollary~\ref{cor:rdiv_discrepancy_bound}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:rdiv_accepted_bounds}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:iter_rdiv_stability}, Corollary~\ref{cor:iter_rdiv_discrepancy_bound}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:iter_rdiv_accepted_bounds}, \cite[Lemma 14]{foster2023orthogonal}, used in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lemma:err_l_dp}: $\delta_n$ is the RDIV fitting scale satisfying $\delta_n\geqslant\delta_{n,\mathcal G}+\delta_{n,\mathcal H}+\delta_{n,\xi}$ and $\delta_n+\nu_n=o(1)$.
- $\nu_n$: $\nu_n$ is a prescribed deterministic RDIV projected-discrepancy accuracy bound, fixed before fitting the candidates, with $\nu_n\geqslant\delta_{n,\mathcal G}+\delta_{n,r}+\delta_{n,\xi}$ and $\delta_n+\nu_n=o(1)$; its calibration multipliers are independent of $n$, $\lambda$, and the unknown source parameter. In Proposition~\ref{prop:exist_lambda_iter_rdiv}, Theorem~\ref{thm:iter_rdiv_rate}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:iter_rdiv_stability}, Corollary~\ref{cor:iter_rdiv_discrepancy_bound}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:iter_rdiv_accepted_bounds}: The bounds use the iteration--grid confidence allocation $\eta_{n,t}=\xi/\{t(K_n+1)\}$; the resulting $\log t$ contribution is absorbed into a constant adjustment of $\nu_n$ for the prescribed fixed $t$. In \cite[Lemma 14 (lem:lipschitz_wainwright); signed-tail evidence: lem:prob_localized and lem:peeling]{foster2023orthogonal}, used in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lemma:iter_rdiv_stability}, Lemma (Recursive RDIV stability): The bounds use the iteration--grid confidence allocation $\eta_{n,t}=\xi/\{t(K_n+1)\}$; the resulting $\log t$ contribution is absorbed into a constant adjustment of $\nu_n$ for the prescribed fixed $t$.
- $\tilde\delta_n$: $\tilde\delta_n(\xi)$ is a prescribed common critical-radius upper bound indexed by sample size $n$ and failure probability $\xi\in(0,1)$ for the five star-shaped classes specified in Assumption~\ref{asp:critical_radius}.
- $\alpha$: $\alpha$ is local notation whose scalar-exponent and RKHS-coefficient meanings are distinct. In Proposition~\ref{prop:rkhs_qp}: $\alpha\in\mathbb R^N$ is the optimization variable representing $h_\alpha(\cdot)=\sum_{j=1}^N\alpha_j k_{\mathcal H}(X_j,\cdot)$; its fitting-sample evaluation vector is $K_{\mathcal H}\alpha$. In Theorem~\ref{thm:deepiv_rate}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:variational_source}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:rdiv_accepted_bounds}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:strong_convexity}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:trae_accepted_bounds}: $\alpha=\min\{\beta,1\}/(1+\min\{\beta,1\})=(p-1)/p$ is the scalar interpolation exponent.
- $\lambda_{\mathrm R}^\circ$: Whenever the defining set is nonempty, $\lambda_{\mathrm R}^\circ$ is the deterministic oracle point $2\rho^{k^\circ}$, where $k^\circ=\min\{k\in\{0,\ldots,K_n\}:2\rho^k\leqslant c_{\mathrm R}\max\{\delta_n^{2/(p+1)},\nu_n^{2/p}\}\}$ and $c_{\mathrm R}>0$ is a sufficiently small fixed constant; this point exists for all sufficiently large $n$.
- $\lambda_{\mathrm T}^\circ$: For all sufficiently large $n$, $\lambda_{\mathrm T}^\circ$ is the deterministic oracle point $2\rho^{k^\circ}$, where $k^\circ=\min\{k\in\{0,\ldots,K_n\}:2\rho^k\leqslant c_{\mathrm T}\delta_n^{2/p}\}$, $c_{\mathrm T}>0$ is a sufficiently small fixed constant, and $\delta_n$ is the TRAE scale in \eqref{eq:delta_trae}.
- $t$: $t\geqslant1$ is a prescribed integer iteration count held fixed as $n$ varies and chosen independently of the data and the source parameter $\beta$.
- $a_t$: $a_t\geqslant1$ is a deterministic calibrated RDIV propagation constant depending only on the prescribed $t$ and fixed population quantities, independent of $n$ and $\lambda$.
- $\lambda_{\mathrm{IR}}^\circ$: Whenever the defining set is nonempty, $\lambda_{\mathrm{IR}}^\circ$ is the deterministic oracle point $2\rho^{k^\circ}$, where $k^\circ=\min\{k\in\{0,\ldots,K_n\}:2\rho^k\leqslant c_{\mathrm{IR}}\max\{(s_{n,t}^{\mathrm R})^{1/(q_t+1)},\nu_n^{2/q_t}\}\}$ and $c_{\mathrm{IR}}>0$ is a sufficiently small fixed constant; this point exists for all sufficiently large $n$.
- $b_t$: $b_t\geqslant2$ is a calibrated deterministic propagation constant depending only on the prescribed iteration count $t$ and the fixed boundedness and mean-square-continuity constants, independently of $n$, $\beta$, and $\|w_0\|$. In Lemma~\ref{eq:iter_trae_strong_propagation}: $b_t$ is the terminal member of a nondecreasing sequence with $b_0=0$ and $b_j\geqslant2$ for $j=1,\ldots,t$; in the proof one may choose $b_j=C(1+b_{j-1})$ for a sufficiently large fixed $C\geqslant2$.
- $\lambda_{\mathrm{IT}}^\circ$: For all sufficiently large $n$, $\lambda_{\mathrm{IT}}^\circ$ is the deterministic oracle point $2\rho^{k^\circ}$, where $k^\circ=\min\{k\in\{0,\ldots,K_n\}:2\rho^k\leqslant c_{\mathrm{IT}}(s_{n,t}^{\mathrm T})^{1/(q_t+1)}\}$ and $c_{\mathrm{IT}}>0$ is a sufficiently small fixed constant.
- $h_{j,\lambda}^*$: $h_{j,\lambda}^*$ is the population minimizer over $\tilde{\mathcal H}$ for positive finite $\lambda$, initialized by $h_{0,\lambda}^*=0$ and recursively defined for positive integer $j$; the iterative procedures use $j=1,\ldots,t$. In Theorem~\ref{thm:iter_trae_rate}, Lemma~\ref{eq:iter_trae_strong_propagation}, Corollary~\ref{cor:iter_trae_discrepancy_bound}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:iter_trae_accepted_stability}: $h_{j,\lambda}^*\in\mathcal H$ for $j=1,\ldots,t$ and $\lambda\in\Lambda_n\cap[\lambda_{\mathrm{IT}}^\circ,2]$, for every $n$ for which the oracle point is defined. In Theorem~\ref{thm:iter_rdiv_rate}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:iter_rdiv_stability}, Corollary~\ref{cor:iter_rdiv_discrepancy_bound}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:iter_rdiv_accepted_bounds}: $h_{j,\lambda}^*\in\mathcal H$ for $j=1,\ldots,t$ and $\lambda\in\Lambda_n\cap[\lambda_{\mathrm{IR}}^\circ,2]$, for every $n$ for which the oracle point is defined.
- $\hat h_{j,\lambda}$: $\hat h_{j,\lambda}$ denotes a selected measurable empirical minimizer in the recursion initialized by $\hat h_{0,\lambda}=0$, with $j=1,\ldots,t$ and the empirical penalty norm evaluated on the fitting sample. In Proposition~\ref{prop:exist_lambda_iter_rdiv}, Theorem~\ref{thm:iter_rdiv_rate}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:iter_rdiv_stability}, Corollary~\ref{cor:iter_rdiv_discrepancy_bound}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:iter_rdiv_accepted_bounds}: The loss in the recursion is $L_n(h)=\mathbb E_{n,\mathrm{fit}}[(Y-\hat{\mathcal T}h(Z))^2]$. In Proposition~\ref{prop:exist_lambda_iter_trae}, Theorem~\ref{thm:iter_trae_rate}, Lemma~\ref{eq:iter_trae_strong_propagation}, Corollary~\ref{cor:iter_trae_discrepancy_bound}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:iter_trae_accepted_stability}: The loss in the recursion is the adversarial loss $L_n(h)=\sup_{f\in\mathcal F}\mathbb E_n[2m(W;f)-2h(X)f(Z)-f(Z)^2]$. In Lemma~\ref{lemma:iter_rdiv_stability}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:iter_trae_accepted_stability}: The empirical penalty center remains $\hat h_{j-1,\lambda}$; the fixed-center concentration comparisons use population iterates as centers. In \cite[Lemma 14 (lem:lipschitz_wainwright); signed-tail evidence: lem:prob_localized and lem:peeling]{foster2023orthogonal}, used in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lemma:iter_rdiv_stability}, Lemma (Recursive RDIV stability): The empirical iterates use $L_n(h)=\mathbb E_{n,\mathrm{fit}}[(Y-\hat{\mathcal T}h(Z))^2]$ and penalty center $\hat h_{j-1,\lambda}$; the fixed-center concentration comparisons use the deterministic population iterates as comparators and centers. In \cite[Lemma 9 (lem:concentration)]{bennett2023source}, used in the proof of Lemma~\ref{eq:iter_trae_strong_propagation}: The empirical iterates use $L_n(h)=\sup_{f\in\mathcal F}\mathbb E_n[2m(W;f)-2h(X)f(Z)-f(Z)^2]$ and penalty center $\hat h_{j-1,\lambda}$; the fixed-center concentration comparisons use the deterministic population iterates as comparators and centers.
- $a_j$: $\{a_j\}_{j=0}^t$ is a deterministic RDIV propagation sequence initialized at $a_0=0$, with $a_j\geqslant1$ for $1\leqslant j\leqslant t$; each positive-index term depends only on $j$ and fixed population quantities and is independent of $n$ and $\lambda$.
- $b_j$: $\{b_j\}_{j=0}^t$ is a nondecreasing deterministic propagation sequence with $b_0=0$ and $b_j\geqslant2$ for $1\leqslant j\leqslant t$, each positive-index term depending only on $j$ and the fixed boundedness and mean-square-continuity constants, independently of $\beta$ and $\|w_0\|$.
- $\mathcal E_n^{\mathrm R}$: $\mathcal E_n^{\mathrm R}$ is the intersection of the preliminary, fitting, and calibration events under the paper's confidence convention, with probability at least $1-\xi$, on which the fitting comparisons hold simultaneously for every $h\in\mathcal H$ and $\lambda\in\Lambda_n$, and the projected calibration comparisons hold simultaneously for the fitted candidates $\hat h_\lambda$, $\lambda\in\Lambda_n$.
- $\mathcal E_{n,t}^{\mathrm{IR}}$: $\mathcal E_{n,t}^{\mathrm{IR}}$ is the common preliminary, recursive fitting, and final calibration event constructed with allocation $\eta_{n,t}=\xi/\{t(K_n+1)\}$, of probability at least $1-\xi$, on which the recursive RDIV stability comparisons hold simultaneously for $j=1,\ldots,t$ and $\lambda\in\Lambda_n\cap[\lambda_{\mathrm{IR}}^\circ,2]$, and the projected calibration comparison holds for the final candidates on that same grid segment.
- $\mathcal E_{n,t}^{\mathrm{IT}}$: $\mathcal E_{n,t}^{\mathrm{IT}}$ is the common iterative TRAE concentration event obtained by intersecting the requisite iteration--grid concentration events with failure-probability allocation $\eta_{n,t}=\xi/\{t(K_n+1)\}$, with probability at least $1-\xi$ under the paper's confidence convention. In Lemma~\ref{eq:iter_trae_strong_propagation}, Corollary~\ref{cor:iter_trae_discrepancy_bound}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:iter_trae_accepted_stability}: The concentration comparisons are simultaneous over the oracle-relevant grid segment $\Lambda_n\cap[\lambda_{\mathrm{IT}}^\circ,2]$, including the recursive comparisons for $j=1,\ldots,t$.
- $b$: $b=\min\{\beta,1\}\in(0,1]$ is the truncated source exponent.
- $r_\lambda$: $r_\lambda(u)=\lambda/(u+\lambda)$ for finite $\lambda>0$ and $u\geqslant0$, and $r_\lambda(\mathcal A)$ denotes this function applied to $\mathcal A$ by spectral functional calculus.
- $H^2$: $H^2(g_1,g_2)=\int(\sqrt{g_1(x)}-\sqrt{g_2(x)})^2\,\mu(dx)$ is defined for probability densities $g_1,g_2$ with respect to the common measure $\mu$ on $\mathcal X$; for conditional densities it is evaluated at each fixed $z$, with any averaging over $Z$ written separately.
- $\beta_h$: $\beta_h>0$ is a source exponent satisfying $h_0=(\mathcal T^*\mathcal T)^{\beta_h/2}w_h$ for some $w_h\in\tilde{\mathcal H}$; it need not be the largest exponent for which such a representation exists.
- $\beta_q$: $\beta_q>0$ is a source exponent satisfying $q_0=(\mathcal T\mathcal T^*)^{\beta_q/2}w_q$ for some $w_q\in\tilde{\mathcal Q}$; it need not be the largest exponent for which such a representation exists.
- $\boldsymbol y$: $\boldsymbol y$ denotes the vector of outcomes on the fitting block, on the outcome scale used by the fit. In Proposition~\ref{prop:rkhs_qp}: $\boldsymbol y=(Y_1,\ldots,Y_N)^\top$, with $N=n_1$ the fitting-sample size.
