  What Lean assumes for it: `Papers.TsAdaptiveEstimationInference.LipschitzVectorLocalizedConcentration` (`Helpers/RDIVSetup.lean:896`)

  ```lean
  noncomputable def LipschitzVectorLocalizedConcentration
      (V : IVWorld 𝒲 𝒳 𝒵) (P : Measure Ω)
      (sample : AnalysisSample V P) (split : SampleSplit) : Sort 0 :=
    IsProbabilityMeasure P →
    ∀ (mu : Measure 𝒳) (G : Set (𝒳 → 𝒵 → ℝ)) (g₀ : 𝒳 → 𝒵 → ℝ)
      (gHat : ℕ → Ω → 𝒳 → 𝒵 → ℝ) (support : Set (𝒳 × 𝒵))
      (B c₀ CG ρ : ℝ),
      RDIVSetting V mu g₀ → RangeCondition V → H0MemHypothesisClass V →
      DensityRealizable V mu G g₀ → DensityBounds G g₀ support c₀ CG →
      OutcomeBounded V B → HypothesisClassBounded V support B →
      MLEMaximizerSelected V P sample split G gHat →
      RDIVClassesMeasurable V P sample split mu G g₀ gHat →
      RDIVFittingSupremumVersion V P sample split mu gHat ρ →
      ∃ C > 0, ∀ (n : ℕ) (ζ r lam : ℝ), 0 < n →
        ζ ∈ Set.Ioo (0 : ℝ) 1 → lam ∈ regGrid ρ n → lam ∈ Set.Ioc (0 : ℝ) 2 →
        confidenceFloor n ζ ≤ r →
        IsCriticalRadiusBound (V.law.map V.xOf) (primalVersion V '' V.H) n r →
        (∀ g ∈ G, IsCriticalRadiusBound (V.law.map V.zOf)
          (densityOperator mu g '' (primalVersion V '' V.H)) n r) →
        ∀ᵐ preliminary ∂P,
          1 - ζ ≤ P.real {ω | ∀ h ∈ V.H,
            |(conditionalRisk V mu gHat n preliminary lam h -
                conditionalRisk V mu gHat n preliminary lam (minNormSolution V)) -
              (empiricalRisk V P sample split mu (fun _ _ => gHat n preliminary) n ω lam h -
                empiricalRisk V P sample split mu (fun _ _ => gHat n preliminary) n ω lam
                  (minNormSolution V))| ≤
            C * (r * inputL2Norm (V.law.map V.zOf)
              (densityOperator mu (gHat n preliminary)
                (primalVersion V h - primalVersion V (minNormSolution V))) +
              r * ‖h - minNormSolution V‖ + r ^ 2)}
  ```

  The passage of the proof:

  > For each fixed $\lambda\in\Lambda_n$, apply the localized concentration inequality in \citet[Lemma~14]{foster2023orthogonal} to the two-coordinate class
  > \[
  > h\longmapsto\{\hat{\mathcal T}h(Z),h(X)\}
  > \]
  > at the fixed comparator $h_0$ and to the loss
  > $\phi_\lambda(y,u,v)=(y-u)^2+\lambda v^2$. This loss is uniformly Lipschitz on the bounded range because $\lambda\leqslant2$. The critical-radius bounds in \cref{asp:cr_deepiv} give
  > \[
  > \left|\{\widehat R^\lambda(h)-\widehat R^\lambda(h_0)\}
  > -\{R_n^\lambda(h)-R_n^\lambda(h_0)\}\right|
  > \leqslant C\left[\delta_{n,\mathcal H}
  > \{\|\hat{\mathcal T}(h-h_0)\|+\|h-h_0\|\}
  > +\delta_{n,\mathcal H}^2\right].
  > \]
- li2024regularized, Localized Concentration (source label lem:local-concen) — checked against source supplied by the user at discovery/cited_sources/li2024regularized.txt

  What Lean assumes for it: `Papers.TsAdaptiveEstimationInference.LiLocalizedConcentration` (`Helpers/RDIVSetup.lean:308`)

  ```lean
  noncomputable def LiLocalizedConcentration : Sort 0 :=
    ∀ (mu : Measure 𝒳) (G : Set (𝒳 → 𝒵 → ℝ)) (g₀ : 𝒳 → 𝒵 → ℝ)
      (support : Set (𝒳 × 𝒵)) (c₀ CG : ℝ),
      RDIVSetting V mu g₀ → DensityRealizable V mu G g₀ → DensityBounds G g₀ support c₀ CG →
      LocalizedProcessesMeasurable (V.law.map (fun w => (V.xOf w, V.zOf w)))
        (likelihoodTransformClass G g₀) →
      ∀ (δG : ℕ → ℝ → ℝ), DensityCriticalRadiusBound V G g₀ δG →
      (∃ c > 0, ∀ n : ℕ, 0 < n → ∀ ξ ∈ Set.Ioo (0 : ℝ) 1,
        c * confidenceFloor n ξ ≤ δG n ξ) →
      ∃ A > 0, ∀ n : ℕ, 0 < n → ∀ ξ ∈ Set.Ioo (0 : ℝ) 1,
        ∃ E : Set (Fin n → 𝒳 × 𝒵), MeasurableSet E ∧
          1 - ξ / 2 ≤ (Measure.pi (fun _ : Fin n =>
            V.law.map (fun w => (V.xOf w, V.zOf w)))).real E ∧
          ∀ x ∈ E, ∀ g ∈ G,
            let f := fun y : 𝒳 × 𝒵 =>
              Real.sqrt ((g y.1 y.2 + g₀ y.1 y.2) / (2 * g₀ y.1 y.2))
            |(n : ℝ)⁻¹ * (∑ i, -Real.log (f (x i))) -
              (∫ y, -Real.log (f y) ∂V.law.map (fun w => (V.xOf w, V.zOf w)))| ≤
              A * (δG n (ξ / 2) *
                inputL2Norm (V.law.map (fun w => (V.xOf w, V.zOf w)))
                  (f - fun _ => 1) + δG n (ξ / 2) ^ 2)
  ```

  The passage of the proof:

  > We establish the density bound directly using the Localized Concentration lemma of \citet{li2024regularized}. Write $P=\mathbb E_{X,Z}$ and $P_n=\mathbb E_{n,\mathrm{pre}}$, and define
  > \[
  > f_g(x,z)=\sqrt{\frac{g(x\mid z)+g_0(x\mid z)}{2g_0(x\mid z)}},\qquad
  > \mathcal U=\{f_g-1:g\in\mathcal G\}.
  > \]
  > Realizability gives $f_{g_0}=1$, so $0\in\mathcal U$. The density bounds give
  > \[
  > 2^{-1/2}\leqslant f_g\leqslant M,\qquad
  > M=\sqrt{\frac{C_G/c_0+1}{2}}.
  > \]
  > Thus $\mathcal U$ is uniformly bounded by a fixed constant. Define the globally Lipschitz loss
  > \[
  > \ell(v)=-\log\bigl(\max\{1+v,2^{-1/2}\}\bigr).
  > \]
  > The maximum map is $1$-Lipschitz, and the derivative of $-\log t$ has absolute value at most $\sqrt2$ for $t\geqslant2^{-1/2}$. Hence $\ell$ is $\sqrt2$-Lipschitz, $\ell(0)=0$, and $\ell(f_g-1)=-\log f_g$.
  > 
  > We verify the localized complexity condition for this application. Put $\mathcal S=\operatorname{star}(\mathcal G_0-\mathcal G_0)$ and choose a deterministic critical radius $d\leqslant\delta_{n,\mathcal G}$ as supplied by Assumption~\ref{asp:cr_deepiv}. By the critical-radius definition, $\mathfrak R_n(d;\mathcal S)\leqslant d^2$. For $s\geqslant d$, star-shapedness implies that $(d/s)v\in\mathcal S$ and $\|(d/s)v\|_{L_2(X,Z)}\leqslant d$ whenever $v\in\mathcal S$ and $\|v\|_{L_2(X,Z)}\leqslant s$. Scaling inside the Rademacher supremum therefore gives
  > \[
  > \mathfrak R_n(s;\mathcal S)
  > \leqslant\frac{s}{d}\mathfrak R_n(d;\mathcal S)
  > \leqslant sd.
  > \]
  > Since $1\in\mathcal G_0$, we have $\operatorname{star}(\mathcal U)\subseteq\mathcal S$. Taking $s=\delta_{n,\mathcal G}$ yields
  > \[
  > \mathfrak R_n\bigl(\delta_{n,\mathcal G};\operatorname{star}(\mathcal U)\bigr)
  > \leqslant\delta_{n,\mathcal G}d
  > \leqslant\delta_{n,\mathcal G}^2.
  > \]
  > Apply the cited localized concentration lemma with input $(X,Z)$, one coordinate, class $\mathcal U$, fixed comparator $0$, loss $\ell$, and failure probability $\xi/2$. The measurability and logarithmic radius floor are supplied by Assumption~\ref{asp:cr_deepiv}, with the confidence evaluation specified in the proof. Consequently, for a fixed constant $A>0$, with probability at least $1-\xi/2$,
  > \[
  > \left|(P_n-P)(-\log f_g)\right|
  > \leqslant A\left\{\delta_{n,\mathcal G}\|f_g-1\|_{L_2(X,Z)}+\delta_{n,\mathcal G}^2\right\},
  > \qquad g\in\mathcal G.
  > \]

  ```lean
  noncomputable def IterRDIVFixedCenterLocalizedConcentration : Sort 0 :=
    ∀ (mu : Measure 𝒳) (G : Set (𝒳 → 𝒵 → ℝ)) (g₀ : 𝒳 → 𝒵 → ℝ)
      (support : Set (𝒳 × 𝒵)) (B c₀ CG : ℝ),
      RDIVSetting V mu g₀ → DensityRealizable V mu G g₀ →
      DensityBounds G g₀ support c₀ CG → OutcomeBounded V B →
      HypothesisClassBounded V support B →
      ∃ C > 0, ∃ c₁ > 0, ∃ c₂ > 0,
      ∀ (P : Measure Ω) (_hProbability : IsProbabilityMeasure P)
        (sample : AnalysisSample V P) (split : SampleSplit)
        (gHat : ℕ → Ω → 𝒳 → 𝒵 → ℝ) (ρ : ℝ),
      MLEMaximizerSelected V P sample split G gHat →
      RDIVClassesMeasurable V P sample split mu G g₀ gHat →
      IterRDIVFixedCenterSupremumVersion V P sample split mu gHat ρ →
      ∀ (n t j : ℕ) (lam r : ℝ) (sourceRadius : ℝ)
        (_hSourceScope : gridValue ρ (terminalIndex ρ n) ≤ sourceRadius ∧
          oraclePoint ρ n sourceRadius ≤ lam), 0 < n → 1 ≤ j → j ≤ t →
      lam ∈ regGrid ρ n → lam ∈ Set.Ioc (0 : ℝ) 2 →
      populationIterate V lam j ∈ V.H →
      (populationIterate V lam (j - 1) ∈ V.H ∨ j = 1) →
      0 < r → 1 < 2 * c₂ * n →
      8 * Real.log (41 * Real.log (2 * c₂ * n)) / (c₂ * n) ≤ r ^ 2 →
      iterRdivAnchoredRadius (V.law.map V.xOf) (primalVersion V '' V.H)
        (primalVersion V (populationIterate V lam j)) n r →
      (∀ g ∈ G, iterRdivAnchoredRadius (V.law.map V.zOf)
        (densityOperator mu g '' (primalVersion V '' V.H))
        (densityOperator mu g (primalVersion V (populationIterate V lam j))) n r) →
      ∀ᵐ a ∂P, ∃ E : Set (Fin n → 𝒲), MeasurableSet E ∧
        1 - 2 * c₁ * Real.exp (-c₂ * n * r ^ 2 / 8) ≤
          (Measure.pi (fun _ : Fin n => V.law)).real E ∧
        ∀ ws ∈ E, ∀ h ∈ V.H,
          let increment := iterRdivFixedCenterLoss V mu (gHat n a) lam j h -
            iterRdivFixedCenterLoss V mu (gHat n a) lam j (populationIterate V lam j)
          |(n : ℝ)⁻¹ * (∑ i, increment (ws i)) - ∫ w, increment w ∂V.law| ≤
            C * r * (inputL2Norm (V.law.map V.zOf)
              (densityOperator mu (gHat n a) (primalVersion V h -
                primalVersion V (populationIterate V lam j))) +
              ‖h - populationIterate V lam j‖ + r)
  ```

  ```lean
  noncomputable def TRAELocalizedConcentration : Sort 0 :=
    ∀ (B : ℝ) (tildeδ : ℕ → ℝ → ℝ),
      RangeCondition V → ClosenessCondition V → MeanSquareContinuity V →
      TRAEBoundedness V B → TRAECommonNullSetBoundedness V B → TRAECriticalRadiusBound V tildeδ →
      ∃ C > 0, ∀ (δ : ℕ → ℝ) (allocation : ℕ → ℝ),
      TRAEScaleCalibration δ tildeδ allocation →
      ∀ (n : ℕ), 0 < n → allocation n ∈ Set.Ioo (0 : ℝ) 1 →
      ∃ E : Set (Fin n → 𝒲), MeasurableSet E ∧
        1 - allocation n ≤ (Measure.pi (fun _ : Fin n => V.law)).real E ∧
        ∀ x ∈ E, ∀ h ∈ V.H, ∀ f ∈ V.F,
          let u := fun w => ![V.m f w,
            primalVersion V h (V.xOf w) * dualVersion V f (V.zOf w),
            dualVersion V f (V.zOf w)]
          |(n : ℝ)⁻¹ * (∑ i, traeClippedLoss B (x i) (u (x i))) -
            (∫ w, traeClippedLoss B w (u w) ∂V.law)| ≤
            C * (δ n * (∑ i : Fin 3, inputL2Norm V.law (fun w => u w i)) + δ n ^ 2)
  ```

  The passage of the proof:

  > The localized concentration bound used in \citet[Lemma~9]{bennett2023source}
  > gives
  > \begin{align*}
  > L( h) &=\sup_{f\in \mathcal{F}}\mathbb{E}\left[ 2( m( W;f) -f( Z) h( X)) -f^{2}( Z)\right] \\
  > & =\mathbb{E}\left[ 2( m( W;f_{h}) -f_{h}( Z) h( X)) -f_{h}^{2}( Z)\right]\\
  >  & \leqslant \mathbb{E}_{n}\left[ 2( m( W;f_{h}) -f_{h}( Z) h( X)) -f_{h}^{2}( Z)\right] +O\left( \delta _{n}^{2} +\delta _{n} \| f_{h} \| \right)\\
  >  & \leqslant \sup_{f\in \mathcal{F}}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[ 2( m( W;f) -f( Z) h( X)) -f^{2}( Z)\right] +O\left( \delta _{n}^{2} +\delta _{n} \| f_{h} \| \right)\\
  >  & \leqslant L_{n}( h) +O\left( \delta _{n}^{2} +\delta _{n} \| f_{h} \| \right) .
  > \end{align*}
  > Since $\|f_h\|^2=L(h)$, Young's inequality gives
  > \[
  > L( h) \leqslant 2L_{n}( h) +O\left( \delta _{n}^{2}\right) .
  > \]
  > Similarly, 
  > \begin{align*}
  > L_{n}( h)&  =\sup_{f\in \mathcal{F}}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[ 2( m( W;f) -f( Z) h( X)) -f^{2}( Z)\right] \\
  >  & \leqslant \sup_{f\in\mathcal F}\left\{\mathbb E\left[2(m(W;f)-f(Z)h(X))-f(Z)^2\right]+C\left(\delta_n^2+\delta_n\|f\|\right)\right\}\\
  >  & \leqslant \sup_{f\in\widetilde{\mathcal Q}}\mathbb{E}\left[ 2( m( W;f) -f( Z) h( X)) -\frac{4}{5} f^{2}( Z)\right] +O\left( \delta _{n}^{2}\right)\\
  >  & =\frac{5}{4}\|\mathcal T(h_0-h)\|^2+O\left( \delta _{n}^{2}\right)\\
  >  & =\frac{5}{4} L( h) +O\left( \delta _{n}^{2}\right) .
  > \end{align*}
- foster2023orthogonal, Lemma 14 — checked against source supplied by the user at discovery/cited_sources/foster2023orthogonal.txt

## Results

### 1. Oracle guarantee for the discrepancy rule `thm:common-dp`

| field | value |
| --- | --- |
| bucket | `verified_with_bookkeeping` |
| source | `main.tex:260-285` (`theorem`, label `thm:common_dp`) |
| statement | \label{thm:common_dp} Let $A_n:\Lambda_n\to[0,\infty)$, and let $\mathcal S_n\subseteq(0,2]$ be a stability region. Fix constants $C_A,C_V,C_b>0$ and exponents $\gamma\in(0,1]$ and $q>1$, and write $B_n(\lambda)=C_b\lambda^q$. Suppose that, on an event $\mathcal E_n$ with probability at least $1-\xi$, the discrepancy bound \begin{equation}\label{eq:common_discrepancy_bound} D_n(\hat h_\lambda)\leqslant A_n(\lambda)+B_n(\lambda), \end{equation} holds simultaneously over $\lambda\in\Lambda_n\cap\mathcal S_n$, and every $\lambda\in\Lambda_n\cap\mathcal S_n$ satisfies \begin{equation}\label{eq:common_accepted_bounds} \begin{aligned} \\|\mathcal T(\hat h_\lambda-h_0)\\|^2 &\leqslant C_A\{D_n(\hat h_\lambda)+A_n(\lambda)\},\\ \\|\hat h_\lambda-h_0\\|^2 &\leqslant C_V\left[\frac{D_n(\hat h_\lambda)+A_n(\lambda)}{\lambda}+\{D_n(\hat h_\lambda)+A_n(\lambda)\}^\gamma\right], \end{aligned} \end{equation} Suppose that some grid point $\lambda^\circ$ satisfies $\Lambda_n\cap[\lambda^\circ,2]\subseteq\mathcal S_n$, that $A_n$ is nonincreasing over the grid points in $[\lambda^\circ,2]$, and that \begin{equation}\label{eq:common_oracle_crossing} B_n(\lambda^\circ)\leqslant\tfrac12A_n(\lambda^\circ). \end{equation} Then \cref{alg:discrepancy} accepts a candidate before exhausting the grid, and returns a parameter satisfying $\lambda_{\mathrm{dp}}\geqslant\lambda^\circ$ with \begin{align}\label{eq:common_rate} \\|\mathcal T(\hat h_{\lambda_{\mathrm{dp}}}-h_0)\\|^2 &\leqslant O\{A_n(\lambda^\circ)\},\\ \\|\hat h_{\lambda_{\mathrm{dp}}}-h_0\\|^2 &\leqslant O\{A_n(\lambda^\circ)/\lambda^\circ+A_n(\lambda^\circ)^\gamma\}. \end{align} |

For every $n\geqslant N_{\mathrm R}(B)$, \cref{alg:discrepancy}, with the projected statistic \eqref{eq:rdiv_projected_loss} and noise level \eqref{eq:rdiv_common_scales}, accepts a candidate after at most $K_n+1=O(\log n)$ regularized fits, with probability at least $1-\xi$. ### 3. thm:deepiv_rate `thm:deepiv-rate`

| field | value |
| --- | --- |
| bucket | `conditional` |
| source | `main.tex:429-448` (`theorem`, label `thm:deepiv_rate`) |
| statement | \label{thm:deepiv_rate} Under the assumptions of \cref{prop:exist_lambda_deepiv}, let $\lambda_{\mathrm{dp}}$ be its output and write \[ p=\min\{\beta+1,2\}, \qquad \alpha=\frac{\min\{\beta,1\}}{1+\min\{\beta,1\}}, \qquad \varepsilon_{n,\beta}=\delta_n^{2p/(p+1)}+\nu_n^2. \] For every $n\geqslant N_{\mathrm R}(B)$, with probability at least $1-\xi$, \begin{align*} \\|\mathcal T(\hat h_{\lambda_{\mathrm{dp}}}-h_0)\\|^2 \leqslant O(\varepsilon_{n,\beta}), \quad \\|\hat h_{\lambda_{\mathrm{dp}}}-h_0\\|^2 \leqslant O\!\left( \delta_n^{2(p-1)/(p+1)} +\varepsilon_{n,\beta}^{\alpha}\right) \leqslant O(\varepsilon_{n,\beta}^\alpha). \end{align*} If $h_0=0$, the squared strong and weak errors are both $O(\delta_n^2)$. |

### 5. Adaptive iterative RDIV `thm:iter-rdiv-rate`

| field | value |
| --- | --- |
| bucket | `conditional` |
| source | `main.tex:531-553` (`theorem`, label `thm:iter_rdiv_rate`) |
| statement | \label{thm:iter_rdiv_rate} Under the assumptions of \cref{prop:exist_lambda_iter_rdiv,asp:iter_realizability_rdiv}, let $\lambda_{\mathrm{dp},t}^{\mathrm R}$ denote the selected parameter and set \[ \varepsilon_{n,t,\beta}^{\mathrm R} =(s_{n,t}^{\mathrm R})^{q_t/(q_t+1)}+\nu_n^2. \] For every fixed $t$ and every $n\geqslant N_{\mathrm R}(B)$, with probability at least $1-\xi$, \begin{align*} \\|\mathcal T(\hat h_{t,\lambda_{\mathrm{dp},t}^{\mathrm R}}-h_0)\\|^2 \leqslant O_t(\varepsilon_{n,t,\beta}^{\mathrm R}), \quad \\|\hat h_{t,\lambda_{\mathrm{dp},t}^{\mathrm R}}-h_0\\|^2 \leqslant O_t\!\left( (s_{n,t}^{\mathrm R})^{(q_t-1)/(q_t+1)} +(\varepsilon_{n,t,\beta}^{\mathrm R})^{\beta/(\beta+1)} \right). \end{align*} Here $O_t(\cdot)$ allows constants that depend on the prescribed iteration count and fixed population quantities. In particular, $a_t$ is independent of $n$. If $\nu_n=O(\delta_n)$ and $2t\geqslant\beta+1$, the bounds reduce to \begin{align*} \\|\hat h_{t,\lambda_{\mathrm{dp},t}^{\mathrm R}}-h_0\\|^2 =O_t\!\left(\delta_n^{2\beta/(\beta+2)}\right), \quad \\|\mathcal T(\hat h_{t,\lambda_{\mathrm{dp},t}^{\mathrm R}}-h_0)\\|^2 =O_t\!\left(\delta_n^{2(\beta+1)/(\beta+2)}\right). \end{align*} |

| statement | \label{cor:normality} Suppose that the assumptions of \cref{thm:tikhonv_rate} hold for both $h_0$ and $q_0$ with (potentially different) source conditions $\beta_h,\beta_q>0$. Choose a deterministic sequence $\xi_n\downarrow0$, and run the primal and dual tuning rules at confidence levels $\xi_n/2$. Let a common deterministic sequence $\delta_n$ satisfy \eqref{eq:delta_trae} for both problems at these confidence levels. Assume that, for some $\gamma_h,\gamma_q>0$, the score maps satisfy \begin{align*} \mathbb{E}\left[\left(\tilde{m}( W;h_{1}) -\tilde{m}( W;h_{2})\right)^{2}\right] & \leqslant C\\| h_{1} -h_{2} \\| ^{\gamma_h },\\ \mathbb{E}\left[\left(m( W;q_{1}) -m( W;q_{2})\right)^{2}\right] & \leqslant C\\| q_{1} -q_{2} \\| ^{\gamma_q } \end{align*} for all $h_1,h_2\in\mathcal H$ and $q_1,q_2\in\mathcal Q$. Let $\beta_m=\max\{\beta_h,\beta_q\}$ and assume that $\delta_n$ satisfies \[ \delta _{n} =o\left( n^{-\alpha _{\mathrm{dr}}}\right) ,\qquad \alpha _{\mathrm{dr}} :=\frac{1+\min\{\beta _{m} ,1\}}{2+4\min\{\beta _{m} ,1\}} . \] Let $\hat h,\hat q$ be the adaptive adversarial Tikhonov estimators, trained and tuned independently of the evaluation sample used in $\hat\theta(\hat h,\hat q)$. Finally, assume that $0<\sigma_0^2:=\mathbb E[\psi_0(W)^2]<\infty$. Then, \[ \sqrt{n}(\hat{\theta }(\hat{h},\hat{q}) -\theta _{0}) =\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum _{i=1}^{n} \psi _{0}( W_i) +o_{p}( 1) , \] where $\psi _{0}( W) =\tilde{m}( W;h_{0}) +m( W;q_{0}) -q_{0}( Z) h_{0}( X) -\theta _{0}$. Consequently, $\sqrt n(\hat\theta(\hat h,\hat q)-\theta_0)\rightsquigarrow N(0,\sigma_0^2)$. |

IndependentEvaluationSample currently supplies jointly measurable versions of the nuisance evaluations only, not of the fitted score maps. Sectionwise measurability and the displayed population $L_2$ continuity bounds do not by themselves supply evaluation coherence. Add only the following measurability bookkeeping to the theorem and corrected paper: writing $S(w;h,q)=\tilde m(w;h)+m(w;q)-h(X(w))q(Z(w))$ and $d_n(w)=S(w;\hat h,\hat q)-S(w;h_0,q_0)$, for each $n$ the fitted score difference admits a version jointly measurable in the full nuisance-training/tuning information and a fresh observation, agreeing with $d_n$ for almost every training realization and almost every fresh observation, and agreeing almost surely at every observation in the finite evaluation block. Preserve the existing independent-evaluation and nuisance-version requirements. This is only well-definedness of the evaluated random score and its conditional expectation, not a supplied probability bound, convergence claim or conditional Chebyshev conclusion. Do not add countability, separability, compactness, a uniform empirical-process condition, or joint measurability of every score on the entire hypothesis space. Choose a deterministic sequence $\xi_n\downarrow0$, and run the primal and dual tuning rules at confidence levels $\xi_n/2$. Let a common deterministic sequence $\delta_n$ satisfy \eqref{eq:delta_trae} for both problems at these confidence levels. Assume that, for some $\gamma_h,\gamma_q>0$, the score maps satisfy \begin{align*} \mathbb{E}\left[\left(\tilde{m}( W;h_{1}) -\tilde{m}( W;h_{2})\right)^{2}\right] & \leqslant C\\| h_{1} -h_{2} \\| ^{\gamma_h },\\ \mathbb{E}\left[\left(m( W;q_{1}) -m( W;q_{2})\right)^{2}\right] & \leqslant C\\| q_{1} -q_{2} \\| ^{\gamma_q } \end{align*} for all $h_1,h_2\in\mathcal H$ and $q_1,q_2\in\mathcal Q$. Let $\beta_m=\max\{\beta_h,\beta_q\}$ and assume that $\delta_n$ satisfies \[ \delta _{n} =o\left( n^{-\alpha _{\mathrm{dr}}}\right) ,\qquad \alpha _{\mathrm{dr}} :=\frac{1+\min\{\beta _{m} ,1\}}{2+4\min\{\beta _{m} ,1\}} . \] Let $\hat h,\hat q$ be the adaptive adversarial Tikhonov estimators, trained and tuned independently of the evaluation sample used in $\hat\theta(\hat h,\hat q)$. For each $n$, write $\mathcal G_n$ for the full nuisance-training and tuning sigma-field. Take jointly measurable versions of the fitted nuisance evaluations, representing the fitted functions for almost every training realization and agreeing almost surely with their evaluations at every observation in the finite evaluation sample. Write \[ S(w;h,q)=\tilde m(w;h)+m(w;q)-h(X(w))q(Z(w)),\qquad  d_n(w)=S(w;\hat h,\hat q)-S(w;h_0,q_0). \] Suppose that, for each $n$, $d_n$ admits a version jointly measurable in $\mathcal G_n$ and a fresh observation, agreeing with $d_n$ for almost every training realization and almost every fresh observation, and agreeing almost surely with $d_n(W_i)$ simultaneously for $i=1,\ldots,n$ in the evaluation sample. Finally, assume that $0<\sigma_0^2:=\mathbb E[\psi_0(W)^2]<\infty$. Then, \[ \sqrt{n}(\hat{\theta }(\hat{h},\hat{q}) -\theta _{0}) =\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum _{i=1}^{n} \psi _{0}( W_i) +o_{p}( 1) , \] where $\psi _{0}( W) =\tilde{m}( W;h_{0}) +m( W;q_{0}) -q_{0}( Z) h_{0}( X) -\theta _{0}$. Consequently, $\sqrt n(\hat\theta(\hat h,\hat q)-\theta_0)\rightsquigarrow N(0,\sigma_0^2)$. · proof change: Apply \cref{thm:tikhonv_rate} to both nuisance estimators at the prescribed confidence levels $\xi_n/2$. Their rate bounds then hold on a common event with probability at least $1-\xi_n\to1$. Because $\beta_h,\beta_q>0$ and $\delta_n\to0$, these bounds give \begin{equation}\label{eq:conv_hq} \\| \hat{h} -h_{0} \\| =o_{p}( 1) ,\qquad \\| \hat{q} -q_{0}\\| =o_{p}( 1) . \end{equation} The remaining bias condition is \begin{equation}\label{eq:square_root} \sqrt{n}\mathbb{E}[(\hat{q}( Z) -q_{0}( Z))(\hat{h}( X) -h_{0}( X))] =o_{p}( 1) . \end{equation} Suppose first that $\beta_m=\beta_h\geqslant\beta_q$. By the adjoint identity and the Cauchy--Schwarz inequality, \begin{align*} \left\|\mathbb{E}[(\hat{q}( Z) -q_{0}( Z))(\hat{h}( X) -h_{0}( X))]\right\| & \leqslant \\|\hat h-h_0\\|\,\\|\mathcal T^*(\hat q-q_0)\\|\\  & =O_{p}\left( \delta _{n}^{\min\{\beta _{m} ,1\} /( 1+\min\{\beta _{m} ,1\})} \cdot \delta _{n}\right)\\  & =O_{p}\left( \delta _{n}^{1+\min\{\beta _{m} ,1\} /( 1+\min\{\beta _{m} ,1\})}\right) . \end{align*} If $\beta_m=\beta_q\geqslant\beta_h$, the symmetric bound \[ \left\|\mathbb{E}[(\hat{q}( Z) -q_{0}( Z))(\hat{h}( X) -h_{0}( X))]\right\| \leqslant \\|\hat q-q_0\\|\,\\|\mathcal T(\hat h-h_0)\\| \] gives the same rate. By assumption, $\delta _{n} =o\left( n^{-( 1+\min\{\beta _{m} ,1\}) /( 2+4\min\{\beta _{m} ,1\})}\right)$, and hence \[ \left\|\mathbb{E}[(\hat{q}( Z) -q_{0}( Z))(\hat{h}( X) -h_{0}( X))]\right\| =o_{p}\left( n^{-1/2}\right), \] which proves \eqref{eq:square_root}.  Use the jointly measurable version of $d_n$ stipulated in Corollary~\ref{cor:normality}. Its agreement with the fitted score difference for almost every training realization and almost every fresh observation identifies its conditional population integrals with those of the score difference. Its almost sure agreement at every evaluation observation identifies its evaluation average with the difference of the two evaluated score averages. Conditional on the nuisance-training data, the evaluation observations are independent and identically distributed. Boundedness of the nuisance classes and the score continuity assumptions imply \[ \mathbb E[d_n(W)^2\mid\text{training}] \leqslant C\left\{ \\|\hat h-h_0\\|^{\gamma_h}+\\|\hat q-q_0\\|^{\gamma_q} +\\|\hat h-h_0\\|^2+\\|\hat q-q_0\\|^2 \right\}=o_p(1). \] Conditional Chebyshev's inequality therefore gives $\sqrt n(\mathbb E_{n,\mathrm{eval}}-\mathbb E)d_n=o_p(1)$, where $\mathbb E d_n$ integrates a fresh observation conditional on the training data. The primal and dual moment equations give \[ \mathbb E d_n =-\mathbb E[(\hat h(X)-h_0(X))(\hat q(Z)-q_0(Z))]. \] Combining this identity with \eqref{eq:square_root} yields \[ \sqrt n\{\hat\theta(\hat h,\hat q)-\theta_0\} =\frac1{\sqrt n}\sum_{i=1}^n\psi_0(W_i)+o_p(1). \] The moment equations also imply $\mathbb E\psi_0(W)=0$. The classical central limit theorem applies because $0<\mathbb E[\psi_0(W)^2]<\infty$, proving the stated normal limit. |

| statement | \label{lemma:variational_source} Let $\mathcal A=\mathcal T^*\mathcal T$, $b=\min\{\beta,1\}$, $p=1+b$, and $\alpha=b/(1+b)$. Under \cref{asp:source_condition}, every $u\in\widetilde{\mathcal H}$ satisfies \[ \|\langle h_0,u\rangle\| \leqslant \\|w_0\\|\,\\|\mathcal Tu\\|^b\\|u\\|^{1-b}. \] Consequently, for every fixed $\epsilon\in(0,1)$ and $\lambda\in(0,2]$, \begin{align*} 2\lambda\|\langle h_0,u\rangle\| &\leqslant \epsilon\{\\|\mathcal Tu\\|^2+\lambda\\|u\\|^2\} +C_\epsilon\\|w_0\\|^2\lambda^p,\\ 2\|\langle h_0,u\rangle\| &\leqslant \epsilon\\|u\\|^2 +C_\epsilon\\|w_0\\|^{2/(1+b)} \\|\mathcal Tu\\|^{2b/(1+b)}. \end{align*} |

### 12. Preliminary RDIV nuisance and operator bounds `lem:t-err-dp`

| findings | [refuted, V1.5] the step its proof takes from [li2024regularized, Theorem 24] (lem:conditional-mle-theorem-24) does not hold as the proof uses it: (a) The cited Theorem 24 explicitly imports the source-condition hypothesis of its referenced RDIV theorem. Lemma~\ref{lemma:t_err_dp} assumes only Assumptions~\ref{asp:re_cd} and \ref{asp:cr_deepiv}; these do not imply a source condition. Thus the citation, as stated, has an unestablished hypothesis. (b) The supplied theorem states an unsigned exponential confidence formula, and its proof explicitly gives $1-\exp(n\delta_n^2)$. Neither supplies the asserted probability $1-\xi/2$. The supplied localized concentration lemma has a usable confidence statement, but obtaining the density-only bound from it requires an explicit specialization rather than the printed direct invocation of Theorem 24. witness: (a) Let $X,Z\in[0,1]$, let $\phi_j$ be the mean-zero orthonormal Rademacher functions, and put $\sigma_j=2^{-j^2-2}$ and $g_0(x\mid z)=1+\sum_{j\geqslant1}\sigma_j\phi_j(x)\phi_j(z)$, with $Z$ uniform. This is a bounded conditional density bounded away from zero. Take the closed primal and dual spaces generated by the constants and the $\phi_j$, set $h_0=\sum_{j\geqslant1}j^{-2}\phi_j$, $r_0=\mathcal T h_0$, $Y=r_0(Z)$, $\hat r=r_0$, $\mathcal H=\{c h_0:1/2\leqslant c\leqslant3/2\}$, and $\mathcal G=\{g_0,1\}$. The host's density, boundedness, measurability, pilot, and critical-radius conditions can all be satisfied. However, for every $\beta>0$, $\sum_{j\geqslant1}j^{-4}\sigma_j^{-2\beta}=\infty$, so the source hypothesis required by the cited theorem fails. (b) The source proof's reported probability $1-\exp(n\delta_n^2)$ is nonpositive, hence gives no positive confidence guarantee; replacing it by $1-\xi/2$ does not follow from that formula. minimal patch (confirmed): proof change: Allocate failure probability $\xi/2$ to the conditional-density estimator and $\xi/2$ to the pilot regression. As elsewhere, evaluate the corresponding deterministic accuracy bounds at these confidence levels and relabel them $\delta_{n,\mathcal G}$ and $\delta_{n,r}$. We establish the density bound directly using the Localized Concentration lemma of \citet{li2024regularized}. Write $P=\mathbb E_{X,Z}$ and $P_n=\mathbb E_{n,\mathrm{pre}}$, and define \[ f_g(x,z)=\sqrt{\frac{g(x\mid z)+g_0(x\mid z)}{2g_0(x\mid z)}},\qquad \mathcal U=\{f_g-1:g\in\mathcal G\}. \] Realizability gives $f_{g_0}=1$, so $0\in\mathcal U$. The density bounds give \[ 2^{-1/2}\leqslant f_g\leqslant M,\qquad M=\sqrt{\frac{C_G/c_0+1}{2}}. \] Thus $\mathcal U$ is uniformly bounded by a fixed constant. Define the globally Lipschitz loss \[ \ell(v)=-\log\bigl(\max\{1+v,2^{-1/2}\}\bigr). \] The maximum map is $1$-Lipschitz, and the derivative of $-\log t$ has absolute value at most $\sqrt2$ for $t\geqslant2^{-1/2}$. Hence $\ell$ is $\sqrt2$-Lipschitz, $\ell(0)=0$, and $\ell(f_g-1)=-\log f_g$.  We verify the localized complexity condition for this application. Put $\mathcal S=\operatorname{star}(\mathcal G_0-\mathcal G_0)$ and choose a deterministic critical radius $d\leqslant\delta_{n,\mathcal G}$ as supplied by Assumption~\ref{asp:cr_deepiv}. By the critical-radius definition, $\mathfrak R_n(d;\mathcal S)\leqslant d^2$. For $s\geqslant d$, star-shapedness implies that $(d/s)v\in\mathcal S$ and $\\|(d/s)v\\|_{L_2(X,Z)}\leqslant d$ whenever $v\in\mathcal S$ and $\\|v\\|_{L_2(X,Z)}\leqslant s$. Scaling inside the Rademacher supremum therefore gives \[ \mathfrak R_n(s;\mathcal S) \leqslant\frac{s}{d}\mathfrak R_n(d;\mathcal S) \leqslant sd. \] Since $1\in\mathcal G_0$, we have $\operatorname{star}(\mathcal U)\subseteq\mathcal S$. Taking $s=\delta_{n,\mathcal G}$ yields \[ \mathfrak R_n\bigl(\delta_{n,\mathcal G};\operatorname{star}(\mathcal U)\bigr) \leqslant\delta_{n,\mathcal G}d \leqslant\delta_{n,\mathcal G}^2. \] Apply the cited localized concentration lemma with input $(X,Z)$, one coordinate, class $\mathcal U$, fixed comparator $0$, loss $\ell$, and failure probability $\xi/2$. The measurability and logarithmic radius floor are supplied by Assumption~\ref{asp:cr_deepiv}, with the confidence evaluation specified in the proof. Consequently, for a fixed constant $A>0$, with probability at least $1-\xi/2$, \[ \left\|(P_n-P)(-\log f_g)\right\| \leqslant A\left\{\delta_{n,\mathcal G}\\|f_g-1\\|_{L_2(X,Z)}+\delta_{n,\mathcal G}^2\right\}, \qquad g\in\mathcal G. \]  To use empirical optimality, note that $P_n\log g_0(X\mid Z)$ is finite by the density bounds. Since $\hat g$ maximizes the empirical log likelihood and all candidate densities are bounded above, $\hat g(X_i\mid Z_i)>0$ at every preliminary observation, almost surely. For $a>0$, the arithmetic-geometric mean inequality gives $(a+1)/2\geqslant\sqrt a$, and hence \[ -\log\sqrt{\frac{a+1}{2}}\leqslant-\frac14\log a. \] Taking $a=\hat g(X_i\mid Z_i)/g_0(X_i\mid Z_i)$ and averaging gives \[ P_n(-\log f_{\hat g}) \leqslant-\frac14P_n\log\frac{\hat g(X\mid Z)}{g_0(X\mid Z)} \leqslant0. \] For every $g\in\mathcal G$, conditional density normalization gives \[ P f_g^2 =\mathbb E_Z\int\frac{g(x\mid Z)+g_0(x\mid Z)}2\,\mu(dx)=1. \] Since $\log t\leqslant t-1$ for $t>0$, it follows that \[ P(-\log f_g)\geqslant1-Pf_g =\frac12P(f_g-1)^2. \] On the concentration event, put $b=\\|f_{\hat g}-1\\|_{L_2(X,Z)}$. Combining these inequalities and using Young's inequality gives \[ \frac{b^2}{2} \leqslant P(-\log f_{\hat g}) \leqslant A\delta_{n,\mathcal G}b+A\delta_{n,\mathcal G}^2 \leqslant\frac{b^2}{4}+(A^2+A)\delta_{n,\mathcal G}^2. \] Thus $b^2\leqslant4(A^2+A)\delta_{n,\mathcal G}^2$.  Finally, for $t\geqslant0$ and $f=\sqrt{(t^2+1)/2}$, we have $f\leqslant t+1$ and \[ \|t-1\|=\frac{2(f+1)}{t+1}\|f-1\|\leqslant4\|f-1\|. \] Apply this pointwise with $t=\sqrt{\hat g/g_0}$ and integrate under the true joint distribution to obtain \[ \begin{aligned} \mathbb E_Z\!\left[H^2\{\hat g(\cdot\mid Z),g_0(\cdot\mid Z)\}\right] &=P\left(\sqrt{\frac{\hat g(X\mid Z)}{g_0(X\mid Z)}}-1\right)^2\\ &\leqslant16b^2 \leqslant64(A^2+A)\delta_{n,\mathcal G}^2. \end{aligned} \] This proves \eqref{eq:rdiv_mle_hellinger} with probability at least $1-\xi/2$. The nuisance-rate assumption gives $\\|\hat r-r_0\\|_{L_2(Z)}\leqslant\delta_{n,r}$ with probability at least $1-\xi/2$. Therefore, without requiring independence between $\hat g$ and $\hat r$, the union bound shows that both inequalities hold simultaneously with probability at least $1-\xi$. We work on this intersection for the rest of the proof. Here \[ H^2(g_1,g_2)=\int\{\sqrt{g_1(x)}-\sqrt{g_2(x)}\}^2\,\mu(dx). \]  Let $P_X$ be the true marginal distribution of $X$ and let $\hat P_X$ be the marginal induced by $Z\sim P_Z$ and $X\mid Z\sim\hat g(\cdot\mid Z)$. The density bounds in \cref{asp:re_cd} give $\hat g(x\mid z)/g_0(x\mid z)\leqslant C_G/c_0$ and hence \[ \frac{d\hat P_X}{dP_X}\leqslant \frac{C_G}{c_0}. \] The total-variation--Hellinger inequality and $\\|h\\|_\infty\leqslant B$ give \[ \\|(\hat{\mathcal T}-\mathcal T)h\\|^2 \leqslant B^2\mathbb E_Z \left[\left\{\int\|\hat g(x\mid Z)-g_0(x\mid Z)\|\,\mu(dx)\right\}^2\right] \leqslant C B^2\delta_{n,\mathcal G}^2, \] which proves \eqref{eq:rdiv_op_global}. For $u=h-h'$, factor $\hat g/g_0-1=(\sqrt{\hat g/g_0}-1)(\sqrt{\hat g/g_0}+1)$. Cauchy--Schwarz and the conditional density-ratio bound yield \begin{align*} \\|(\hat{\mathcal T}-\mathcal T)u\\|_1 &\leqslant \mathbb E\left[\|u(X)\| \left\|\sqrt{\frac{\hat g(X\mid Z)}{g_0(X\mid Z)}}-1\right\| \left\{\sqrt{\frac{\hat g(X\mid Z)}{g_0(X\mid Z)}}+1\right\}\right]\\ &\leqslant C\\|u\\| \mathbb E_Z\!\left[H^2\{\hat g(\cdot\mid Z),g_0(\cdot\mid Z)\}\right]^{1/2} \leqslant C\delta_{n,\mathcal G}\\|u\\|, \end{align*} which proves \eqref{eq:rdiv_op_relative}. \citet{li2024regularized} use the same operator argument in their MLE analysis.  Finally, write $e_h=(\hat{\mathcal T}-\mathcal T)h$. Pointwise, \begin{align*} &(Y-\hat{\mathcal T}h)^2-(Y-\hat{\mathcal T}h')^2 -\{(Y-\mathcal T h)^2-(Y-\mathcal T h')^2\}\\ &\quad=-(\hat{\mathcal T}-\mathcal T)u (2Y-\hat{\mathcal T}h-\hat{\mathcal T}h') +\mathcal T u(e_h+e_{h'}). \end{align*} The expectation of the first term is at most $4B\\|(\hat{\mathcal T}-\mathcal T)u\\|_1$; the second is at most $\\|\mathcal T u\\|(\\|e_h\\|+\\|e_{h'}\\|)$. Apply \eqref{eq:rdiv_op_global}, \eqref{eq:rdiv_op_relative}, and conditional Jensen to obtain \eqref{eq:rdiv_first_stage_loss}. |

| statement | Under the assumptions of \cref{thm:iter_trae_rate}, there is a nondecreasing deterministic sequence with $b_0=0$ and $b_j\geqslant2$, $j=1,\ldots,t$, where each $b_j$ depends only on $j$ and the prescribed boundedness and mean-square-continuity constants, independently of $\beta$ and $\\|w_0\\|$. | findings | [refuted, V1.5] the step its proof takes from [bennett2023source, Lemma 11] (lem:iter-trae-excess-loss-lemma-11) does not hold as the proof uses it: (a) The citation's hypotheses are not established: Lemma 11 assumes the conditions of the source's iterative theorem, which inherits condition (c), $\mathbb E[h_0(X)-h(X)\mid Z=\cdot]\in\mathcal F$. The host assumes only $\mathcal T(h_0-h)\in\mathcal F$, allowing a proper projected test space. (b) The source's standing assumption ``Realizability and Closedness'' requires convexity of $\mathcal H$, whereas the host allows general hypothesis classes. These are defects of the citation application; the random penalty center itself is permitted by the source. Also: the step its proof takes from [bennett2023source, Lemma 10] (lem:iter-trae-excess-loss-lemma-10) does not hold as the proof uses it: (a) Lemma 10 assumes the conditions of the source's one-step theorem, including condition (c), $\mathbb E[h_0(X)-h(X)\mid Z=\cdot]\in\mathcal F$. The host's projected closedness assumption does not establish this condition. (b) The source's standing ``Realizability and Closedness'' assumption requires convexity of $\mathcal H$, which the host does not impose. Substituting the random centered penalty is supported by the source, but it does not resolve these missing hypotheses. witness: (a) Let $V,\varepsilon$ be independent Rademacher variables, $X=Z=V$, $Y=1/2+\varepsilon/4$, $\tilde{\mathcal H}=\operatorname{span}\{1,x\}$, $\tilde{\mathcal Q}=\operatorname{span}\{1\}$, and $m(W;f)=Yf(Z)$. Take $\mathcal H=\{x\mapsto a+bx:0\leqslant a\leqslant1/2,\ b\in\{-1/4,0,1/4\}\}$ and $\mathcal F=\{z\mapsto d:\|d\|\leqslant1\}$. Then $\mathcal T(a+bx)=a$, $h_0=1/2$, and every population iterate $h_{j,\lambda}^*=\{1-(\lambda/(1+\lambda))^j\}/2$ belongs to $\mathcal H$. The host's boundedness, source, projected closedness, continuity, complexity, and measurable-minimum requirements hold. However, for $h(x)=x/4$, the unprojected residual $1/2-z/4$ is not in $\mathcal F$, violating the source's condition (c). (b) In the same instance, $0,x/4\in\mathcal H$ but $x/8\notin\mathcal H$, violating the source's standing convexity hypothesis. minimal patch (confirmed): proof change: We adapt the iterative argument of \citet[Theorem~6]{bennett2023source} to the finite grid and give the recursive comparison explicitly. Fix $j$ and $\lambda$, and set \[ e_j=\\|\hat h_{j,\lambda}-h_{j,\lambda}^*\\|, \qquad u_j=\\|\mathcal T(\hat h_{j,\lambda}-h_{j,\lambda}^*)\\|, \qquad f_j^*=\mathcal T(h_0-h_{j,\lambda}^*). \] Also define \[ M_{\leqslant j} =\max_{1\leqslant\ell\leqslant j} \left\{\lambda,\\|f_\ell^*\\|_\infty^2\right\}. \] By \cref{asp:iter_realizability_trae,asp:closeness}, $f_\ell^*\in\mathcal F$. The boundedness part of \cref{asp:critical_radius} therefore gives $M_{\leqslant j}\leqslant\max\{2,B^2\}$ for every grid point under consideration.  To track the random penalty center, write \begin{align*} I_{n,j} &=\\|h_{j,\lambda}^*-\hat h_{j-1,\lambda}\\|_{2,n}^2 -\\|\hat h_{j,\lambda}-\hat h_{j-1,\lambda}\\|_{2,n}^2,\\ I_j &=\\|h_{j,\lambda}^*-h_{j-1,\lambda}^*\\|^2 -\\|\hat h_{j,\lambda}-h_{j-1,\lambda}^*\\|^2. \end{align*} We derive the excess-loss comparison directly. Write $g=h_{j,\lambda}^*$, $k=f_j^*$, $\bar f=\mathcal T(h_0-\hat h_{j,\lambda})$, and \[ \ell(h,f)=2m(W;f)-2h(X)f(Z)-f(Z)^2. \] By \cref{asp:closeness,asp:iter_realizability_trae}, $k,\bar f\in\mathcal F$. For every $h\in\mathcal H$ and $f\in\mathcal F$, the Riesz identity in Section~\ref{sec:pre} and the definition of the projected operator give \[ \mathbb E[\ell(h,f)] =2\langle\mathcal T(h_0-h),f\rangle-\\|f\\|^2 =\\|\mathcal T(h_0-h)\\|^2-\\|f-\mathcal T(h_0-h)\\|^2. \] Indeed, $f\in\widetilde{\mathcal Q}$ implies $\mathbb E[h(X)f(Z)]=\langle\mathcal T h,f\rangle$, and $\mathbb E[m(W;f)]=\langle r_0,f\rangle=\langle\mathcal T h_0,f\rangle$.  Apply the localized concentration inequality of \citet[Lemma~9]{bennett2023source} to the product and moment-image classes in \cref{asp:critical_radius}, and apply its bounded Lipschitz version to the squared test functions. With the deterministic anchors $g$ and $k$, these inequalities give, uniformly over $h\in\mathcal H$ and $f\in\mathcal F$, \[ \begin{split} &\left\|(\mathbb E_n-\mathbb E)[\ell(h,f)-\ell(g,k)]\right\|\\ &\quad\leqslant C\delta_n\left\{ \bigl(\mathbb E[m(W;f-k)^2]\bigr)^{1/2} +\\|h(X)f(Z)-g(X)k(Z)\\|_{L_2(W)} +\\|f-k\\|\right\}+C\delta_n^2\\ &\quad\leqslant C\left\{\delta_n\\|h-g\\|\\|k\\|_\infty +\delta_n\\|f-k\\|+\delta_n^2\right\}. \end{split} \] For the second inequality, \cref{asp:iter_mean_sq_continuity} bounds the first norm by $C\\|f-k\\|$, while \[ h(X)f(Z)-g(X)k(Z) =h(X)(f-k)(Z)+(h-g)(X)k(Z) \] implies that the product norm is at most $B\\|f-k\\|+\\|h-g\\|\\|k\\|_\infty$. The square map is $2B$-Lipschitz on the range of the test functions. Also, $h_0\in\mathcal H$ and \cref{asp:closeness} imply $0\in\mathcal F$. Applying the same concentration inequality with zero moment, product, and test-function anchors, and using \cref{asp:mean_sq_continuity}, gives \[ \left\|(\mathbb E_n-\mathbb E)\ell(h,f)\right\| \leqslant C\{\delta_n\\|f\\|+\delta_n^2\}, \qquad h\in\mathcal H,\ f\in\mathcal F. \] Allocate fixed fractions of $\eta_{n,t}$ to these finitely many concentration inequalities and to the comparison of Lemma~\ref{lemma:concentrate_l} used below, absorbing the resulting fixed constants into the calibrated scale.  Taking $h=g$ in the increment bound and using the population identity yields \[ \begin{split} \mathbb E_n[\ell(g,f)-\ell(g,k)] &\leqslant-\\|f-k\\|^2+C\delta_n\\|f-k\\|+C\delta_n^2\\ &\leqslant C\delta_n^2. \end{split} \] Taking the supremum over $f\in\mathcal F$ therefore proves \[ 0\leqslant L_n(g)-\mathbb E_n\ell(g,k)\leqslant C\delta_n^2, \] without requiring an empirical maximizing test function. Next, $\\|\bar f-k\\|=u_j$, so the increment bound at $(\hat h_{j,\lambda},\bar f)$ gives \[ \begin{split} L(\hat h_{j,\lambda})-L(g) &\leqslant\mathbb E_n[\ell(\hat h_{j,\lambda},\bar f)-\ell(g,k)] +C\{\delta_ne_j\\|k\\|_\infty+\delta_nu_j+\delta_n^2\}\\ &\leqslant L_n(\hat h_{j,\lambda})-L_n(g) +C\{\delta_ne_j\\|k\\|_\infty+\delta_nu_j+\delta_n^2\}. \end{split} \] Empirical optimality relative to $g\in\mathcal H$ gives $L_n(\hat h_{j,\lambda})-L_n(g)\leqslant\lambda I_{n,j}$, including when the penalty center is random. Consequently, \begin{equation*} L(\hat h_{j,\lambda})-L(h_{j,\lambda}^*) \leqslant \lambda I_{n,j} +C\left\{ \delta_ne_j\\|f_j^*\\|_\infty +\delta_nu_j+\delta_n^2 \right\}. \end{equation*} This comparison uses the localized bounds for $\operatorname{star}(\mathcal H\cdotp\mathcal F)$, $\operatorname{star}(m\circ\mathcal F)$, and $\operatorname{star}(\mathcal F)$ from \cref{asp:critical_radius}, together with \cref{asp:iter_mean_sq_continuity}. In particular, that bound controls $\mathbb E[m(W;f_{\hat h_{j,\lambda}}-f_j^*)^2]$ by $Cu_j^2$, where $f_h=\mathcal T(h_0-h)$. The population normal equation for $h_{j,\lambda}^*$ gives the exact quadratic lower bound \begin{equation}\label{eq:iter_trae_population_curvature} \lambda e_j^2+u_j^2 \leqslant L(\hat h_{j,\lambda})-L(h_{j,\lambda}^*)-\lambda I_j. \end{equation} The quadratic identity for \eqref{eq:iter_population}, together with $L(h)=\\|\mathcal T(h-h_0)\\|^2$ on $\mathcal H$, proves this bound.  Expanding the squared penalties separates the center error as $2\langle\hat h_{j,\lambda}-h_{j,\lambda}^*, \hat h_{j-1,\lambda}-h_{j-1,\lambda}^*\rangle_n$. For the fixed-center squared-loss comparisons, apply the same localized concentration inequality of \citet[Lemma~9]{bennett2023source} to the bounded clipped losses corresponding to $(h-h_{j-1,\lambda}^*)^2$ at comparator $h_{j,\lambda}^*$ and $(h-h_{k,\lambda}^*)^2$ at comparator $h_{k,\lambda}^*$, for the fixed iteration--grid pairs under consideration. The fixed-population-center squared-loss measurability condition in Assumption~\ref{asp:critical_radius} supplies measurable localized empirical and Rademacher suprema for these applications.  Here is the scalar specialization and the penalty expansion. Put $M=\max\{1,B\}$ and $\mathcal U=\mathcal H/M$. For each deterministic center $c=h_{j-1,\lambda}^*$ or $c=h_{k,\lambda}^*$, use the loss \[ \ell_c(v;X)=\{M\max\{-1,\min\{v,1\}\}-c(X)\}^2. \] On the common full-probability set, $\|c(X)\|\leqslant M$, including $c=h_{0,\lambda}^*=0$, so this loss is $4M^2$-Lipschitz in $v$ and agrees with $(h(X)-c(X))^2$ at $v=h(X)/M$. For a comparator $g\in\mathcal H$, the inclusion $\operatorname{star}(\mathcal H-g)\subseteq\mathcal S:=\operatorname{star}(\mathcal H-\mathcal H)$ supplies the anchored complexity. Indeed, if $d\leqslant\delta_n$ is a critical radius for $\mathcal S$, then $\mathfrak R_n(d;\mathcal S)\leqslant d^2$ because $0\in\mathcal S$ and $\mathcal S\subseteq\operatorname{star}(\mathcal S-\mathcal S)$. Star scaling gives $\mathfrak R_n(s;\mathcal S)\leqslant sd$ for $s\geqslant d$. Hence, for $s\geqslant\delta_n$, \[ \mathfrak R_n(s;\operatorname{star}(\mathcal U-g/M)) \leqslant M^{-1}\mathfrak R_n(Ms;\mathcal S) \leqslant s\delta_n. \] The measurable squared-loss processes in Assumption~\ref{asp:critical_radius} are exactly the loss-increment processes for the stated centers and comparators, after this normalization. Lemma~9 therefore gives, on the allocated concentration event, \[ \left\|(\mathbb E_n-\mathbb E) \bigl[\{h(X)-h_{j-1,\lambda}^*(X)\}^2 -\{h_{j,\lambda}^*(X)-h_{j-1,\lambda}^*(X)\}^2\bigr]\right\| \leqslant C\{\delta_n\\|h-h_{j,\lambda}^*\\|+\delta_n^2\}, \] and, for $k=1,\ldots,t$, \[ \left\|\\|h-h_{k,\lambda}^*\\|_{2,n}^2-\\|h-h_{k,\lambda}^*\\|^2\right\| \leqslant C\{\delta_n\\|h-h_{k,\lambda}^*\\|+\delta_n^2\}. \] Allocate fixed fractions of $\eta_{n,t}$ to these scalar comparisons. Their constants depend only on the fixed class bounds.  Write $\Delta_j=\hat h_{j,\lambda}-h_{j,\lambda}^*$ and $\Delta_0=0$. Expansion gives \[ \begin{split} I_{n,j}-I_j ={}&(\mathbb E_n-\mathbb E) \bigl[\{h_{j,\lambda}^*-h_{j-1,\lambda}^*\}^2 -\{\hat h_{j,\lambda}-h_{j-1,\lambda}^*\}^2\bigr] +2\mathbb E_n[\Delta_j(X)\Delta_{j-1}(X)]. \end{split} \] The first term is at most $C(\delta_ne_j+\delta_n^2)$. The second scalar comparison and $2\delta_ne_k\leqslant e_k^2+\delta_n^2$ give $\\|\Delta_k\\|_{2,n}\leqslant C(e_k+\delta_n)$ for $k\geqslant1$; this bound also holds for $k=0$ because $\Delta_0=0$. Empirical Cauchy--Schwarz thus bounds the last term by \[ 2\\|\Delta_j\\|_{2,n}\\|\Delta_{j-1}\\|_{2,n} \leqslant 2Ce_je_{j-1}+C\delta_n(e_j+e_{j-1})+C\delta_n^2. \] Combining these bounds yields \begin{equation}\label{eq:iter_trae_center_comparison} I_{n,j}-I_j \leqslant C\delta_n(e_j+e_{j-1})+C\delta_n^2+2Ce_je_{j-1}. \end{equation} Combining \eqref{eq:iter_trae_biasless}--\eqref{eq:iter_trae_center_comparison} and applying Young's inequality gives \[ e_j^2\leqslant C\frac{\delta_n^2M_{\leqslant j}}{\lambda^2}+Ce_{j-1}^2. \] All terms proportional to $\delta_n^2$, including those multiplied by $\lambda\leqslant2$, are covered by $\delta_n^2M_{\leqslant j}/\lambda$ before division by $\lambda$. Since $e_0=0$ and $M_{\leqslant j}$ is uniformly bounded, enlarge the fixed constant $C\geqslant2$ if necessary, set $b_0=0$, and recursively choose $b_j=C(1+b_{j-1})$. Induction gives \eqref{eq:iter_trae_strong_propagation}. Thus each $b_j$ depends only on $j$ and the fixed constants.  For the weak bound, put $v_j=\\|\mathcal T(h_{j,\lambda}^*-h_0)\\|=\\|k\\|$. The absolute concentration inequality established in this proof, evaluated at the feasible residual $\bar f$, gives \[ L(\hat h_{j,\lambda}) \leqslant L_n(\hat h_{j,\lambda}) +C\{\delta_n\\|\bar f\\|+\delta_n^2\}. \] The assumptions of Lemma~\ref{lemma:concentrate_l} are inherited from the assumptions of \cref{thm:iter_trae_rate}. Its upper comparison, applied to $g=h_{j,\lambda}^*\in\mathcal H$ with the allocated confidence level, gives \[ L_n(g)\leqslant\frac54L(g)+C\delta_n^2 =\frac54v_j^2+C\delta_n^2. \] Empirical optimality, $L(g)=v_j^2$, and $\\|\bar f\\|\leqslant v_j+u_j$ therefore imply \[ \begin{split} L(\hat h_{j,\lambda})-L(g) &\leqslant\lambda I_{n,j}+\frac14v_j^2 +C\delta_n(v_j+u_j)+C\delta_n^2\\ &\leqslant\lambda I_{n,j}+C\{v_j^2+\delta_nu_j+\delta_n^2\}, \end{split} \] where the last inequality uses $\delta_nv_j\leqslant(v_j^2+\delta_n^2)/2$. Combining this with \eqref{eq:iter_trae_population_curvature} gives \[ \lambda e_j^2+u_j^2 \leqslant C\{v_j^2+\delta_nu_j+\delta_n^2\} +\lambda(I_{n,j}-I_j). \] Substituting \eqref{eq:iter_trae_center_comparison} and absorbing the linear terms gives \[ \frac{\lambda}{2}e_j^2+\frac12u_j^2 \leqslant C\left(v_j^2+\delta_n^2+\lambda e_{j-1}^2\right). \] At the final iteration, boundedness of $\mathcal H$ and \eqref{eq:iter_trae_strong_propagation} give the two simultaneous bounds \[ \lambda e_{t-1}^2\leqslant C\lambda, \qquad \lambda e_{t-1}^2 \leqslant C\frac{b_t\delta_n^2}{\lambda}. \] Taking their minimum, applying the squared triangle inequality, and using the weak-bias bound \eqref{eq:iter_weak_bias} prove \eqref{eq:iter_trae_weak_propagation}. The argument used only $\lambda\in(0,2]$, with the upper endpoint absorbed into fixed constants. Each comparison is applied to the fixed iteration--grid pairs in the oracle-relevant segment. The definition of $\eta_{n,t}$ and a union bound give the simultaneous event. |

### 28. Iterative TRAE error bounds `lem:iter-trae-accepted-stability`

| field | value |
| --- | --- |
| bucket | `conditional` |
| source | `appendices.tex:941-966` (`lemma`, label `lemma:iter_trae_accepted_stability`) |
| statement | \label{lemma:iter_trae_accepted_stability} Let $\lambda\in\Lambda_n\cap[\lambda_{\mathrm{IT}}^\circ,2]$ satisfy $\lambda\geqslant\sqrt{s_{n,t}^{\mathrm T}}$. On $\mathcal E_{n,t}^{\mathrm{IT}}$, \begin{align} &\\|\mathcal T(\hat h_{t,\lambda}-h_0)\\|^2 \leqslant C_t \{D_n(\hat h_{t,\lambda})+A_{n,t}^{\mathrm T}(\lambda)\}, \label{eq:iter_trae_accepted_total}\\ &\\|\hat h_{t,\lambda}-h_0\\|^2 \leqslant C_t\left[ \frac{1}{\lambda}\{D_n(\hat h_{t,\lambda})+A_{n,t}^{\mathrm T}(\lambda)\} +\{D_n(\hat h_{t,\lambda})+A_{n,t}^{\mathrm T}(\lambda)\}^{\beta/(\beta+1)} \right], \label{eq:iter_trae_accepted_total_strong}\\ &\lambda\\|\hat h_{t,\lambda}-h_{t,\lambda}^*\\|^2 +\\|\mathcal T(\hat h_{t,\lambda}-h_{t,\lambda}^*)\\|^2 \leqslant C_t \{D_n(\hat h_{t,\lambda})+A_{n,t}^{\mathrm T}(\lambda)\}, \label{eq:iter_trae_accepted_variance}\\ &\\|\mathcal T(h_{t,\lambda}^*-h_0)\\|^2 \leqslant C_t \{D_n(\hat h_{t,\lambda})+A_{n,t}^{\mathrm T}(\lambda)\}, \label{eq:iter_trae_accepted_bias}\\ &\\|\hat h_{t,\lambda}-h_{t,\lambda}^*\\|^2 \leqslant \frac{C_t}{\lambda} \{D_n(\hat h_{t,\lambda})+A_{n,t}^{\mathrm T}(\lambda)\}. \label{eq:iter_trae_accepted_strong} \end{align} |

### 29. RKHS quadratic reduction `prop:rkhs-qp`

| field | value |
| --- | --- |
| bucket | `verified` |
| source | `appendices.tex:1297-1312` (`proposition`, label `prop:rkhs_qp`) |
| statement | \label{prop:rkhs_qp} Let $\mathcal H$ and $\mathcal F$ be the RKHSs generated by $k_{\mathcal H}$ and $k_{\mathcal F}$, and let $K_{\mathcal H}$, $K_{\mathcal F}$, and $P_\eta$ be defined as in \cref{sec:rkhs_computation}. Among functions with the same fitting-sample evaluations, take the representative with minimum RKHS norm. Then the minimizer of \[ L_{N,\eta}(h)+\lambda\mathbb E_N[h(X)^2] \] has the form $\widehat h_\lambda(\cdot)=\sum_{j=1}^N\widehat\alpha_{\lambda,j}k_{\mathcal H}(X_j,\cdot)$, where $\widehat\alpha_\lambda$ solves \eqref{eq:rkhs_qp}. The minimum-Euclidean-norm coefficient vector is \begin{equation} \label{eq:rkhs_closed_form} \widehat\alpha_\lambda =\left(K_{\mathcal H}P_\eta K_{\mathcal H} +\lambda K_{\mathcal H}^2\right)^\dagger K_{\mathcal H}P_\eta\boldsymbol y, \end{equation} where $A^\dagger$ denotes the Moore--Penrose pseudoinverse of $A$. |

- **`thm:normality`** (`main.tex:791-807`): [F3 reviewer] The evaluated random score in Theorem~\ref{cor:normality} needs a coherent jointly measurable version. The printed statement (main.tex:791--807) and its conditional Chebyshev argument (appendices.tex:1106--1123) do not state this well-definedness convention. IndependentEvaluationSample currently supplies jointly measurable versions of the nuisance evaluations only, not of the fitted score maps. Sectionwise measurability and the displayed population $L_2$ continuity bounds do not by themselves supply evaluation coherence. Add only the following measurability bookkeeping to the theorem and corrected paper: writing $S(w;h,q)=\tilde m(w;h)+m(w;q)-h(X(w))q(Z(w))$ and $d_n(w)=S(w;\hat h,\hat q)-S(w;h_0,q_0)$, for each $n$ the fitted score difference admits a version jointly measurable in the full nuisance-training/tuning information and a fresh observation, agreeing with $d_n$ for almost every training realization and almost every fresh observation, and agreeing almost surely at every observation in the finite evaluation block. Preserve the existing independent-evaluation and nuisance-version requirements. This is only well-definedness of the evaluated random score and its conditional expectation, not a supplied probability bound, convergence claim or conditional Chebyshev conclusion. Do not add countability, separability, compactness, a uniform empirical-process condition, or joint measurability of every score on the entire hypothesis space. Keep every rate, source, confidence and variance condition and the full asymptotic conclusion. The fixer must write this minimal convention explicitly into the corrected theorem; V1.5 must review it before Lean uses it. No substantive weakening of a main result is authorized.

  - What is wrong: ``For completeness, define the uncentered score \vsDisplay{S(W;h,q)=\tilde m(W;h)+m(W;q)-h(X)q(Z), \qquad d_n(W)=S(W;\hat h,\hat q)-S(W;h_0,q_0).\mbox{''}} Defining the fitted score difference sectionwise does not ensure a jointly measurable version coherent with both its conditional population integrals and its evaluations on the finite evaluation sample. Counterexample: Let $U$ and $V$ be independent uniform random variables on $[0,1]$, realized on the uncompleted Borel product probability space, with $U$ representing training information and $V$ a fresh observation. Let $A\subset[0,1]$ be a non-Borel set. Consider the sectionwise representatives $g(u,v)=\mathbf 1_A(u)\mathbf 1_{\{0\}}(v)$ and $\bar g(u,v)=0$, which represent the same zero population-$L_2$ function for every fixed $u$. Every fixed-training section of $g$ is measurable and has zero population-$L_2$ norm, but $g(U,V)$ is not measurable on this product space because its nonzero set is $A\times\{0\}$; choosing the coherent measurable version $\bar g$ resolves this version ambiguity.

### `thm:common-dp` (`main.tex:260-285`)

- as printed: \label{thm:common_dp} Let $A_n:\Lambda_n\to[0,\infty)$, and let $\mathcal S_n\subseteq(0,2]$ be a stability region. Fix constants $C_A,C_V,C_b>0$ and exponents $\gamma\in(0,1]$ and $q>1$, and write $B_n(\lambda)=C_b\lambda^q$. Suppose that, on an event $\mathcal E_n$ with probability at least $1-\xi$, the discrepancy bound \begin{equation}\label{eq:common_discrepancy_bound} D_n(\hat h_\lambda)\leqslant A_n(\lambda)+B_n(\lambda), \end{equation} holds simultaneously over $\lambda\in\Lambda_n\cap\mathcal S_n$, and every $\lambda\in\Lambda_n\cap\mathcal S_n$ satisfies \begin{equation}\label{eq:common_accepted_bounds} \begin{aligned} \|\mathcal T(\hat h_\lambda-h_0)\|^2 &\leqslant C_A\{D_n(\hat h_\lambda)+A_n(\lambda)\},\\ \|\hat h_\lambda-h_0\|^2 &\leqslant C_V\left[\frac{D_n(\hat h_\lambda)+A_n(\lambda)}{\lambda}+\{D_n(\hat h_\lambda)+A_n(\lambda)\}^\gamma\right], \end{aligned} \end{equation} Suppose that some grid point $\lambda^\circ$ satisfies $\Lambda_n\cap[\lambda^\circ,2]\subseteq\mathcal S_n$, that $A_n$ is nonincreasing over the grid points in $[\lambda^\circ,2]$, and that \begin{equation}\label{eq:common_oracle_crossing} B_n(\lambda^\circ)\leqslant\tfrac12A_n(\lambda^\circ). \end{equation} Then \cref{alg:discrepancy} accepts a candidate before exhausting the grid, and returns a parameter satisfying $\lambda_{\mathrm{dp}}\geqslant\lambda^\circ$ with \begin{align}\label{eq:common_rate} \|\mathcal T(\hat h_{\lambda_{\mathrm{dp}}}-h_0)\|^2 &\leqslant O\{A_n(\lambda^\circ)\},\\ \|\hat h_{\lambda_{\mathrm{dp}}}-h_0\|^2 &\leqslant O\{A_n(\lambda^\circ)/\lambda^\circ+A_n(\lambda^\circ)^\gamma\}. \end{align}
- corrected: statement unchanged
- no error: only a side condition or reading the referee made explicit (see below)
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-142`: On $\mathcal E_n$, the algorithm terminates through its acceptance branch at an index $k\leqslant k^\circ$, where $\lambda^\circ=2\rho^{k^\circ}$ and $k^\circ\leqslant K_n$; acceptance at $k=K_n$ is allowed, and both error bounds hold on this event. — pins reading of thm:common-dp: The prose following the algorithm restricts acceptance guarantees to the stated events. The proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:common_dp} establishes acceptance at the oracle point and explicitly identifies the consequence as avoiding fallback.

### `prop:exist-lambda-deepiv` (`main.tex:425-427`)

- as printed: \label{prop:exist_lambda_deepiv} Suppose \cref{asp:range,asp:source_condition,asp:closeness_deepiv,asp:re_cd,asp:cr_deepiv} hold. For every $n\geqslant N_{\mathrm R}(B)$, \cref{alg:discrepancy}, with the projected statistic \eqref{eq:rdiv_projected_loss} and noise level \eqref{eq:rdiv_common_scales}, accepts a candidate after at most $K_n+1=O(\log n)$ regularized fits, with probability at least $1-\xi$. If $h_0=0$, the initial parameter is accepted. In general, the returned parameter either is the initial parameter or is a noninitial grid point satisfying \eqref{eq:discrepancy_principle}.

### `thm:iter-rdiv-rate` (`main.tex:531-553`)

- as printed: \label{thm:iter_rdiv_rate} Under the assumptions of \cref{prop:exist_lambda_iter_rdiv,asp:iter_realizability_rdiv}, let $\lambda_{\mathrm{dp},t}^{\mathrm R}$ denote the selected parameter and set \[ \varepsilon_{n,t,\beta}^{\mathrm R} =(s_{n,t}^{\mathrm R})^{q_t/(q_t+1)}+\nu_n^2. \] For every fixed $t$ and every $n\geqslant N_{\mathrm R}(B)$, with probability at least $1-\xi$, \begin{align*} \|\mathcal T(\hat h_{t,\lambda_{\mathrm{dp},t}^{\mathrm R}}-h_0)\|^2 \leqslant O_t(\varepsilon_{n,t,\beta}^{\mathrm R}), \quad \|\hat h_{t,\lambda_{\mathrm{dp},t}^{\mathrm R}}-h_0\|^2 \leqslant O_t\!\left( (s_{n,t}^{\mathrm R})^{(q_t-1)/(q_t+1)} +(\varepsilon_{n,t,\beta}^{\mathrm R})^{\beta/(\beta+1)} \right). \end{align*} Here $O_t(\cdot)$ allows constants that depend on the prescribed iteration count and fixed population quantities. In particular, $a_t$ is independent of $n$. If $\nu_n=O(\delta_n)$ and $2t\geqslant\beta+1$, the bounds reduce to \begin{align*} \|\hat h_{t,\lambda_{\mathrm{dp},t}^{\mathrm R}}-h_0\|^2 =O_t\!\left(\delta_n^{2\beta/(\beta+2)}\right), \quad \|\mathcal T(\hat h_{t,\lambda_{\mathrm{dp},t}^{\mathrm R}}-h_0)\|^2 =O_t\!\left(\delta_n^{2(\beta+1)/(\beta+2)}\right). \end{align*}

### `thm:iter-trae-rate` (`main.tex:741-765`)

- as printed: \label{thm:iter_trae_rate} Under the assumptions of \cref{prop:exist_lambda_iter_trae,asp:iter_realizability_trae,asp:iter_mean_sq_continuity}, let $\lambda_{\mathrm{dp},t}^{\mathrm T}$ denote the selected parameter, with $s_{n,t}^{\mathrm T}$ and $q_t$ defined above. For every fixed $t$ and every $n\geqslant N_{\mathrm{IT}}(B)$, with probability at least $1-\xi$, \begin{align*} \|\mathcal T(\hat h_{t,\lambda_{\mathrm{dp},t}^{\mathrm T}}-h_0)\|^2 \leqslant O_t\!\left( (s_{n,t}^{\mathrm T})^{q_t/(q_t+1)} \right), \quad \|\hat h_{t,\lambda_{\mathrm{dp},t}^{\mathrm T}}-h_0\|^2 \leqslant O_t\!\left( (s_{n,t}^{\mathrm T})^{(q_t-1)/(q_t+1)} \right). \end{align*} Because $t$ is fixed, $s_{n,t}^{\mathrm T}=b_t\delta_n^2=O_t(\delta_n^2)$, so $b_t$ affects only the constants hidden by $O_t(\cdot)$. If $2t\geqslant\beta+1$, these bounds become \begin{align*} \|\hat h_{t,\lambda_{\mathrm{dp},t}^{\mathrm T}}-h_0\|^2 =O_t\!\left(\delta_n^{2\beta/(\beta+2)}\right), \quad \|\mathcal T(\hat h_{t,\lambda_{\mathrm{dp},t}^{\mathrm T}}-h_0)\|^2 =O_t\!\left(\delta_n^{2(\beta+1)/(\beta+2)}\right). \end{align*}
- corrected: statement unchanged
- no error: only a side condition or reading the referee made explicit (see below)
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:bk-12`: With $\mathcal U$ and $\ell_B$ as in Assumption~\ref{asp:critical_radius}, for every fixed iteration--grid pair $(j,\lambda)$ under consideration and every $r>0$, both $\sup_{u\in\mathcal U,\,\|u-u_{j,\lambda}^*\|_{L_2(W;\mathbb R^3)}\leqslant r}|(\mathbb E_n-\mathbb E)\{\ell_B(u(W))-\ell_B(u_{j,\lambda}^*(W))\}|$ and $\sup_{u\in\mathcal U,\,\|u-u_{j,\lambda}^*\|_{L_2(W;\mathbb R^3)}\leqslant r}|n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^n\epsilon_i\{\ell_B(u(W_i))-\ell_B(u_{j,\lambda}^*(W_i))\}|$ are measurable under the fitting-sample law and its product with the independent Rademacher law, respectively, where $f_j^*=\mathcal T(h_0-h_{j,\lambda}^*)$ and $u_{j,\lambda}^*(W)=(m(W;f_j^*),h_{j,\lambda}^*(X)f_j^*(Z),f_j^*(Z))$. — The localized excess-loss concentration uses this nonzero deterministic anchor; the stipulated zero-anchor and fixed-center squared-loss measurability do not supply these suprema.

### `thm:normality` (`main.tex:791-807`)

- as printed: \label{cor:normality} Suppose that the assumptions of \cref{thm:tikhonv_rate} hold for both $h_0$ and $q_0$ with (potentially different) source conditions $\beta_h,\beta_q>0$. Choose a deterministic sequence $\xi_n\downarrow0$, and run the primal and dual tuning rules at confidence levels $\xi_n/2$. Let a common deterministic sequence $\delta_n$ satisfy \eqref{eq:delta_trae} for both problems at these confidence levels. Assume that, for some $\gamma_h,\gamma_q>0$, the score maps satisfy \begin{align*} \mathbb{E}\left[\left(\tilde{m}( W;h_{1}) -\tilde{m}( W;h_{2})\right)^{2}\right] & \leqslant C\| h_{1} -h_{2} \| ^{\gamma_h },\\ \mathbb{E}\left[\left(m( W;q_{1}) -m( W;q_{2})\right)^{2}\right] & \leqslant C\| q_{1} -q_{2} \| ^{\gamma_q } \end{align*} for all $h_1,h_2\in\mathcal H$ and $q_1,q_2\in\mathcal Q$. Let $\beta_m=\max\{\beta_h,\beta_q\}$ and assume that $\delta_n$ satisfies \[ \delta _{n} =o\left( n^{-\alpha _{\mathrm{dr}}}\right) ,\qquad \alpha _{\mathrm{dr}} :=\frac{1+\min\{\beta _{m} ,1\}}{2+4\min\{\beta _{m} ,1\}} . \] Let $\hat h,\hat q$ be the adaptive adversarial Tikhonov estimators, trained and tuned independently of the evaluation sample used in $\hat\theta(\hat h,\hat q)$. Finally, assume that $0<\sigma_0^2:=\mathbb E[\psi_0(W)^2]<\infty$. Then, \[ \sqrt{n}(\hat{\theta }(\hat{h},\hat{q}) -\theta _{0}) =\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum _{i=1}^{n} \psi _{0}( W_i) +o_{p}( 1) , \] where $\psi _{0}( W) =\tilde{m}( W;h_{0}) +m( W;q_{0}) -q_{0}( Z) h_{0}( X) -\theta _{0}$. Consequently, $\sqrt n(\hat\theta(\hat h,\hat q)-\theta_0)\rightsquigarrow N(0,\sigma_0^2)$.
- corrected: Suppose that the assumptions of \cref{thm:tikhonv_rate} hold for both $h_0$ and $q_0$ with (potentially different) source conditions $\beta_h,\beta_q>0$. Choose a deterministic sequence $\xi_n\downarrow0$, and run the primal and dual tuning rules at confidence levels $\xi_n/2$. Let a common deterministic sequence $\delta_n$ satisfy \eqref{eq:delta_trae} for both problems at these confidence levels. Assume that, for some $\gamma_h,\gamma_q>0$, the score maps satisfy \begin{align*} \mathbb{E}\left[\left(\tilde{m}( W;h_{1}) -\tilde{m}( W;h_{2})\right)^{2}\right] & \leqslant C\| h_{1} -h_{2} \| ^{\gamma_h },\\ \mathbb{E}\left[\left(m( W;q_{1}) -m( W;q_{2})\right)^{2}\right] & \leqslant C\| q_{1} -q_{2} \| ^{\gamma_q } \end{align*} for all $h_1,h_2\in\mathcal H$ and $q_1,q_2\in\mathcal Q$. Let $\beta_m=\max\{\beta_h,\beta_q\}$ and assume that $\delta_n$ satisfies \[ \delta _{n} =o\left( n^{-\alpha _{\mathrm{dr}}}\right) ,\qquad \alpha _{\mathrm{dr}} :=\frac{1+\min\{\beta _{m} ,1\}}{2+4\min\{\beta _{m} ,1\}} . \] Let $\hat h,\hat q$ be the adaptive adversarial Tikhonov estimators, trained and tuned independently of the evaluation sample used in $\hat\theta(\hat h,\hat q)$. For each $n$, write $\mathcal G_n$ for the full nuisance-training and tuning sigma-field. Take jointly measurable versions of the fitted nuisance evaluations, representing the fitted functions for almost every training realization and agreeing almost surely with their evaluations at every observation in the finite evaluation sample. Write \[ S(w;h,q)=\tilde m(w;h)+m(w;q)-h(X(w))q(Z(w)),\qquad d_n(w)=S(w;\hat h,\hat q)-S(w;h_0,q_0). \] Suppose that, for each $n$, $d_n$ admits a version jointly measurable in $\mathcal G_n$ and a fresh observation, agreeing with $d_n$ for almost every training realization and almost every fresh observation, and agreeing almost surely with $d_n(W_i)$ simultaneously for $i=1,\ldots,n$ in the evaluation sample. Finally, assume that $0<\sigma_0^2:=\mathbb E[\psi_0(W)^2]<\infty$. Then, \[ \sqrt{n}(\hat{\theta }(\hat{h},\hat{q}) -\theta _{0}) =\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum _{i=1}^{n} \psi _{0}( W_i) +o_{p}( 1) , \] where $\psi _{0}( W) =\tilde{m}( W;h_{0}) +m( W;q_{0}) -q_{0}( Z) h_{0}( X) -\theta _{0}$. Consequently, $\sqrt n(\hat\theta(\hat h,\hat q)-\theta_0)\rightsquigarrow N(0,\sigma_0^2)$.

- proof change: the printed proof, revised step by step] Apply \cref{thm:tikhonv_rate} to both nuisance estimators at the prescribed confidence levels $\xi_n/2$. Their rate bounds then hold on a common event with probability at least $1-\xi_n\to1$. Because $\beta_h,\beta_q>0$ and $\delta_n\to0$, these bounds give \begin{equation}\label{eq:conv_hq} \| \hat{h} -h_{0} \| =o_{p}( 1) ,\qquad \| \hat{q} -q_{0}\| =o_{p}( 1) . \end{equation} The remaining bias condition is \begin{equation}\label{eq:square_root} \sqrt{n}\mathbb{E}[(\hat{q}( Z) -q_{0}( Z))(\hat{h}( X) -h_{0}( X))] =o_{p}( 1) . \end{equation} Suppose first that $\beta_m=\beta_h\geqslant\beta_q$. By the adjoint identity and the Cauchy--Schwarz inequality, \begin{align*} \left|\mathbb{E}[(\hat{q}( Z) -q_{0}( Z))(\hat{h}( X) -h_{0}( X))]\right| & \leqslant \|\hat h-h_0\|\,\|\mathcal T^*(\hat q-q_0)\|\\  & =O_{p}\left( \delta _{n}^{\min\{\beta _{m} ,1\} /( 1+\min\{\beta _{m} ,1\})} \cdot \delta _{n}\right)\\  & =O_{p}\left( \delta _{n}^{1+\min\{\beta _{m} ,1\} /( 1+\min\{\beta _{m} ,1\})}\right) . \end{align*} If $\beta_m=\beta_q\geqslant\beta_h$, the symmetric bound \[ \left|\mathbb{E}[(\hat{q}( Z) -q_{0}( Z))(\hat{h}( X) -h_{0}( X))]\right| \leqslant \|\hat q-q_0\|\,\|\mathcal T(\hat h-h_0)\| \] gives the same rate. By assumption, $\delta _{n} =o\left( n^{-( 1+\min\{\beta _{m} ,1\}) /( 2+4\min\{\beta _{m} ,1\})}\right)$, and hence \[ \left|\mathbb{E}[(\hat{q}( Z) -q_{0}( Z))(\hat{h}( X) -h_{0}( X))]\right| =o_{p}\left( n^{-1/2}\right), \] which proves \eqref{eq:square_root}.  Use the jointly measurable version of $d_n$ stipulated in Corollary~\ref{cor:normality}. Its agreement with the fitted score difference for almost every training realization and almost every fresh observation identifies its conditional population integrals with those of the score difference. Its almost sure agreement at every evaluation observation identifies its evaluation average with the difference of the two evaluated score averages. Conditional on the nuisance-training data, the evaluation observations are independent and identically distributed. Boundedness of the nuisance classes and the score continuity assumptions imply \[ \mathbb E[d_n(W)^2\mid\text{training}] \leqslant C\left\{ \|\hat h-h_0\|^{\gamma_h}+\|\hat q-q_0\|^{\gamma_q} +\|\hat h-h_0\|^2+\|\hat q-q_0\|^2 \right\}=o_p(1). \] Conditional Chebyshev's inequality therefore gives $\sqrt n(\mathbb E_{n,\mathrm{eval}}-\mathbb E)d_n=o_p(1)$, where $\mathbb E d_n$ integrates a fresh observation conditional on the training data. The primal and dual moment equations give \[ \mathbb E d_n =-\mathbb E[(\hat h(X)-h_0(X))(\hat q(Z)-q_0(Z))]. \] Combining this identity with \eqref{eq:square_root} yields \[ \sqrt n\{\hat\theta(\hat h,\hat q)-\theta_0\} =\frac1{\sqrt n}\sum_{i=1}^n\psi_0(W_i)+o_p(1). \] The moment equations also imply $\mathbb E\psi_0(W)=0$. The classical central limit theorem applies because $0<\mathbb E[\psi_0(W)^2]<\infty$, proving the stated normal limit.
- why: [F3 reviewer] The evaluated random score in Theorem~\ref{cor:normality} needs a coherent jointly measurable version. The printed statement (main.tex:791--807) and its conditional Chebyshev argument (appendices.tex:1106--1123) do not state this well-definedness convention. IndependentEvaluationSample currently supplies jointly measurable versions of the nuisance evaluations only, not of the fitted score maps. Sectionwise measurability and the displayed population $L_2$ continuity bounds do not by themselves supply evaluation coherence. Add only the following measurability bookkeeping to the theorem and corrected paper: writing $S(w;h,q)=\tilde m(w;h)+m(w;q)-h(X(w))q(Z(w))$ and $d_n(w)=S(w;\hat h,\hat q)-S(w;h_0,q_0)$, for each $n$ the fitted score difference admits a version jointly measurable in the full nuisance-training/tuning information and a fresh observation, agreeing with $d_n$ for almost every training realization and almost every fresh observation, and agreeing almost surely at every observation in the finite evaluation block. Preserve the existing independent-evaluation and nuisance-version requirements. This is only well-definedness of the evaluated random score and its conditional expectation, not a supplied probability bound, convergence claim or conditional Chebyshev conclusion. Do not add countability, separability, compactness, a uniform empirical-process condition, or joint measurability of every score on the entire hypothesis space. Keep every rate, source, confidence and variance condition and the full asymptotic conclusion. The fixer must write this minimal convention explicitly into the corrected theorem; V1.5 must review it before Lean uses it. No substantive weakening of a main result is authorized.

### `lem:t-err-dp` (`appendices.tex:102-124`)

- as printed: \label{lemma:t_err_dp} Under \cref{asp:re_cd,asp:cr_deepiv}, with probability at least $1-\xi$, \begin{align} \mathbb E_Z\!\left[H^2\{\hat g(\cdot\mid Z),g_0(\cdot\mid Z)\}\right] &\leqslant C\delta_{n,\mathcal G}^2, \label{eq:rdiv_mle_hellinger}\\ \|\hat r-r_0\|_{L_2(Z)}&\leqslant \delta_{n,r},\nonumber\\ \sup_{h\in\mathcal H} \|(\hat{\mathcal T}-\mathcal T)h\|_{L_2(Z)} &\leqslant C\delta_{n,\mathcal G},\label{eq:rdiv_op_global}\\ \|(\hat{\mathcal T}-\mathcal T)(h-h')\|_{L_1(Z)} &\leqslant C\delta_{n,\mathcal G}\|h-h'\|, \qquad h,h'\in\mathcal H.\label{eq:rdiv_op_relative} \end{align} Moreover, if \[ \widehat L(h):=\mathbb E[(Y-\hat{\mathcal T}h(Z))^2\mid I_{\mathrm{pre}}], \] then, uniformly over $h,h'\in\mathcal H$, \begin{equation}\label{eq:rdiv_first_stage_loss} |\{\widehat L(h)-\widehat L(h')\}-\{L(h)-L(h')\}| \leqslant C\delta_{n,\mathcal G}\|h-h'\|. \end{equation}
- corrected: statement unchanged
- no longer uses `lem:conditional-mle-theorem-24`
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-153`: For $\mathcal H$ and, for each fixed $g\in\mathcal G$, $\mathcal T_g\mathcal H$, the empirical-process suprema used in the critical-radius analysis are measurable under their respective input distributions. — pins reading of ass:cr-deepiv-measurability: Assumption \ref{asp:cr_deepiv} places the measurability sentence immediately before the critical-radius requirements for these classes; the notation section defines the relevant Rademacher complexities using expectations of suprema.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-fix-4d0cd055`: For every preliminary sample size $n\geqslant1$, the empirical log likelihood $g\mapsto\mathbb E_{n,\mathrm{pre}}[\log g(X\mid Z)]$ attains its maximum over $\mathcal G$ almost surely, and $\hat g\in\mathcal G$ is a measurable maximizer selected using only $I_{\mathrm{pre}}$. — pins reading of def:rdiv-preliminary: stated by the repair
- proof change: the printed proof, revised step by step] Allocate failure probability $\xi/2$ to the conditional-density estimator and $\xi/2$ to the pilot regression. As elsewhere, evaluate the corresponding deterministic accuracy bounds at these confidence levels and relabel them $\delta_{n,\mathcal G}$ and $\delta_{n,r}$. We establish the density bound directly using the Localized Concentration lemma of \citet{li2024regularized}. Write $P=\mathbb E_{X,Z}$ and $P_n=\mathbb E_{n,\mathrm{pre}}$, and define \[ f_g(x,z)=\sqrt{\frac{g(x\mid z)+g_0(x\mid z)}{2g_0(x\mid z)}},\qquad \mathcal U=\{f_g-1:g\in\mathcal G\}. \] Realizability gives $f_{g_0}=1$, so $0\in\mathcal U$. The density bounds give \[ 2^{-1/2}\leqslant f_g\leqslant M,\qquad M=\sqrt{\frac{C_G/c_0+1}{2}}. \] Thus $\mathcal U$ is uniformly bounded by a fixed constant. Define the globally Lipschitz loss \[ \ell(v)=-\log\bigl(\max\{1+v,2^{-1/2}\}\bigr). \] The maximum map is $1$-Lipschitz, and the derivative of $-\log t$ has absolute value at most $\sqrt2$ for $t\geqslant2^{-1/2}$. Hence $\ell$ is $\sqrt2$-Lipschitz, $\ell(0)=0$, and $\ell(f_g-1)=-\log f_g$.  We verify the localized complexity condition for this application. Put $\mathcal S=\operatorname{star}(\mathcal G_0-\mathcal G_0)$ and choose a deterministic critical radius $d\leqslant\delta_{n,\mathcal G}$ as supplied by Assumption~\ref{asp:cr_deepiv}. By the critical-radius definition, $\mathfrak R_n(d;\mathcal S)\leqslant d^2$. For $s\geqslant d$, star-shapedness implies that $(d/s)v\in\mathcal S$ and $\|(d/s)v\|_{L_2(X,Z)}\leqslant d$ whenever $v\in\mathcal S$ and $\|v\|_{L_2(X,Z)}\leqslant s$. Scaling inside the Rademacher supremum therefore gives \[ \mathfrak R_n(s;\mathcal S) \leqslant\frac{s}{d}\mathfrak R_n(d;\mathcal S) \leqslant sd. \] Since $1\in\mathcal G_0$, we have $\operatorname{star}(\mathcal U)\subseteq\mathcal S$. Taking $s=\delta_{n,\mathcal G}$ yields \[ \mathfrak R_n\bigl(\delta_{n,\mathcal G};\operatorname{star}(\mathcal U)\bigr) \leqslant\delta_{n,\mathcal G}d \leqslant\delta_{n,\mathcal G}^2. \] Apply the cited localized concentration lemma with input $(X,Z)$, one coordinate, class $\mathcal U$, fixed comparator $0$, loss $\ell$, and failure probability $\xi/2$. The measurability and logarithmic radius floor are supplied by Assumption~\ref{asp:cr_deepiv}, with the confidence evaluation specified in the proof. Consequently, for a fixed constant $A>0$, with probability at least $1-\xi/2$, \[ \left|(P_n-P)(-\log f_g)\right| \leqslant A\left\{\delta_{n,\mathcal G}\|f_g-1\|_{L_2(X,Z)}+\delta_{n,\mathcal G}^2\right\}, \qquad g\in\mathcal G. \]  To use empirical optimality, note that $P_n\log g_0(X\mid Z)$ is finite by the density bounds. Since $\hat g$ maximizes the empirical log likelihood and all candidate densities are bounded above, $\hat g(X_i\mid Z_i)>0$ at every preliminary observation, almost surely. For $a>0$, the arithmetic-geometric mean inequality gives $(a+1)/2\geqslant\sqrt a$, and hence \[ -\log\sqrt{\frac{a+1}{2}}\leqslant-\frac14\log a. \] Taking $a=\hat g(X_i\mid Z_i)/g_0(X_i\mid Z_i)$ and averaging gives \[ P_n(-\log f_{\hat g}) \leqslant-\frac14P_n\log\frac{\hat g(X\mid Z)}{g_0(X\mid Z)} \leqslant0. \] For every $g\in\mathcal G$, conditional density normalization gives \[ P f_g^2 =\mathbb E_Z\int\frac{g(x\mid Z)+g_0(x\mid Z)}2\,\mu(dx)=1. \] Since $\log t\leqslant t-1$ for $t>0$, it follows that \[ P(-\log f_g)\geqslant1-Pf_g =\frac12P(f_g-1)^2. \] On the concentration event, put $b=\|f_{\hat g}-1\|_{L_2(X,Z)}$. Combining these inequalities and using Young's inequality gives \[ \frac{b^2}{2} \leqslant P(-\log f_{\hat g}) \leqslant A\delta_{n,\mathcal G}b+A\delta_{n,\mathcal G}^2 \leqslant\frac{b^2}{4}+(A^2+A)\delta_{n,\mathcal G}^2. \] Thus $b^2\leqslant4(A^2+A)\delta_{n,\mathcal G}^2$.  Finally, for $t\geqslant0$ and $f=\sqrt{(t^2+1)/2}$, we have $f\leqslant t+1$ and \[ |t-1|=\frac{2(f+1)}{t+1}|f-1|\leqslant4|f-1|. \] Apply this pointwise with $t=\sqrt{\hat g/g_0}$ and integrate under the true joint distribution to obtain \[ \begin{aligned} \mathbb E_Z\!\left[H^2\{\hat g(\cdot\mid Z),g_0(\cdot\mid Z)\}\right] &=P\left(\sqrt{\frac{\hat g(X\mid Z)}{g_0(X\mid Z)}}-1\right)^2\\ &\leqslant16b^2 \leqslant64(A^2+A)\delta_{n,\mathcal G}^2. \end{aligned} \] This proves \eqref{eq:rdiv_mle_hellinger} with probability at least $1-\xi/2$. The nuisance-rate assumption gives $\|\hat r-r_0\|_{L_2(Z)}\leqslant\delta_{n,r}$ with probability at least $1-\xi/2$. Therefore, without requiring independence between $\hat g$ and $\hat r$, the union bound shows that both inequalities hold simultaneously with probability at least $1-\xi$. We work on this intersection for the rest of the proof. Here \[ H^2(g_1,g_2)=\int\{\sqrt{g_1(x)}-\sqrt{g_2(x)}\}^2\,\mu(dx). \]  Let $P_X$ be the true marginal distribution of $X$ and let $\hat P_X$ be the marginal induced by $Z\sim P_Z$ and $X\mid Z\sim\hat g(\cdot\mid Z)$. The density bounds in \cref{asp:re_cd} give $\hat g(x\mid z)/g_0(x\mid z)\leqslant C_G/c_0$ and hence \[ \frac{d\hat P_X}{dP_X}\leqslant \frac{C_G}{c_0}. \] The total-variation--Hellinger inequality and $\|h\|_\infty\leqslant B$ give \[ \|(\hat{\mathcal T}-\mathcal T)h\|^2 \leqslant B^2\mathbb E_Z \left[\left\{\int|\hat g(x\mid Z)-g_0(x\mid Z)|\,\mu(dx)\right\}^2\right] \leqslant C B^2\delta_{n,\mathcal G}^2, \] which proves \eqref{eq:rdiv_op_global}. For $u=h-h'$, factor $\hat g/g_0-1=(\sqrt{\hat g/g_0}-1)(\sqrt{\hat g/g_0}+1)$. Cauchy--Schwarz and the conditional density-ratio bound yield \begin{align*} \|(\hat{\mathcal T}-\mathcal T)u\|_1 &\leqslant \mathbb E\left[|u(X)| \left|\sqrt{\frac{\hat g(X\mid Z)}{g_0(X\mid Z)}}-1\right| \left\{\sqrt{\frac{\hat g(X\mid Z)}{g_0(X\mid Z)}}+1\right\}\right]\\ &\leqslant C\|u\| \mathbb E_Z\!\left[H^2\{\hat g(\cdot\mid Z),g_0(\cdot\mid Z)\}\right]^{1/2} \leqslant C\delta_{n,\mathcal G}\|u\|, \end{align*} which proves \eqref{eq:rdiv_op_relative}. \citet{li2024regularized} use the same operator argument in their MLE analysis.  Finally, write $e_h=(\hat{\mathcal T}-\mathcal T)h$. Pointwise, \begin{align*} &(Y-\hat{\mathcal T}h)^2-(Y-\hat{\mathcal T}h')^2 -\{(Y-\mathcal T h)^2-(Y-\mathcal T h')^2\}\\ &\quad=-(\hat{\mathcal T}-\mathcal T)u (2Y-\hat{\mathcal T}h-\hat{\mathcal T}h') +\mathcal T u(e_h+e_{h'}). \end{align*} The expectation of the first term is at most $4B\|(\hat{\mathcal T}-\mathcal T)u\|_1$; the second is at most $\|\mathcal T u\|(\|e_h\|+\|e_{h'}\|)$. Apply \eqref{eq:rdiv_op_global}, \eqref{eq:rdiv_op_relative}, and conditional Jensen to obtain \eqref{eq:rdiv_first_stage_loss}.
- why: the step its proof takes from [li2024regularized, Theorem 24] (lem:conditional-mle-theorem-24) does not hold as the proof uses it: (a) The cited Theorem 24 explicitly imports the source-condition hypothesis of its referenced RDIV theorem. Lemma~\ref{lemma:t_err_dp} assumes only Assumptions~\ref{asp:re_cd} and \ref{asp:cr_deepiv}; these do not imply a source condition. Thus the citation, as stated, has an unestablished hypothesis. (b) The supplied theorem states an unsigned exponential confidence formula, and its proof explicitly gives $1-\exp(n\delta_n^2)$. Neither supplies the asserted probability $1-\xi/2$. The supplied localized concentration lemma has a usable confidence statement, but obtaining the density-only bound from it requires an explicit specialization rather than the printed direct invocation of Theorem 24.
