- proof change: the printed proof, revised step by step] Write $e_\lambda=\hat h_\lambda-h_0$. The localized squared-norm inequality in \citet[Lemma~14]{foster2023orthogonal}, applied using the squared-loss measurability condition in Assumption~\ref{asp:critical_radius}, gives, uniformly over $h\in\mathcal H$, \[ \left|\{\|h\|_{2,n}^2-\|h_0\|_{2,n}^2\} -\{\|h\|^2-\|h_0\|^2\}\right| \leqslant C\{\delta_n\|h-h_0\|+\delta_n^2\}. \] \footnote{Put $M=\max\{1,B\}$, $\mathcal U=\{h/M:h\in\mathcal H\}$, and $u_0=h_0/M$. Assumption~\ref{asp:closeness_deepiv} gives $u_0\in\mathcal U$, and boundedness gives $|u(X)|\leqslant1$ uniformly over $u\in\mathcal U$ on the common full-probability set. The loss \[ \ell(v)=M^2\bigl(\max\{-1,\min\{v,1\}\}\bigr)^2 \] is $L$-Lipschitz with $L=2M^2$, since clipping is $1$-Lipschitz and $|a^2-b^2|\leqslant2|a-b|$ for $a,b\in[-1,1]$. Moreover, $\ell(h(X)/M)=h(X)^2$.  Set $\mathcal S=\operatorname{star}(\mathcal H-\mathcal H)$. By Assumption~\ref{asp:critical_radius} and \eqref{eq:delta_trae}, there is a deterministic critical radius $d\leqslant\delta_n$ for $\mathcal S$. Since $0\in\mathcal S$, we have $\mathcal S\subseteq\operatorname{star}(\mathcal S-\mathcal S)$, so the critical-radius definition implies $\mathfrak R_n(d;\mathcal S)\leqslant d^2$. For $s\geqslant d$, scaling by $d/s$ preserves membership in $\mathcal S$ and gives \[ \mathfrak R_n(s;\mathcal S) \leqslant\frac{s}{d}\mathfrak R_n(d;\mathcal S) \leqslant sd. \] Also, $h_0\in\mathcal H$ implies $\operatorname{star}(\mathcal H-h_0)\subseteq\mathcal S$. Thus, for every $s\geqslant\delta_n$, \[ \begin{split} \mathfrak R_n\bigl(s;\operatorname{star}(\mathcal U-u_0)\bigr) &=M^{-1}\mathfrak R_n\bigl(Ms;\operatorname{star}(\mathcal H-h_0)\bigr)\\ &\leqslant M^{-1}\mathfrak R_n(Ms;\mathcal S) \leqslant sd\leqslant s\delta_n. \end{split} \] In particular, every $r\geqslant\delta_n$ satisfies the anchored fixed-point inequality for the normalized class.  For $R>0$, define \[ Z_n(R)=\sup_{\substack{u\in\mathcal U\\\|u-u_0\|\leqslant R}} \left|(\mathbb E_n-\mathbb E)[\ell(u(X))-\ell(u_0(X))]\right|. \] Its empirical and Rademacher suprema are precisely the squared-loss suprema in Assumption~\ref{asp:critical_radius}, with localization radius $MR$, and hence are measurable. The localized concentration inequality used in the proof of \citet[Lemma~14]{foster2023orthogonal}, applied to this measurable loss process and the anchored fixed point, gives universal constants $c_1,c_2>0$ such that, for $R\geqslant r\geqslant\delta_n$ and $v>0$, \[ \mathbb P\{Z_n(R)\geqslant16LRr+v\} \leqslant c_1\exp\left\{-\frac{c_2nv^2}{L^2R^2+2Lv}\right\}. \] Taking $v=LRr$ and using $r\leqslant R$ gives \[ \mathbb P\{Z_n(R)\geqslant17LRr\} \leqslant c_1\exp\{-c_2nr^2/3\}. \] Choose $r=a\delta_n$ with a fixed sufficiently large $a\geqslant1$. If $r\geqslant2$, set $J=0$; otherwise set $J=\lceil\log(2/r)/\log2\rceil$. Put $R_j=2^jr$ for $j=0,\ldots,J$. A union bound shows that the measurable event \[ Z_n(R_j)<17LR_jr,\qquad j=0,\ldots,J, \] has failure probability at most $(J+1)c_1\exp\{-c_2nr^2/3\}$. Since $r\geqslant\delta_n\geqslant n^{-1/2}$, there is a universal constant $C_0$ such that $J+1\leqslant C_0\log(en)$. Equation~\eqref{eq:delta_trae} gives $n\delta_n^2\geqslant1$ and a fixed positive lower bound for \[ \frac{n\delta_n^2}{\log\log(en)+\log(1/\eta_n)}. \] Consequently, a fixed sufficiently large $a$ makes this failure probability at most $\eta_n/4$.  On this event, let $u\in\mathcal U$ and write $b=\|u-u_0\|$. If $b\leqslant r$, the loss increment is bounded by $Z_n(r)<17Lr^2$. If $b>r$, boundedness gives $b\leqslant2$, so there is an index $j\in\{1,\ldots,J\}$ with $R_{j-1}<b\leqslant R_j$. Then $R_j<2b$, and the loss increment is bounded by $Z_n(R_j)<34Lrb$. Thus, in both cases, \[ \left|(\mathbb E_n-\mathbb E)[\ell(u(X))-\ell(u_0(X))]\right| \leqslant34Lr(b+r). \] Substitution of $u=h/M$, $L=2M^2$, and $r=a\delta_n$ bounds the squared-norm difference by \[ 68M^2a\delta_n\{\|h-h_0\|/M+a\delta_n\} \leqslant C\{\delta_n\|h-h_0\|+\delta_n^2\}, \] uniformly over $h\in\mathcal H$. The constant depends only on the fixed boundedness and calibration constants. Allocating $\eta_n/4$ to this comparison at each grid point uses at most $(K_n+1)\eta_n/4=\xi/4$ of the total failure probability, supplying the squared-norm comparison included in $\mathcal E_n$.} The event $\mathcal E_n$ includes this bound and $L_n(h_0)\leqslant C\delta_n^2$. Empirical optimality relative to $h_0\in\mathcal H$ therefore gives \begin{equation}\label{eq:trae_empirical_optimality} L_n(\hat h_\lambda) +\lambda\{\|e_\lambda\|^2+2\langle h_0,e_\lambda\rangle\} \leqslant C\{\delta_n^2+\lambda\delta_n\|e_\lambda\|\}. \end{equation}  By \cref{lemma:concentrate_l}, $L(\hat h_\lambda)\leqslant2L_n(\hat h_\lambda)+C\delta_n^2$. Apply the first consequence of \cref{lemma:variational_source} to the inner-product term in \eqref{eq:trae_empirical_optimality}. Young's inequality also gives $C\lambda\delta_n\|e_\lambda\| \leqslant\lambda\|e_\lambda\|^2/8+C\delta_n^2$. Absorbing the resulting empirical-loss and squared-norm terms proves the first bound.  For the second bound, the lower comparison in \cref{lemma:concentrate_l} gives \[ L(\hat h_\lambda)\leqslant2L_n(\hat h_\lambda)+C\delta_n^2 \leqslant C \{L_n(\hat h_\lambda)+\delta_n^2\}. \] By \cref{asp:closeness_deepiv,asp:closeness}, taking $h=h_0$ gives $0=\mathcal T(h_0-h_0)\in\mathcal F$. Hence $L_n(\hat h_\lambda)\geqslant0$. Drop this term from \eqref{eq:trae_empirical_optimality}, divide by $\lambda$, and apply Young's inequality to obtain \[ \|e_\lambda\|^2 \leqslant C\frac{\delta_n^2}{\lambda} +C|\langle h_0,e_\lambda\rangle|. \] The second consequence of \cref{lemma:variational_source} and the weak bound above complete the proof.

### `lem:iter-trae-strong-propagation` (`appendices.tex:807-822`)

- as printed: Under the assumptions of \cref{thm:iter_trae_rate}, there is a nondecreasing deterministic sequence with $b_0=0$ and $b_j\geqslant2$, $j=1,\ldots,t$, where each $b_j$ depends only on $j$ and the prescribed boundedness and mean-square-continuity constants, independently of $\beta$ and $\|w_0\|$. This sequence may be calibrated so that, on $\mathcal E_{n,t}^{\mathrm{IT}}$, simultaneously for $\lambda\in\Lambda_n\cap[\lambda_{\mathrm{IT}}^\circ,2]$, \begin{align} \|\hat h_{j,\lambda}-h_{j,\lambda}^*\|^2 &\leqslant C\frac{b_j\delta_n^2}{\lambda^2}, \qquad j=1,\ldots,t, \label{eq:iter_trae_strong_propagation}\\ L(\hat h_{t,\lambda}) &\leqslant C\left[ \delta_n^2+\min\left\{\lambda, \frac{b_t\delta_n^2}{\lambda}\right\} +\|w_0\|^2\lambda^{q_t} \right]. \label{eq:iter_trae_weak_propagation} \end{align}

- proof change: the printed proof, revised step by step] We adapt the iterative argument of \citet[Theorem~6]{bennett2023source} to the finite grid and give the recursive comparison explicitly. Fix $j$ and $\lambda$, and set \[ e_j=\|\hat h_{j,\lambda}-h_{j,\lambda}^*\|, \qquad u_j=\|\mathcal T(\hat h_{j,\lambda}-h_{j,\lambda}^*)\|, \qquad f_j^*=\mathcal T(h_0-h_{j,\lambda}^*). \] Also define \[ M_{\leqslant j} =\max_{1\leqslant\ell\leqslant j} \left\{\lambda,\|f_\ell^*\|_\infty^2\right\}. \] By \cref{asp:iter_realizability_trae,asp:closeness}, $f_\ell^*\in\mathcal F$. The boundedness part of \cref{asp:critical_radius} therefore gives $M_{\leqslant j}\leqslant\max\{2,B^2\}$ for every grid point under consideration.  To track the random penalty center, write \begin{align*} I_{n,j} &=\|h_{j,\lambda}^*-\hat h_{j-1,\lambda}\|_{2,n}^2 -\|\hat h_{j,\lambda}-\hat h_{j-1,\lambda}\|_{2,n}^2,\\ I_j &=\|h_{j,\lambda}^*-h_{j-1,\lambda}^*\|^2 -\|\hat h_{j,\lambda}-h_{j-1,\lambda}^*\|^2. \end{align*} We derive the excess-loss comparison directly. Write $g=h_{j,\lambda}^*$, $k=f_j^*$, $\bar f=\mathcal T(h_0-\hat h_{j,\lambda})$, and \[ \ell(h,f)=2m(W;f)-2h(X)f(Z)-f(Z)^2. \] By \cref{asp:closeness,asp:iter_realizability_trae}, $k,\bar f\in\mathcal F$. For every $h\in\mathcal H$ and $f\in\mathcal F$, the Riesz identity in Section~\ref{sec:pre} and the definition of the projected operator give \[ \mathbb E[\ell(h,f)] =2\langle\mathcal T(h_0-h),f\rangle-\|f\|^2 =\|\mathcal T(h_0-h)\|^2-\|f-\mathcal T(h_0-h)\|^2. \] Indeed, $f\in\widetilde{\mathcal Q}$ implies $\mathbb E[h(X)f(Z)]=\langle\mathcal T h,f\rangle$, and $\mathbb E[m(W;f)]=\langle r_0,f\rangle=\langle\mathcal T h_0,f\rangle$.  Apply the localized concentration inequality of \citet[Lemma~9]{bennett2023source} to the product and moment-image classes in \cref{asp:critical_radius}, and apply its bounded Lipschitz version to the squared test functions. With the deterministic anchors $g$ and $k$, these inequalities give, uniformly over $h\in\mathcal H$ and $f\in\mathcal F$, \[ \begin{split} &\left|(\mathbb E_n-\mathbb E)[\ell(h,f)-\ell(g,k)]\right|\\ &\quad\leqslant C\delta_n\left\{ \bigl(\mathbb E[m(W;f-k)^2]\bigr)^{1/2} +\|h(X)f(Z)-g(X)k(Z)\|_{L_2(W)} +\|f-k\|\right\}+C\delta_n^2\\ &\quad\leqslant C\left\{\delta_n\|h-g\|\|k\|_\infty +\delta_n\|f-k\|+\delta_n^2\right\}. \end{split} \] For the second inequality, \cref{asp:iter_mean_sq_continuity} bounds the first norm by $C\|f-k\|$, while \[ h(X)f(Z)-g(X)k(Z) =h(X)(f-k)(Z)+(h-g)(X)k(Z) \] implies that the product norm is at most $B\|f-k\|+\|h-g\|\|k\|_\infty$. The square map is $2B$-Lipschitz on the range of the test functions. Also, $h_0\in\mathcal H$ and \cref{asp:closeness} imply $0\in\mathcal F$. Applying the same concentration inequality with zero moment, product, and test-function anchors, and using \cref{asp:mean_sq_continuity}, gives \[ \left|(\mathbb E_n-\mathbb E)\ell(h,f)\right| \leqslant C\{\delta_n\|f\|+\delta_n^2\}, \qquad h\in\mathcal H,\ f\in\mathcal F. \] Allocate fixed fractions of $\eta_{n,t}$ to these finitely many concentration inequalities and to the comparison of Lemma~\ref{lemma:concentrate_l} used below, absorbing the resulting fixed constants into the calibrated scale.  Taking $h=g$ in the increment bound and using the population identity yields \[ \begin{split} \mathbb E_n[\ell(g,f)-\ell(g,k)] &\leqslant-\|f-k\|^2+C\delta_n\|f-k\|+C\delta_n^2\\ &\leqslant C\delta_n^2. \end{split} \] Taking the supremum over $f\in\mathcal F$ therefore proves \[ 0\leqslant L_n(g)-\mathbb E_n\ell(g,k)\leqslant C\delta_n^2, \] without requiring an empirical maximizing test function. Next, $\|\bar f-k\|=u_j$, so the increment bound at $(\hat h_{j,\lambda},\bar f)$ gives \[ \begin{split} L(\hat h_{j,\lambda})-L(g) &\leqslant\mathbb E_n[\ell(\hat h_{j,\lambda},\bar f)-\ell(g,k)] +C\{\delta_ne_j\|k\|_\infty+\delta_nu_j+\delta_n^2\}\\ &\leqslant L_n(\hat h_{j,\lambda})-L_n(g) +C\{\delta_ne_j\|k\|_\infty+\delta_nu_j+\delta_n^2\}. \end{split} \] Empirical optimality relative to $g\in\mathcal H$ gives $L_n(\hat h_{j,\lambda})-L_n(g)\leqslant\lambda I_{n,j}$, including when the penalty center is random. Consequently, \begin{equation*} L(\hat h_{j,\lambda})-L(h_{j,\lambda}^*) \leqslant \lambda I_{n,j} +C\left\{ \delta_ne_j\|f_j^*\|_\infty +\delta_nu_j+\delta_n^2 \right\}. \end{equation*} This comparison uses the localized bounds for $\operatorname{star}(\mathcal H\cdotp\mathcal F)$, $\operatorname{star}(m\circ\mathcal F)$, and $\operatorname{star}(\mathcal F)$ from \cref{asp:critical_radius}, together with \cref{asp:iter_mean_sq_continuity}. In particular, that bound controls $\mathbb E[m(W;f_{\hat h_{j,\lambda}}-f_j^*)^2]$ by $Cu_j^2$, where $f_h=\mathcal T(h_0-h)$. The population normal equation for $h_{j,\lambda}^*$ gives the exact quadratic lower bound \begin{equation}\label{eq:iter_trae_population_curvature} \lambda e_j^2+u_j^2 \leqslant L(\hat h_{j,\lambda})-L(h_{j,\lambda}^*)-\lambda I_j. \end{equation} The quadratic identity for \eqref{eq:iter_population}, together with $L(h)=\|\mathcal T(h-h_0)\|^2$ on $\mathcal H$, proves this bound.  Expanding the squared penalties separates the center error as $2\langle\hat h_{j,\lambda}-h_{j,\lambda}^*, \hat h_{j-1,\lambda}-h_{j-1,\lambda}^*\rangle_n$. For the fixed-center squared-loss comparisons, apply the same localized concentration inequality of \citet[Lemma~9]{bennett2023source} to the bounded clipped losses corresponding to $(h-h_{j-1,\lambda}^*)^2$ at comparator $h_{j,\lambda}^*$ and $(h-h_{k,\lambda}^*)^2$ at comparator $h_{k,\lambda}^*$, for the fixed iteration--grid pairs under consideration. The fixed-population-center squared-loss measurability condition in Assumption~\ref{asp:critical_radius} supplies measurable localized empirical and Rademacher suprema for these applications.  Here is the scalar specialization and the penalty expansion. Put $M=\max\{1,B\}$ and $\mathcal U=\mathcal H/M$. For each deterministic center $c=h_{j-1,\lambda}^*$ or $c=h_{k,\lambda}^*$, use the loss \[ \ell_c(v;X)=\{M\max\{-1,\min\{v,1\}\}-c(X)\}^2. \] On the common full-probability set, $|c(X)|\leqslant M$, including $c=h_{0,\lambda}^*=0$, so this loss is $4M^2$-Lipschitz in $v$ and agrees with $(h(X)-c(X))^2$ at $v=h(X)/M$. For a comparator $g\in\mathcal H$, the inclusion $\operatorname{star}(\mathcal H-g)\subseteq\mathcal S:=\operatorname{star}(\mathcal H-\mathcal H)$ supplies the anchored complexity. Indeed, if $d\leqslant\delta_n$ is a critical radius for $\mathcal S$, then $\mathfrak R_n(d;\mathcal S)\leqslant d^2$ because $0\in\mathcal S$ and $\mathcal S\subseteq\operatorname{star}(\mathcal S-\mathcal S)$. Star scaling gives $\mathfrak R_n(s;\mathcal S)\leqslant sd$ for $s\geqslant d$. Hence, for $s\geqslant\delta_n$, \[ \mathfrak R_n(s;\operatorname{star}(\mathcal U-g/M)) \leqslant M^{-1}\mathfrak R_n(Ms;\mathcal S) \leqslant s\delta_n. \] The measurable squared-loss processes in Assumption~\ref{asp:critical_radius} are exactly the loss-increment processes for the stated centers and comparators, after this normalization. Lemma~9 therefore gives, on the allocated concentration event, \[ \left|(\mathbb E_n-\mathbb E) \bigl[\{h(X)-h_{j-1,\lambda}^*(X)\}^2 -\{h_{j,\lambda}^*(X)-h_{j-1,\lambda}^*(X)\}^2\bigr]\right| \leqslant C\{\delta_n\|h-h_{j,\lambda}^*\|+\delta_n^2\}, \] and, for $k=1,\ldots,t$, \[ \left|\|h-h_{k,\lambda}^*\|_{2,n}^2-\|h-h_{k,\lambda}^*\|^2\right| \leqslant C\{\delta_n\|h-h_{k,\lambda}^*\|+\delta_n^2\}. \] Allocate fixed fractions of $\eta_{n,t}$ to these scalar comparisons. Their constants depend only on the fixed class bounds.  Write $\Delta_j=\hat h_{j,\lambda}-h_{j,\lambda}^*$ and $\Delta_0=0$. Expansion gives \[ \begin{split} I_{n,j}-I_j ={}&(\mathbb E_n-\mathbb E) \bigl[\{h_{j,\lambda}^*-h_{j-1,\lambda}^*\}^2 -\{\hat h_{j,\lambda}-h_{j-1,\lambda}^*\}^2\bigr] +2\mathbb E_n[\Delta_j(X)\Delta_{j-1}(X)]. \end{split} \] The first term is at most $C(\delta_ne_j+\delta_n^2)$. The second scalar comparison and $2\delta_ne_k\leqslant e_k^2+\delta_n^2$ give $\|\Delta_k\|_{2,n}\leqslant C(e_k+\delta_n)$ for $k\geqslant1$; this bound also holds for $k=0$ because $\Delta_0=0$. Empirical Cauchy--Schwarz thus bounds the last term by \[ 2\|\Delta_j\|_{2,n}\|\Delta_{j-1}\|_{2,n} \leqslant 2Ce_je_{j-1}+C\delta_n(e_j+e_{j-1})+C\delta_n^2. \] Combining these bounds yields \begin{equation}\label{eq:iter_trae_center_comparison} I_{n,j}-I_j \leqslant C\delta_n(e_j+e_{j-1})+C\delta_n^2+2Ce_je_{j-1}. \end{equation} Combining \eqref{eq:iter_trae_biasless}--\eqref{eq:iter_trae_center_comparison} and applying Young's inequality gives \[ e_j^2\leqslant C\frac{\delta_n^2M_{\leqslant j}}{\lambda^2}+Ce_{j-1}^2. \] All terms proportional to $\delta_n^2$, including those multiplied by $\lambda\leqslant2$, are covered by $\delta_n^2M_{\leqslant j}/\lambda$ before division by $\lambda$. Since $e_0=0$ and $M_{\leqslant j}$ is uniformly bounded, enlarge the fixed constant $C\geqslant2$ if necessary, set $b_0=0$, and recursively choose $b_j=C(1+b_{j-1})$. Induction gives \eqref{eq:iter_trae_strong_propagation}. Thus each $b_j$ depends only on $j$ and the fixed constants.  For the weak bound, put $v_j=\|\mathcal T(h_{j,\lambda}^*-h_0)\|=\|k\|$. The absolute concentration inequality established in this proof, evaluated at the feasible residual $\bar f$, gives \[ L(\hat h_{j,\lambda}) \leqslant L_n(\hat h_{j,\lambda}) +C\{\delta_n\|\bar f\|+\delta_n^2\}. \] The assumptions of Lemma~\ref{lemma:concentrate_l} are inherited from the assumptions of \cref{thm:iter_trae_rate}. Its upper comparison, applied to $g=h_{j,\lambda}^*\in\mathcal H$ with the allocated confidence level, gives \[ L_n(g)\leqslant\frac54L(g)+C\delta_n^2 =\frac54v_j^2+C\delta_n^2. \] Empirical optimality, $L(g)=v_j^2$, and $\|\bar f\|\leqslant v_j+u_j$ therefore imply \[ \begin{split} L(\hat h_{j,\lambda})-L(g) &\leqslant\lambda I_{n,j}+\frac14v_j^2 +C\delta_n(v_j+u_j)+C\delta_n^2\\ &\leqslant\lambda I_{n,j}+C\{v_j^2+\delta_nu_j+\delta_n^2\}, \end{split} \] where the last inequality uses $\delta_nv_j\leqslant(v_j^2+\delta_n^2)/2$. Combining this with \eqref{eq:iter_trae_population_curvature} gives \[ \lambda e_j^2+u_j^2 \leqslant C\{v_j^2+\delta_nu_j+\delta_n^2\} +\lambda(I_{n,j}-I_j). \] Substituting \eqref{eq:iter_trae_center_comparison} and absorbing the linear terms gives \[ \frac{\lambda}{2}e_j^2+\frac12u_j^2 \leqslant C\left(v_j^2+\delta_n^2+\lambda e_{j-1}^2\right). \] At the final iteration, boundedness of $\mathcal H$ and \eqref{eq:iter_trae_strong_propagation} give the two simultaneous bounds \[ \lambda e_{t-1}^2\leqslant C\lambda, \qquad \lambda e_{t-1}^2 \leqslant C\frac{b_t\delta_n^2}{\lambda}. \] Taking their minimum, applying the squared triangle inequality, and using the weak-bias bound \eqref{eq:iter_weak_bias} prove \eqref{eq:iter_trae_weak_propagation}. The argument used only $\lambda\in(0,2]$, with the upper endpoint absorbed into fixed constants. Each comparison is applied to the fixed iteration--grid pairs in the oracle-relevant segment. The definition of $\eta_{n,t}$ and a union bound give the simultaneous event.
- why: the step its proof takes from [bennett2023source, Lemma 11] (lem:iter-trae-excess-loss-lemma-11) does not hold as the proof uses it: (a) The citation's hypotheses are not established: Lemma 11 assumes the conditions of the source's iterative theorem, which inherits condition (c), $\mathbb E[h_0(X)-h(X)\mid Z=\cdot]\in\mathcal F$. The host assumes only $\mathcal T(h_0-h)\in\mathcal F$, allowing a proper projected test space. (b) The source's standing assumption ``Realizability and Closedness'' requires convexity of $\mathcal H$, whereas the host allows general hypothesis classes. These are defects of the citation application; the random penalty center itself is permitted by the source. Also: the step its proof takes from [bennett2023source, Lemma 10] (lem:iter-trae-excess-loss-lemma-10) does not hold as the proof uses it: (a) Lemma 10 assumes the conditions of the source's one-step theorem, including condition (c), $\mathbb E[h_0(X)-h(X)\mid Z=\cdot]\in\mathcal F$. The host's projected closedness assumption does not establish this condition. (b) The source's standing ``Realizability and Closedness'' assumption requires convexity of $\mathcal H$, which the host does not impose. Substituting the random centered penalty is supported by the source, but it does not resolve these missing hypotheses.

### `thm:iter-trae-discrepancy-bound` (`appendices.tex:924-926`)

- as printed: \label{cor:iter_trae_discrepancy_bound} Under the assumptions of \cref{thm:iter_trae_rate}, the calibrated iterative TRAE scales satisfy \eqref{eq:iter_trae_discrepancy_bound} simultaneously for all $\lambda\in\Lambda_n\cap[\lambda_{\mathrm{IT}}^\circ,2]$ on $\mathcal E_{n,t}^{\mathrm{IT}}$.

- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:bk-12`: With $\mathcal U$ and $\ell_B$ as in Assumption~\ref{asp:critical_radius}, for every fixed iteration--grid pair $(j,\lambda)$ under consideration and every $r>0$, both $\sup_{u\in\mathcal U,\,\|u-u_{j,\lambda}^*\|_{L_2(W;\mathbb R^3)}\leqslant r}|(\mathbb E_n-\mathbb E)\{\ell_B(u(W))-\ell_B(u_{j,\lambda}^*(W))\}|$ and $\sup_{u\in\mathcal U,\,\|u-u_{j,\lambda}^*\|_{L_2(W;\mathbb R^3)}\leqslant r}|n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^n\epsilon_i\{\ell_B(u(W_i))-\ell_B(u_{j,\lambda}^*(W_i))\}|$ are measurable under the fitting-sample law and its product with the independent Rademacher law, respectively, where $f_j^*=\mathcal T(h_0-h_{j,\lambda}^*)$ and $u_{j,\lambda}^*(W)=(m(W;f_j^*),h_{j,\lambda}^*(X)f_j^*(Z),f_j^*(Z))$. — The localized excess-loss concentration uses this nonzero deterministic anchor; the stipulated zero-anchor and fixed-center squared-loss measurability do not supply these suprema.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-156`: The empirical loss $L_n$ is measurable, and the asserted attainment and measurable selection concern the minimum of $h\mapsto L_n(h)+\lambda\mathbb E_n[h(X)^2]$ over $h\in\mathcal H$. — pins reading of ass:trae-minima: The sentence immediately follows the displayed definition of the TRAE estimator as the minimizer of this penalized objective.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-188`: Under the assumptions of Theorem~\ref{thm:iter_trae_rate}, the displayed inequality holds simultaneously for all $\lambda\in\Lambda_n\cap[\lambda_{\mathrm{IT}}^\circ,2]$ on $\mathcal E_{n,t}^{\mathrm{IT}}$, where $\mathbb P(\mathcal E_{n,t}^{\mathrm{IT}})\geqslant1-\xi$. — pins reading of def:iter-trae-discrepancy-bound: The sentence immediately preceding the display invokes Corollary~\ref{cor:iter_trae_discrepancy_bound}, whose statement explicitly places this simultaneous bound on the common concentration event.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-206`: There exists a fixed $C<\infty$, independent of $f$, $n$, and $\lambda$, such that $\mathbb E[m(W;f)^2]\leqslant C\|f\|^2$ for every $f\in\mathcal F$. — pins reading of ass:mean-sq-continuity: Assumption~\ref{asp:mean_sq_continuity} names mean-square continuity; the calibration convention in discovery/flat.tex, line 292, expressly treats its constant as fixed and independent of $n$ and $\lambda$.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-fix-958aeb7a`: The lower bound denoted by $\Omega$ means that there exists a constant $c>0$, independent of $n$ and $\xi$, such that for every integer $n\geqslant1$ and every $\xi\in(0,1)$, $\tilde\delta_n(\xi)\geqslant c\sqrt{\{\log\log(en)+\log(1/\xi)\}/n}$. — pins reading of ass:critical-radius-complexity: stated by the repair

### `lem:iter-trae-accepted-stability` (`appendices.tex:941-966`)

- as printed: \label{lemma:iter_trae_accepted_stability} Let $\lambda\in\Lambda_n\cap[\lambda_{\mathrm{IT}}^\circ,2]$ satisfy $\lambda\geqslant\sqrt{s_{n,t}^{\mathrm T}}$. On $\mathcal E_{n,t}^{\mathrm{IT}}$, \begin{align} &\|\mathcal T(\hat h_{t,\lambda}-h_0)\|^2 \leqslant C_t \{D_n(\hat h_{t,\lambda})+A_{n,t}^{\mathrm T}(\lambda)\}, \label{eq:iter_trae_accepted_total}\\ &\|\hat h_{t,\lambda}-h_0\|^2 \leqslant C_t\left[ \frac{1}{\lambda}\{D_n(\hat h_{t,\lambda})+A_{n,t}^{\mathrm T}(\lambda)\} +\{D_n(\hat h_{t,\lambda})+A_{n,t}^{\mathrm T}(\lambda)\}^{\beta/(\beta+1)} \right], \label{eq:iter_trae_accepted_total_strong}\\ &\lambda\|\hat h_{t,\lambda}-h_{t,\lambda}^*\|^2 +\|\mathcal T(\hat h_{t,\lambda}-h_{t,\lambda}^*)\|^2 \leqslant C_t \{D_n(\hat h_{t,\lambda})+A_{n,t}^{\mathrm T}(\lambda)\}, \label{eq:iter_trae_accepted_variance}\\ &\|\mathcal T(h_{t,\lambda}^*-h_0)\|^2 \leqslant C_t \{D_n(\hat h_{t,\lambda})+A_{n,t}^{\mathrm T}(\lambda)\}, \label{eq:iter_trae_accepted_bias}\\ &\|\hat h_{t,\lambda}-h_{t,\lambda}^*\|^2 \leqslant \frac{C_t}{\lambda} \{D_n(\hat h_{t,\lambda})+A_{n,t}^{\mathrm T}(\lambda)\}. \label{eq:iter_trae_accepted_strong} \end{align}

## Suggestions to the authors

Not errors: readings the paper determines but does not state, and side conditions the proofs use silently.

- clarification (`ass:pin-142`, used by `thm:common-dp`): On $\mathcal E_n$, the algorithm terminates through its acceptance branch at an index $k\leqslant k^\circ$, where $\lambda^\circ=2\rho^{k^\circ}$ and $k^\circ\leqslant K_n$; acceptance at $k=K_n$ is allowed, and both error bounds hold on this event. — pins reading of thm:common-dp: The prose following the algorithm restricts acceptance guarantees to the stated events. The proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:common_dp} establishes acceptance at the oracle point and explicitly identifies the consequence as avoiding fallback.
- clarification (`ass:pin-153`, used by `prop:exist-lambda-deepiv`): For $\mathcal H$ and, for each fixed $g\in\mathcal G$, $\mathcal T_g\mathcal H$, the empirical-process suprema used in the critical-radius analysis are measurable under their respective input distributions. — pins reading of ass:cr-deepiv-measurability: Assumption \ref{asp:cr_deepiv} places the measurability sentence immediately before the critical-radius requirements for these classes; the notation section defines the relevant Rademacher complexities using expectations of suprema.
- clarification (`ass:pin-155`, used by `prop:exist-lambda-deepiv`): For every $\lambda\in\Lambda_n$, the empirical RDIV minimum is attained and $\hat h_\lambda$ is a measurable selected minimizer of $L_n(h)+\lambda\mathbb E_n[h(X)^2]$ over $h\in\mathcal H$. — pins reading of ass:rdiv-minima: The assumption immediately follows the RDIV estimator's defining minimization in the Adaptive Regularized DeepIV Estimator subsection, and the binding definition of $\hat h_\lambda$ specifies a measurable selected minimizer.
- clarification (`ass:pin-172`, used by `prop:exist-lambda-deepiv`): For each fixed $n\geqslant N_{\mathrm R}(B)$, all probabilistic conclusions, including initial acceptance when $h_0=0$, hold on the same event $\mathcal E_n^{\mathrm R}$ of probability at least $1-\xi$. — pins reading of prop:exist-lambda-deepiv: The proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:exist_lambda_deepiv} explicitly works throughout on $\mathcal E_n^{\mathrm R}$ and derives initial acceptance there.
- clarification (`ass:pin-174`, used by `prop:exist-lambda-deepiv`): The assumptions imported from Theorem~\ref{thm:deepiv_rate} are the general hypotheses of Proposition~\ref{prop:exist_lambda_deepiv}, without imposing $h_0=0$. — pins reading of thm:rdiv-discrepancy-bound: Theorem~\ref{thm:deepiv_rate} imports the proposition's assumptions and treats $h_0=0$ only in a separate conditional conclusion. The present proof uses Lemmas~\ref{lemma:err_l_dp} and~\ref{lemma:str_dp} for general $h_0$.
- clarification (`ass:pin-fix-4d0cd055`, used by `prop:exist-lambda-deepiv`): For every preliminary sample size $n\geqslant1$, the empirical log likelihood $g\mapsto\mathbb E_{n,\mathrm{pre}}[\log g(X\mid Z)]$ attains its maximum over $\mathcal G$ almost surely, and $\hat g\in\mathcal G$ is a measurable maximizer selected using only $I_{\mathrm{pre}}$. — pins reading of def:rdiv-preliminary: stated by the repair
- clarification (`ass:pin-190`, used by `thm:deepiv-rate`): For each fixed $n\geqslant N_{\mathrm R}(B)$, the displayed error bounds and, when $h_0=0$, both sharper $O(\delta_n^2)$ bounds hold jointly with probability at least $1-\xi$ over the preliminary, fitting, and calibration samples. — pins reading of thm:deepiv-rate: The proof obtains the zero-signal bounds from the probabilistic initial-acceptance conclusion of Proposition~\ref{prop:exist_lambda_deepiv} and Lemma~\ref{lemma:str_dp}; the general case uses the common RDIV event and the stated confidence convention.
- clarification (`ass:pin-196`, used by `thm:deepiv-rate`): The assumptions of \cref{thm:deepiv_rate} mean its standing hypotheses inherited from \cref{prop:exist_lambda_deepiv}; the bounds here hold for arbitrary $h_0$, without imposing $h_0=0$. — pins reading of lem:rdiv-accepted-bounds: The zero condition introduces only the last conclusion of \cref{thm:deepiv_rate}; the proof of \cref{lemma:rdiv_accepted_bounds} treats arbitrary $h_0$ using the variational source inequality.
- clarification (`ass:pin-187`, used by `prop:exist-lambda-iter-rdiv`): The displayed discrepancy inequality holds on $\mathcal E_{n,t}^{\mathrm{IR}}$, with $\mathbb P(\mathcal E_{n,t}^{\mathrm{IR}})\geqslant1-\xi$, simultaneously for every $\lambda\in\Lambda_n\cap[\lambda_{\mathrm{IR}}^\circ,2]$. — pins reading of def:iter-rdiv-discrepancy-bound: The sentence introducing the display cites Corollary~\ref{cor:iter_rdiv_discrepancy_bound}, which expressly states simultaneity on this event; Lemma~\ref{lemma:iter_rdiv_stability} specifies its probability.

- reading of the symbol `r_0`: $r_0\in\tilde{\mathcal Q}$ is the Riesz representer of the continuous linear functional $q\mapsto\mathbb E[m(W;q)]$, satisfying $\mathbb E[m(W;q)]=\mathbb E[r_0(Z)q(Z)]$ for every $q\in\tilde{\mathcal Q}$. In Proposition~\ref{prop:exist_lambda_deepiv}, Theorem~\ref{thm:deepiv_rate}, Proposition~\ref{prop:exist_lambda_iter_rdiv}, Theorem~\ref{thm:iter_rdiv_rate}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:t_err_dp}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:err_l_dp}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:err_r_dp}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:str_dp}, Corollary~\ref{cor:rdiv_discrepancy_bound}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:rdiv_accepted_bounds}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:iter_rdiv_stability}, Corollary~\ref{cor:iter_rdiv_discrepancy_bound}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:iter_rdiv_accepted_bounds}, \cite[Lemma 14]{foster2023orthogonal}, used in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lemma:err_l_dp}: $\tilde{\mathcal Q}=L_2(Z)$ and $r_0(z)=\mathbb E[Y\mid Z=z]$. — Section~\ref{sec:rdiv} specializes the Riesz representer to the conditional outcome mean. The enclosing iterative RDIV results use the same specialization.
- reading of the symbol `q`: The letter $q$ is locally reused for function-valued arguments in $\tilde{\mathcal Q}$ and for a deterministic scalar bias exponent in $(1,\infty)$; these uses denote distinct objects. In Theorem~\ref{thm:common_dp}: In $B_n(\lambda)=C_b\lambda^q$, $q>1$ is a fixed scalar exponent. In Theorem~\ref{thm:deepiv_rate}, Theorem~\ref{thm:tikhonv_rate}: In the application of the generic discrepancy guarantee, the scalar exponent is $q=p=\min\{\beta+1,2\}$. In Theorem~\ref{thm:iter_rdiv_rate}, Theorem~\ref{thm:iter_trae_rate}: In the proof's scalar abbreviation and application of the generic discrepancy guarantee, $q=q_t=\min\{\beta+1,2t\}$. In Corollary~\ref{cor:normality}: In the dual minimization, $q\in\mathcal Q$ is the candidate function of $Z$; in $\hat\theta(h,q)$ it is the dual nuisance argument. — The Riesz identity in Section~\ref{sec:pre} quantifies over $q\in\tilde{\mathcal Q}$. Theorem~\ref{thm:common_dp} introduces the scalar exponent. The proofs of Theorems~\ref{thm:deepiv_rate} and~\ref{thm:tikhonv_rate} set $q=p$, while the proofs of Theorems~\ref{thm:iter_rdiv_rate} and~\ref{thm:iter_trae_rate} set $q=q_t$. The dual minimization preceding Corollary~\ref{cor:normality} ranges over $q\in\mathcal Q$.
- reading of the symbol `Y`: $Y$ is the scalar outcome, with $r_0(z)=\mathbb E[Y\mid Z=z]$ in the IV setting. In Proposition~\ref{prop:exist_lambda_deepiv}, Theorem~\ref{thm:deepiv_rate}, Proposition~\ref{prop:exist_lambda_iter_rdiv}, Theorem~\ref{thm:iter_rdiv_rate}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:t_err_dp}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:err_l_dp}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:err_r_dp}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:str_dp}, Corollary~\ref{cor:rdiv_discrepancy_bound}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:rdiv_accepted_bounds}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:iter_rdiv_stability}, Corollary~\ref{cor:iter_rdiv_discrepancy_bound}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:iter_rdiv_accepted_bounds}, \cite[Lemma 14]{foster2023orthogonal}, used in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lemma:err_l_dp}: $\|Y\|\leqslant B$ almost surely for a fixed $B<\infty$. — Assumption~\ref{asp:cr_deepiv} bounds the outcome, and the iterative RDIV results retain that assumption.

- reading of the symbol `\lambda^\circ`: $\lambda^\circ\in\Lambda_n$ is any oracle comparison point satisfying $\Lambda_n\cap[\lambda^\circ,2]\subseteq\mathcal S_n$, nonincreasing $A_n$ on that grid segment, and $B_n(\lambda^\circ)\leqslant\tfrac12A_n(\lambda^\circ)$; no uniqueness or computable selection rule is required. — Theorem~\ref{thm:common_dp} assumes that some grid point satisfies exactly these three conditions.
- reading of the symbol `\lambda_{\mathrm{dp}}`: $\lambda_{\mathrm{dp}}=2\rho^{k_*}$, where $k_*=\min\{k\in\{0,\ldots,K_n\}:D_n(\hat h_{2\rho^k})\leqslant\tfrac32A_n(2\rho^k)\}$ if this set is nonempty, and $k_*=K_n$ otherwise, using the candidates, discrepancy, and noise level supplied to the rule. In Proposition~\ref{prop:exist_lambda_deepiv}, Theorem~\ref{thm:deepiv_rate}: The rule uses the one-step RDIV candidates $\hat h_\lambda$, the projected calibration discrepancy, and $A_n^{\mathrm R}(\lambda)=\delta_n^2/\lambda+\nu_n^2$. - reading of the symbol `c_0`: $c_0>0$ is a fixed finite constant, independent of sample size, such that $g_0(x\mid z)\geqslant c_0$ on the common conditional-density support. — Assumption~\ref{asp:re_cd} gives the fixed positive lower bound; Assumption~\ref{asp:cr_deepiv} identifies the common support, and the proof of Lemma~\ref{lemma:t_err_dp} uses the bound in density ratios.

- reading of the symbol `\mathcal T_g`: For every $g\in\mathcal G$, $\mathcal T_g$ maps $h\in\mathcal H$ to the $P_Z$-square-integrable function $(\mathcal T_g h)(z)=\int h(x)g(x\mid z)\,\mu(dx)$, with $z$ understood $P_Z$-almost everywhere. — Assumption~\ref{asp:cr_deepiv} introduces the operator for $g\in\mathcal G$ and defines its image class under the respective input distribution; the notation paragraph specifies the population norm for functions of $Z$.

- reading of the symbol `\lambda_{\mathrm{IR}}^\circ`: Whenever the defining set is nonempty, $\lambda_{\mathrm{IR}}^\circ$ is the deterministic oracle point $2\rho^{k^\circ}$, where $k^\circ=\min\{k\in\{0,\ldots,K_n\}:2\rho^k\leqslant c_{\mathrm{IR}}\max\{(s_{n,t}^{\mathrm R})^{1/(q_t+1)},\nu_n^{2/q_t}\}\}$ and $c_{\mathrm{IR}}>0$ is a sufficiently small fixed constant; this point exists for all sufficiently large $n$. — The definition preceding \eqref{eq:iter_rdiv_oracle_scales} specifies the threshold, and Assumption~\ref{asp:iter_realizability_rdiv} explicitly says 'For every $n$ for which $\lambda_{\mathrm{IR}}^\circ$ is defined'; Algorithm~\ref{alg:discrepancy} specifies the decreasing grid order.
- reading of the symbol `b_t`: $b_t\geqslant2$ is a calibrated deterministic propagation constant depending only on the prescribed iteration count $t$ and the fixed boundedness and mean-square-continuity constants, independently of $n$, $\beta$, and $\\|w_0\\|$. In Lemma~\ref{eq:iter_trae_strong_propagation}: $b_t$ is the terminal member of a nondecreasing sequence with $b_0=0$ and $b_j\geqslant2$ for $j=1,\ldots,t$; in the proof one may choose $b_j=C(1+b_{j-1})$ for a sufficiently large fixed $C\geqslant2$. — The paragraph preceding equation~\eqref{eq:iter_trae_scale} specifies its dependencies. Lemma~\ref{eq:iter_trae_strong_propagation} supplies the calibrated sequence, and its proof constructs that sequence recursively.
- reading of the symbol `\lambda_{\mathrm{IT}}^\circ`: For all sufficiently large $n$, $\lambda_{\mathrm{IT}}^\circ$ is the deterministic oracle point $2\rho^{k^\circ}$, where $k^\circ=\min\{k\in\{0,\ldots,K_n\}:2\rho^k\leqslant c_{\mathrm{IT}}(s_{n,t}^{\mathrm T})^{1/(q_t+1)}\}$ and $c_{\mathrm{IT}}>0$ is a sufficiently small fixed constant. — The definition preceding \eqref{eq:iter_trae_oracle_scales} explicitly restricts the construction to all sufficiently large $n$; Algorithm~\ref{alg:discrepancy} orders the grid from $k=0$ to $K_n$.
- reading of the symbol `h_{j,\lambda}^*`: $h_{j,\lambda}^*$ is the population minimizer over $\tilde{\mathcal H}$ for positive finite $\lambda$, initialized by $h_{0,\lambda}^*=0$ and recursively defined for positive integer $j$; the iterative procedures use $j=1,\ldots,t$. In Theorem~\ref{thm:iter_trae_rate}, Lemma~\ref{eq:iter_trae_strong_propagation}, Corollary~\ref{cor:iter_trae_discrepancy_bound}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:iter_trae_accepted_stability}: $h_{j,\lambda}^*\in\mathcal H$ for $j=1,\ldots,t$ and $\lambda\in\Lambda_n\cap[\lambda_{\mathrm{IT}}^\circ,2]$, for every $n$ for which the oracle point is defined. In Theorem~\ref{thm:iter_rdiv_rate}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:iter_rdiv_stability}, Corollary~\ref{cor:iter_rdiv_discrepancy_bound}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:iter_rdiv_accepted_bounds}: $h_{j,\lambda}^*\in\mathcal H$ for $j=1,\ldots,t$ and $\lambda\in\Lambda_n\cap[\lambda_{\mathrm{IR}}^\circ,2]$, for every $n$ for which the oracle point is defined. — Assumptions~\ref{asp:iter_realizability_rdiv} and~\ref{asp:iter_realizability_trae} specify membership of the population iterates on the respective oracle grid segments. The population definition and the remaining stability arguments retain the same comparators and penalty centers.
- reading of the symbol `\hat h_{j,\lambda}`: $\hat h_{j,\lambda}$ denotes a selected measurable empirical minimizer in the recursion initialized by $\hat h_{0,\lambda}=0$, with $j=1,\ldots,t$ and the empirical penalty norm evaluated on the fitting sample. In Proposition~\ref{prop:exist_lambda_iter_rdiv}, Theorem~\ref{thm:iter_rdiv_rate}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:iter_rdiv_stability}, Corollary~\ref{cor:iter_rdiv_discrepancy_bound}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:iter_rdiv_accepted_bounds}: The loss in the recursion is $L_n(h)=\mathbb E_{n,\mathrm{fit}}[(Y-\hat{\mathcal T}h(Z))^2]$. In Proposition~\ref{prop:exist_lambda_iter_trae}, Theorem~\ref{thm:iter_trae_rate}, Lemma~\ref{eq:iter_trae_strong_propagation}, Corollary~\ref{cor:iter_trae_discrepancy_bound}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:iter_trae_accepted_stability}: The loss in the recursion is the adversarial loss $L_n(h)=\sup_{f\in\mathcal F}\mathbb E_n[2m(W;f)-2h(X)f(Z)-f(Z)^2]$. In Lemma~\ref{lemma:iter_rdiv_stability}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:iter_trae_accepted_stability}: The empirical penalty center remains $\hat h_{j-1,\lambda}$; the fixed-center concentration comparisons use population iterates as centers. In \cite[Lemma 14 (lem:lipschitz_wainwright); signed-tail evidence: lem:prob_localized and lem:peeling]{foster2023orthogonal}, used in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lemma:iter_rdiv_stability}, Lemma (Recursive RDIV stability): The empirical iterates use $L_n(h)=\mathbb E_{n,\mathrm{fit}}[(Y-\hat{\mathcal T}h(Z))^2]$ and penalty center $\hat h_{j-1,\lambda}$; the fixed-center concentration comparisons use the deterministic population iterates as comparators and centers. In \cite[Lemma 9 (lem:concentration)]{bennett2023source}, used in the proof of Lemma~\ref{eq:iter_trae_strong_propagation}: The empirical iterates use $L_n(h)=\sup_{f\in\mathcal F}\mathbb E_n[2m(W;f)-2h(X)f(Z)-f(Z)^2]$ and penalty center $\hat h_{j-1,\lambda}$; the fixed-center concentration comparisons use the deterministic population iterates as comparators and centers. — The recursions in \eqref{eq:iter_rdiv_estimator} and the iterative TRAE definition specify the losses and empirical centers. The fixed-center comparisons in the proofs of Lemma~\ref{lemma:iter_rdiv_stability} and Lemma~\ref{lemma:iter_trae_accepted_stability} use population centers.
- reading of the symbol `a_j`: $\{a_j\}_{j=0}^t$ is a deterministic RDIV propagation sequence initialized at $a_0=0$, with $a_j\geqslant1$ for $1\leqslant j\leqslant t$; each positive-index term depends only on $j$ and fixed population quantities and is independent of $n$ and $\lambda$. — Lemma~\ref{lemma:iter_rdiv_stability} specifies the positive-index constants and their dependencies; its proof initializes $a_0=0$ and chooses $a_j=C(1+a_{j-1})$ for fixed $C>1$.
- reading of the symbol `b_j`: $\{b_j\}_{j=0}^t$ is a nondecreasing deterministic propagation sequence with $b_0=0$ and $b_j\geqslant2$ for $1\leqslant j\leqslant t$, each positive-index term depending only on $j$ and the fixed boundedness and mean-square-continuity constants, independently of $\beta$ and $\\|w_0\\|$. — The statement of Lemma~\ref{eq:iter_trae_strong_propagation} gives the index range, monotonicity, and dependencies; its proof chooses $b_j=C(1+b_{j-1})$ with a fixed $C\geqslant2$.
- reading of the symbol `\mathcal E_n^{\mathrm R}`: $\mathcal E_n^{\mathrm R}$ is the intersection of the preliminary, fitting, and calibration events under the paper's confidence convention, with probability at least $1-\xi$, on which the fitting comparisons hold simultaneously for every $h\in\mathcal H$ and $\lambda\in\Lambda_n$, and the projected calibration comparisons hold simultaneously for the fitted candidates $\hat h_\lambda$, $\lambda\in\Lambda_n$. — Lemma~\ref{lemma:err_l_dp} states the simultaneous fitting and projected calibration comparisons. Its proof conditions first on the preliminary sample for fitting and then on preliminary and fitting samples for calibration, and concludes by combining all three event families.
- reading of the symbol `\mathcal E_{n,t}^{\mathrm{IR}}`: $\mathcal E_{n,t}^{\mathrm{IR}}$ is the common preliminary, recursive fitting, and final calibration event constructed with allocation $\eta_{n,t}=\xi/\{t(K_n+1)\}$, of probability at least $1-\xi$, on which the recursive RDIV stability comparisons hold simultaneously for $j=1,\ldots,t$ and $\lambda\in\Lambda_n\cap[\lambda_{\mathrm{IR}}^\circ,2]$, and the projected calibration comparison holds for the final candidates on that same grid segment. — Lemma~\ref{lemma:iter_rdiv_stability} states the probability, index ranges, and common calibration comparison. Its proof unions the recursive comparisons over iteration--grid pairs and intersects their event with the final calibration event under the confidence allocation.
- reading of the symbol `\mathcal E_{n,t}^{\mathrm{IT}}`: $\mathcal E_{n,t}^{\mathrm{IT}}$ is the common iterative TRAE concentration event obtained by intersecting the requisite iteration--grid concentration events with failure-probability allocation $\eta_{n,t}=\xi/\{t(K_n+1)\}$, with probability at least $1-\xi$ under the paper's confidence convention. In Lemma~\ref{eq:iter_trae_strong_propagation}, Corollary~\ref{cor:iter_trae_discrepancy_bound}, Lemma~\ref{lemma:iter_trae_accepted_stability}: The concentration comparisons are simultaneous over the oracle-relevant grid segment $\Lambda_n\cap[\lambda_{\mathrm{IT}}^\circ,2]$, including the recursive comparisons for $j=1,\ldots,t$. — The defining sentence at line 2070 specifies the common event and allocation. The proof of Lemma~\ref{eq:iter_trae_strong_propagation} applies the comparisons at fixed iteration--grid pairs and concludes with a union bound; the confidence convention preceding Theorem~\ref{thm:common_dp} permits fixed numerical rescalings.
- reading of the symbol `b`: $b=\min\{\beta,1\}\in(0,1]$ is the truncated source exponent. — Lemma~\ref{lemma:variational_source} defines $b=\min\{\beta,1\}$. The current squared-norm concentration argument no longer introduces a normalized-class distance called $b$.
- reading of the symbol `r_\lambda`: $r_\lambda(u)=\lambda/(u+\lambda)$ for finite $\lambda>0$ and $u\geqslant0$, and $r_\lambda(\mathcal A)$ denotes this function applied to $\mathcal A$ by spectral functional calculus. — Lemma~\ref{lemma:iter_bias} defines the scalar filter and immediately uses its operator functional calculus in $r_\lambda(\mathcal A)^j$; its proof uses the spectrum of $\mathcal A$ in $[0,1]$.
- reading of the symbol `H^2`: $H^2(g_1,g_2)=\int(\sqrt{g_1(x)}-\sqrt{g_2(x)})^2\,\mu(dx)$ is defined for probability densities $g_1,g_2$ with respect to the common measure $\mu$ on $\mathcal X$; for conditional densities it is evaluated at each fixed $z$, with any averaging over $Z$ written separately. — The proof of Lemma~\ref{lemma:t_err_dp} gives the integral definition, and its statement writes $\mathbb E_Z[H^2\{\hat g(\cdot\mid Z),g_0(\cdot\mid Z)\}]$.
- reading of the symbol `\beta_h`: $\beta_h>0$ is a source exponent satisfying $h_0=(\mathcal T^*\mathcal T)^{\beta_h/2}w_h$ for some $w_h\in\tilde{\mathcal H}$; it need not be the largest exponent for which such a representation exists. — Assumption~\ref{asp:source_condition} defines a source exponent by existence of a source element, and Corollary~\ref{cor:normality} applies that condition to $h_0$ with exponent $\beta_h$.
- reading of the symbol `\beta_q`: $\beta_q>0$ is a source exponent satisfying $q_0=(\mathcal T\mathcal T^*)^{\beta_q/2}w_q$ for some $w_q\in\tilde{\mathcal Q}$; it need not be the largest exponent for which such a representation exists. — The dual equation is $\mathcal T^*q_0=a_0$; Corollary~\ref{cor:normality} applies Assumption~\ref{asp:source_condition} to this dual problem with exponent $\beta_q$.
- reading of the symbol `\boldsymbol y`: $\boldsymbol y$ denotes the vector of outcomes on the fitting block, on the outcome scale used by the fit. In Proposition~\ref{prop:rkhs_qp}: $\boldsymbol y=(Y_1,\ldots,Y_N)^\top$, with $N=n_1$ the fitting-sample size. — Section~\ref{sec:rkhs_computation} defines $\boldsymbol y=(Y_1,\ldots,Y_N)^\top$; the later RDIV computation explicitly uses standardized outcomes.
- added condition (`ass:bk-12`, `thm:iter-trae-rate`): With $\mathcal U$ and $\ell_B$ as in Assumption~\ref{asp:critical_radius}, for every fixed iteration--grid pair $(j,\lambda)$ under consideration and every $r>0$, both $\sup_{u\in\mathcal U,\,\\|u-u_{j,\lambda}^*\\|_{L_2(W;\mathbb R^3)}\leqslant r}\|(\mathbb E_n-\mathbb E)\{\ell_B(u(W))-\ell_B(u_{j,\lambda}^*(W))\}\|$ and $\sup_{u\in\mathcal U,\,\\|u-u_{j,\lambda}^*\\|_{L_2(W;\mathbb R^3)}\leqslant r}\|n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^n\epsilon_i\{\ell_B(u(W_i))-\ell_B(u_{j,\lambda}^*(W_i))\}\|$ are measurable under the fitting-sample law and its product with the independent Rademacher law, respectively, where $f_j^*=\mathcal T(h_0-h_{j,\lambda}^*)$ and $u_{j,\lambda}^*(W)=(m(W;f_j^*),h_{j,\lambda}^*(X)f_j^*(Z),f_j^*(Z))$. — The localized excess-loss concentration uses this nonzero deterministic anchor; the stipulated zero-anchor and fixed-center squared-loss measurability do not supply these suprema.
- added condition (`ass:bk-12`, `lem:iter-trae-strong-propagation`): With $\mathcal U$ and $\ell_B$ as in Assumption~\ref{asp:critical_radius}, for every fixed iteration--grid pair $(j,\lambda)$ under consideration and every $r>0$, both $\sup_{u\in\mathcal U,\,\\|u-u_{j,\lambda}^*\\|_{L_2(W;\mathbb R^3)}\leqslant r}\|(\mathbb E_n-\mathbb E)\{\ell_B(u(W))-\ell_B(u_{j,\lambda}^*(W))\}\|$ and $\sup_{u\in\mathcal U,\,\\|u-u_{j,\lambda}^*\\|_{L_2(W;\mathbb R^3)}\leqslant r}\|n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^n\epsilon_i\{\ell_B(u(W_i))-\ell_B(u_{j,\lambda}^*(W_i))\}\|$ are measurable under the fitting-sample law and its product with the independent Rademacher law, respectively, where $f_j^*=\mathcal T(h_0-h_{j,\lambda}^*)$ and $u_{j,\lambda}^*(W)=(m(W;f_j^*),h_{j,\lambda}^*(X)f_j^*(Z),f_j^*(Z))$. — The localized excess-loss concentration uses this nonzero deterministic anchor; the stipulated zero-anchor and fixed-center squared-loss measurability do not supply these suprema.
- added condition (`ass:bk-12`, `thm:iter-trae-discrepancy-bound`): With $\mathcal U$ and $\ell_B$ as in Assumption~\ref{asp:critical_radius}, for every fixed iteration--grid pair $(j,\lambda)$ under consideration and every $r>0$, both $\sup_{u\in\mathcal U,\,\\|u-u_{j,\lambda}^*\\|_{L_2(W;\mathbb R^3)}\leqslant r}\|(\mathbb E_n-\mathbb E)\{\ell_B(u(W))-\ell_B(u_{j,\lambda}^*(W))\}\|$ and $\sup_{u\in\mathcal U,\,\\|u-u_{j,\lambda}^*\\|_{L_2(W;\mathbb R^3)}\leqslant r}\|n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^n\epsilon_i\{\ell_B(u(W_i))-\ell_B(u_{j,\lambda}^*(W_i))\}\|$ are measurable under the fitting-sample law and its product with the independent Rademacher law, respectively, where $f_j^*=\mathcal T(h_0-h_{j,\lambda}^*)$ and $u_{j,\lambda}^*(W)=(m(W;f_j^*),h_{j,\lambda}^*(X)f_j^*(Z),f_j^*(Z))$. — The localized excess-loss concentration uses this nonzero deterministic anchor; the stipulated zero-anchor and fixed-center squared-loss measurability do not supply these suprema.
- added condition (`ass:bk-12`, `lem:iter-trae-accepted-stability`): With $\mathcal U$ and $\ell_B$ as in Assumption~\ref{asp:critical_radius}, for every fixed iteration--grid pair $(j,\lambda)$ under consideration and every $r>0$, both $\sup_{u\in\mathcal U,\,\\|u-u_{j,\lambda}^*\\|_{L_2(W;\mathbb R^3)}\leqslant r}\|(\mathbb E_n-\mathbb E)\{\ell_B(u(W))-\ell_B(u_{j,\lambda}^*(W))\}\|$ and $\sup_{u\in\mathcal U,\,\\|u-u_{j,\lambda}^*\\|_{L_2(W;\mathbb R^3)}\leqslant r}\|n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^n\epsilon_i\{\ell_B(u(W_i))-\ell_B(u_{j,\lambda}^*(W_i))\}\|$ are measurable under the fitting-sample law and its product with the independent Rademacher law, respectively, where $f_j^*=\mathcal T(h_0-h_{j,\lambda}^*)$ and $u_{j,\lambda}^*(W)=(m(W;f_j^*),h_{j,\lambda}^*(X)f_j^*(Z),f_j^*(Z))$. — The localized excess-loss concentration uses this nonzero deterministic anchor; the stipulated zero-anchor and fixed-center squared-loss measurability do not supply these suprema.

`lem:rdiv-localized-concentration-lemma-14` (foster2023orthogonal, Lemma 14), invoked in the proof of `lem:err-l-dp`:

What Lean assumes for it: `Papers.TsAdaptiveEstimationInference.LipschitzVectorLocalizedConcentration` (`Helpers/RDIVSetup.lean:896`)

```lean
noncomputable def LipschitzVectorLocalizedConcentration
    (V : IVWorld 𝒲 𝒳 𝒵) (P : Measure Ω)
    (sample : AnalysisSample V P) (split : SampleSplit) : Sort 0 :=
  IsProbabilityMeasure P →
  ∀ (mu : Measure 𝒳) (G : Set (𝒳 → 𝒵 → ℝ)) (g₀ : 𝒳 → 𝒵 → ℝ)
    (gHat : ℕ → Ω → 𝒳 → 𝒵 → ℝ) (support : Set (𝒳 × 𝒵))
    (B c₀ CG ρ : ℝ),
    RDIVSetting V mu g₀ → RangeCondition V → H0MemHypothesisClass V →
    DensityRealizable V mu G g₀ → DensityBounds G g₀ support c₀ CG →
    OutcomeBounded V B → HypothesisClassBounded V support B →
    MLEMaximizerSelected V P sample split G gHat →
    RDIVClassesMeasurable V P sample split mu G g₀ gHat →
    RDIVFittingSupremumVersion V P sample split mu gHat ρ →
    ∃ C > 0, ∀ (n : ℕ) (ζ r lam : ℝ), 0 < n →
      ζ ∈ Set.Ioo (0 : ℝ) 1 → lam ∈ regGrid ρ n → lam ∈ Set.Ioc (0 : ℝ) 2 →
      confidenceFloor n ζ ≤ r →
      IsCriticalRadiusBound (V.law.map V.xOf) (primalVersion V '' V.H) n r →
      (∀ g ∈ G, IsCriticalRadiusBound (V.law.map V.zOf)
        (densityOperator mu g '' (primalVersion V '' V.H)) n r) →
      ∀ᵐ preliminary ∂P,
        1 - ζ ≤ P.real {ω | ∀ h ∈ V.H,
          |(conditionalRisk V mu gHat n preliminary lam h -
              conditionalRisk V mu gHat n preliminary lam (minNormSolution V)) -
            (empiricalRisk V P sample split mu (fun _ _ => gHat n preliminary) n ω lam h -
              empiricalRisk V P sample split mu (fun _ _ => gHat n preliminary) n ω lam
                (minNormSolution V))| ≤
          C * (r * inputL2Norm (V.law.map V.zOf)
            (densityOperator mu (gHat n preliminary)
              (primalVersion V h - primalVersion V (minNormSolution V))) +
            r * ‖h - minNormSolution V‖ + r ^ 2)}
```

The passage of the proof:

> For each fixed $\lambda\in\Lambda_n$, apply the localized concentration inequality in \citet[Lemma~14]{foster2023orthogonal} to the two-coordinate class
> \[
> h\longmapsto\{\hat{\mathcal T}h(Z),h(X)\}
> \]
> at the fixed comparator $h_0$ and to the loss
> $\phi_\lambda(y,u,v)=(y-u)^2+\lambda v^2$. This loss is uniformly Lipschitz on the bounded range because $\lambda\leqslant2$. The critical-radius bounds in \cref{asp:cr_deepiv} give
> \[
> \left|\{\widehat R^\lambda(h)-\widehat R^\lambda(h_0)\}
> -\{R_n^\lambda(h)-R_n^\lambda(h_0)\}\right|
> \leqslant C\left[\delta_{n,\mathcal H}
> \{\|\hat{\mathcal T}(h-h_0)\|+\|h-h_0\|\}
> +\delta_{n,\mathcal H}^2\right].
> \]

`lem:rdiv-mle-localized-concentration-li2024` (li2024regularized, Localized Concentration (source label lem:local-concen)), invoked in the proof of `lem:t-err-dp`:

What Lean assumes for it: `Papers.TsAdaptiveEstimationInference.LiLocalizedConcentration` (`Helpers/RDIVSetup.lean:308`)

```lean
noncomputable def LiLocalizedConcentration : Sort 0 :=
  ∀ (mu : Measure 𝒳) (G : Set (𝒳 → 𝒵 → ℝ)) (g₀ : 𝒳 → 𝒵 → ℝ)
    (support : Set (𝒳 × 𝒵)) (c₀ CG : ℝ),
    RDIVSetting V mu g₀ → DensityRealizable V mu G g₀ → DensityBounds G g₀ support c₀ CG →
    LocalizedProcessesMeasurable (V.law.map (fun w => (V.xOf w, V.zOf w)))
      (likelihoodTransformClass G g₀) →
    ∀ (δG : ℕ → ℝ → ℝ), DensityCriticalRadiusBound V G g₀ δG →
    (∃ c > 0, ∀ n : ℕ, 0 < n → ∀ ξ ∈ Set.Ioo (0 : ℝ) 1,
      c * confidenceFloor n ξ ≤ δG n ξ) →
    ∃ A > 0, ∀ n : ℕ, 0 < n → ∀ ξ ∈ Set.Ioo (0 : ℝ) 1,
      ∃ E : Set (Fin n → 𝒳 × 𝒵), MeasurableSet E ∧
        1 - ξ / 2 ≤ (Measure.pi (fun _ : Fin n =>
          V.law.map (fun w => (V.xOf w, V.zOf w)))).real E ∧
        ∀ x ∈ E, ∀ g ∈ G,
          let f := fun y : 𝒳 × 𝒵 =>
            Real.sqrt ((g y.1 y.2 + g₀ y.1 y.2) / (2 * g₀ y.1 y.2))
          |(n : ℝ)⁻¹ * (∑ i, -Real.log (f (x i))) -
            (∫ y, -Real.log (f y) ∂V.law.map (fun w => (V.xOf w, V.zOf w)))| ≤
            A * (δG n (ξ / 2) *
              inputL2Norm (V.law.map (fun w => (V.xOf w, V.zOf w)))
                (f - fun _ => 1) + δG n (ξ / 2) ^ 2)
```

The passage of the proof:

> We establish the density bound directly using the Localized Concentration lemma of \citet{li2024regularized}. Write $P=\mathbb E_{X,Z}$ and $P_n=\mathbb E_{n,\mathrm{pre}}$, and define
> \[
> f_g(x,z)=\sqrt{\frac{g(x\mid z)+g_0(x\mid z)}{2g_0(x\mid z)}},\qquad
> \mathcal U=\{f_g-1:g\in\mathcal G\}.
> \]
> Realizability gives $f_{g_0}=1$, so $0\in\mathcal U$. The density bounds give
> \[
> 2^{-1/2}\leqslant f_g\leqslant M,\qquad
> M=\sqrt{\frac{C_G/c_0+1}{2}}.
> \]
> Thus $\mathcal U$ is uniformly bounded by a fixed constant. Define the globally Lipschitz loss
> \[
> \ell(v)=-\log\bigl(\max\{1+v,2^{-1/2}\}\bigr).
> \]
> The maximum map is $1$-Lipschitz, and the derivative of $-\log t$ has absolute value at most $\sqrt2$ for $t\geqslant2^{-1/2}$. Hence $\ell$ is $\sqrt2$-Lipschitz, $\ell(0)=0$, and $\ell(f_g-1)=-\log f_g$.
> 
> We verify the localized complexity condition for this application. Put $\mathcal S=\operatorname{star}(\mathcal G_0-\mathcal G_0)$ and choose a deterministic critical radius $d\leqslant\delta_{n,\mathcal G}$ as supplied by Assumption~\ref{asp:cr_deepiv}. By the critical-radius definition, $\mathfrak R_n(d;\mathcal S)\leqslant d^2$. For $s\geqslant d$, star-shapedness implies that $(d/s)v\in\mathcal S$ and $\|(d/s)v\|_{L_2(X,Z)}\leqslant d$ whenever $v\in\mathcal S$ and $\|v\|_{L_2(X,Z)}\leqslant s$. Scaling inside the Rademacher supremum therefore gives
> \[
> \mathfrak R_n(s;\mathcal S)
> \leqslant\frac{s}{d}\mathfrak R_n(d;\mathcal S)
> \leqslant sd.
> \]
> Since $1\in\mathcal G_0$, we have $\operatorname{star}(\mathcal U)\subseteq\mathcal S$. Taking $s=\delta_{n,\mathcal G}$ yields
> \[
> \mathfrak R_n\bigl(\delta_{n,\mathcal G};\operatorname{star}(\mathcal U)\bigr)
> \leqslant\delta_{n,\mathcal G}d
> \leqslant\delta_{n,\mathcal G}^2.
> \]
> Apply the cited localized concentration lemma with input $(X,Z)$, one coordinate, class $\mathcal U$, fixed comparator $0$, loss $\ell$, and failure probability $\xi/2$. The measurability and logarithmic radius floor are supplied by Assumption~\ref{asp:cr_deepiv}, with the confidence evaluation specified in the proof. Consequently, for a fixed constant $A>0$, with probability at least $1-\xi/2$,
> \[
> \left|(P_n-P)(-\log f_g)\right|
> \leqslant A\left\{\delta_{n,\mathcal G}\|f_g-1\|_{L_2(X,Z)}+\delta_{n,\mathcal G}^2\right\},
> \qquad g\in\mathcal G.
> \]

`lem:iter-rdiv-fixed-center-concentration-lemma-14` (foster2023orthogonal, Lemma 14 (lem:lipschitz_wainwright); signed-tail evidence: lem:prob_localized and lem:peeling), invoked in the proof of `lem:iter-rdiv-stability`:

What Lean assumes for it: `Papers.TsAdaptiveEstimationInference.IterRDIVFixedCenterLocalizedConcentration` (`Helpers/IterRDIVConcentration.lean:128`)

```lean
noncomputable def IterRDIVFixedCenterLocalizedConcentration : Sort 0 :=
  ∀ (mu : Measure 𝒳) (G : Set (𝒳 → 𝒵 → ℝ)) (g₀ : 𝒳 → 𝒵 → ℝ)
    (support : Set (𝒳 × 𝒵)) (B c₀ CG : ℝ),
    RDIVSetting V mu g₀ → DensityRealizable V mu G g₀ →
    DensityBounds G g₀ support c₀ CG → OutcomeBounded V B →
    HypothesisClassBounded V support B →
    ∃ C > 0, ∃ c₁ > 0, ∃ c₂ > 0,
    ∀ (P : Measure Ω) (_hProbability : IsProbabilityMeasure P)
      (sample : AnalysisSample V P) (split : SampleSplit)
      (gHat : ℕ → Ω → 𝒳 → 𝒵 → ℝ) (ρ : ℝ),
    MLEMaximizerSelected V P sample split G gHat →
    RDIVClassesMeasurable V P sample split mu G g₀ gHat →
    IterRDIVFixedCenterSupremumVersion V P sample split mu gHat ρ →
    ∀ (n t j : ℕ) (lam r : ℝ) (sourceRadius : ℝ)
      (_hSourceScope : gridValue ρ (terminalIndex ρ n) ≤ sourceRadius ∧
        oraclePoint ρ n sourceRadius ≤ lam), 0 < n → 1 ≤ j → j ≤ t →
    lam ∈ regGrid ρ n → lam ∈ Set.Ioc (0 : ℝ) 2 →
    populationIterate V lam j ∈ V.H →
    (populationIterate V lam (j - 1) ∈ V.H ∨ j = 1) →
    0 < r → 1 < 2 * c₂ * n →
    8 * Real.log (41 * Real.log (2 * c₂ * n)) / (c₂ * n) ≤ r ^ 2 →
    iterRdivAnchoredRadius (V.law.map V.xOf) (primalVersion V '' V.H)
      (primalVersion V (populationIterate V lam j)) n r →
    (∀ g ∈ G, iterRdivAnchoredRadius (V.law.map V.zOf)
      (densityOperator mu g '' (primalVersion V '' V.H))
      (densityOperator mu g (primalVersion V (populationIterate V lam j))) n r) →
    ∀ᵐ a ∂P, ∃ E : Set (Fin n → 𝒲), MeasurableSet E ∧
      1 - 2 * c₁ * Real.exp (-c₂ * n * r ^ 2 / 8) ≤
        (Measure.pi (fun _ : Fin n => V.law)).real E ∧
      ∀ ws ∈ E, ∀ h ∈ V.H,
        let increment := iterRdivFixedCenterLoss V mu (gHat n a) lam j h -
          iterRdivFixedCenterLoss V mu (gHat n a) lam j (populationIterate V lam j)
        |(n : ℝ)⁻¹ * (∑ i, increment (ws i)) - ∫ w, increment w ∂V.law| ≤
          C * r * (inputL2Norm (V.law.map V.zOf)
            (densityOperator mu (gHat n a) (primalVersion V h -
              primalVersion V (populationIterate V lam j))) +
            ‖h - populationIterate V lam j‖ + r)
```

The passage of the proof:

> The fitting loss is Lipschitz in the two coordinates
> $\{\hat{\mathcal T}h(Z),h(X)\}$, uniformly over these bounded centers. This is the fixed-comparator fitting application of \citet[Lemma~14]{foster2023orthogonal}, with comparator $h_{j,\lambda}^*$ and penalty center $h_{j-1,\lambda}^*$.

`lem:iter-rdiv-squared-loss-concentration-lemma-14` (foster2023orthogonal, Lemma 14 (lem:lipschitz_wainwright); signed-tail evidence: lem:prob_localized and lem:peeling), invoked in the proof of `lem:iter-rdiv-stability`:

What Lean assumes for it: `Papers.TsAdaptiveEstimationInference.IterRDIVSquaredLossLocalizedConcentration` (`Helpers/IterRDIVConcentration.lean:187`)

```lean
noncomputable def IterRDIVSquaredLossLocalizedConcentration : Sort 0 :=
  ∀ (mu : Measure 𝒳) (G : Set (𝒳 → 𝒵 → ℝ)) (g₀ : 𝒳 → 𝒵 → ℝ)
    (support : Set (𝒳 × 𝒵)) (B c₀ CG : ℝ),
    RDIVSetting V mu g₀ → DensityRealizable V mu G g₀ →
    DensityBounds G g₀ support c₀ CG → HypothesisClassBounded V support B →
    ∃ C > 0, ∃ c₁ > 0, ∃ c₂ > 0,
    ∀ (P : Measure Ω) (_hProbability : IsProbabilityMeasure P)
      (sample : AnalysisSample V P) (split : SampleSplit)
      (gHat : ℕ → Ω → 𝒳 → 𝒵 → ℝ) (ρ : ℝ),
    RDIVClassesMeasurable V P sample split mu G g₀ gHat →
    IterRDIVFixedCenterSupremumVersion V P sample split mu gHat ρ →
    ∀ (n t k : ℕ) (lam r : ℝ) (sourceRadius : ℝ)
      (_hSourceScope : gridValue ρ (terminalIndex ρ n) ≤ sourceRadius ∧
        oraclePoint ρ n sourceRadius ≤ lam), 0 < n → 1 ≤ k → k ≤ t →
    lam ∈ regGrid ρ n → lam ∈ Set.Ioc (0 : ℝ) 2 →
    populationIterate V lam k ∈ V.H →
    0 < r → 1 < 2 * c₂ * n →
    4 * Real.log (41 * Real.log (2 * c₂ * n)) / (c₂ * n) ≤ r ^ 2 →
    iterRdivAnchoredRadius (V.law.map V.xOf) (primalVersion V '' V.H)
      (primalVersion V (populationIterate V lam k)) n r →
    ∀ᵐ a ∂P, ∃ E : Set (Fin n → 𝒲), MeasurableSet E ∧
      1 - 2 * c₁ * Real.exp (-c₂ * n * r ^ 2 / 4) ≤
        (Measure.pi (fun _ : Fin n => V.law)).real E ∧
      ∀ ws ∈ E, ∀ h ∈ V.H,
        |(n : ℝ)⁻¹ * (∑ i, (primalVersion V h (V.xOf (ws i)) -
          primalVersion V (populationIterate V lam k) (V.xOf (ws i))) ^ 2) -
          ‖h - populationIterate V lam k‖ ^ 2| ≤
          C * r * (‖h - populationIterate V lam k‖ + r)
```

The passage of the proof:

> Apply the localized squared-loss comparison to
> $(h-h_{k,\lambda}^*)^2$, whose comparator and center are fixed. Here the localized squared-loss comparison is the scalar application of \citet[Lemma~14]{foster2023orthogonal} at the fixed comparator $h_{k,\lambda}^*$.

`lem:trae-localized-concentration-lemma-9` (bennett2023source, Lemma 9), invoked in the proof of `lem:concentrate-l`:

What Lean assumes for it: `Papers.TsAdaptiveEstimationInference.TRAELocalizedConcentration` (`Helpers/TRAESetup.lean:845`)

```lean
noncomputable def TRAELocalizedConcentration : Sort 0 :=
  ∀ (B : ℝ) (tildeδ : ℕ → ℝ → ℝ),
    RangeCondition V → ClosenessCondition V → MeanSquareContinuity V →
    TRAEBoundedness V B → TRAECommonNullSetBoundedness V B → TRAECriticalRadiusBound V tildeδ →
    ∃ C > 0, ∀ (δ : ℕ → ℝ) (allocation : ℕ → ℝ),
    TRAEScaleCalibration δ tildeδ allocation →
    ∀ (n : ℕ), 0 < n → allocation n ∈ Set.Ioo (0 : ℝ) 1 →
    ∃ E : Set (Fin n → 𝒲), MeasurableSet E ∧
      1 - allocation n ≤ (Measure.pi (fun _ : Fin n => V.law)).real E ∧
      ∀ x ∈ E, ∀ h ∈ V.H, ∀ f ∈ V.F,
        let u := fun w => ![V.m f w,
          primalVersion V h (V.xOf w) * dualVersion V f (V.zOf w),
          dualVersion V f (V.zOf w)]
        |(n : ℝ)⁻¹ * (∑ i, traeClippedLoss B (x i) (u (x i))) -
          (∫ w, traeClippedLoss B w (u w) ∂V.law)| ≤
          C * (δ n * (∑ i : Fin 3, inputL2Norm V.law (fun w => u w i)) + δ n ^ 2)
```

The passage of the proof:

> The localized concentration bound used in \citet[Lemma~9]{bennett2023source}
> gives
> \begin{align*}
> L( h) &=\sup_{f\in \mathcal{F}}\mathbb{E}\left[ 2( m( W;f) -f( Z) h( X)) -f^{2}( Z)\right] \\
> & =\mathbb{E}\left[ 2( m( W;f_{h}) -f_{h}( Z) h( X)) -f_{h}^{2}( Z)\right]\\
>  & \leqslant \mathbb{E}_{n}\left[ 2( m( W;f_{h}) -f_{h}( Z) h( X)) -f_{h}^{2}( Z)\right] +O\left( \delta _{n}^{2} +\delta _{n} \| f_{h} \| \right)\\
>  & \leqslant \sup_{f\in \mathcal{F}}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[ 2( m( W;f) -f( Z) h( X)) -f^{2}( Z)\right] +O\left( \delta _{n}^{2} +\delta _{n} \| f_{h} \| \right)\\
>  & \leqslant L_{n}( h) +O\left( \delta _{n}^{2} +\delta _{n} \| f_{h} \| \right) .
> \end{align*}
> Since $\|f_h\|^2=L(h)$, Young's inequality gives
> \[
> L( h) \leqslant 2L_{n}( h) +O\left( \delta _{n}^{2}\right) .
> \]
> Similarly, 
> \begin{align*}
> L_{n}( h)&  =\sup_{f\in \mathcal{F}}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[ 2( m( W;f) -f( Z) h( X)) -f^{2}( Z)\right] \\
>  & \leqslant \sup_{f\in\mathcal F}\left\{\mathbb E\left[2(m(W;f)-f(Z)h(X))-f(Z)^2\right]+C\left(\delta_n^2+\delta_n\|f\|\right)\right\}\\
>  & \leqslant \sup_{f\in\widetilde{\mathcal Q}}\mathbb{E}\left[ 2( m( W;f) -f( Z) h( X)) -\frac{4}{5} f^{2}( Z)\right] +O\left( \delta _{n}^{2}\right)\\
>  & =\frac{5}{4}\|\mathcal T(h_0-h)\|^2+O\left( \delta _{n}^{2}\right)\\
>  & =\frac{5}{4} L( h) +O\left( \delta _{n}^{2}\right) .
> \end{align*}

`lem:trae-squared-norm-lemma-14` (foster2023orthogonal, Lemma 14), invoked in the proof of `lem:strong-convexity`:

What Lean assumes for it: `Papers.TsAdaptiveEstimationInference.ScalarLipschitzLocalizedConcentration` (`Helpers/TRAESetup.lean:937`)

```lean
noncomputable def ScalarLipschitzLocalizedConcentration
    (V : IVWorld 𝒲 𝒳 𝒵) (P : Measure Ω)
    (sample : AnalysisSample V P) (split : SampleSplit) : Sort 0 :=
  IsProbabilityMeasure P →
  ∀ (B : ℝ) (tildeδ : ℕ → ℝ → ℝ),
  TRAEBoundedness V B → TRAECommonNullSetBoundedness V B → TRAECriticalRadiusBound V tildeδ →
  ∃ C > 0, ∀ (δ allocation : ℕ → ℝ),
  H0MemHypothesisClass V → TRAEScaleCalibration δ tildeδ allocation →
  ∀ n : ℕ, 0 < n → allocation n ∈ Set.Ioo (0 : ℝ) 1 →
    ∃ E : Set Ω, MeasurableSet E ∧ 1 - allocation n ≤ P.real E ∧
      ∀ ω ∈ E, ∀ h ∈ V.H,
        |(primalEmpSq V P sample split n ω h -
            primalEmpSq V P sample split n ω (minNormSolution V)) -
          (‖h‖ ^ 2 - ‖minNormSolution V‖ ^ 2)| ≤
            C * (δ n * ‖h - minNormSolution V‖ + δ n ^ 2)
```

The passage of the proof:

> The localized squared-norm inequality
> in \citet[Lemma~14]{foster2023orthogonal} gives, uniformly over
> $h\in\mathcal H$,
> \[
> \left|\{\|h\|_{2,n}^2-\|h_0\|_{2,n}^2\}
> -\{\|h\|^2-\|h_0\|^2\}\right|
> \leqslant C\{\delta_n\|h-h_0\|+\delta_n^2\}.
> \]

`lem:iter-trae-anchored-concentration-lemma-9` (bennett2023source, Lemma 9 (lem:concentration)), invoked in the proof of `lem:iter-trae-strong-propagation`:

What Lean assumes for it: `Papers.TsAdaptiveEstimationInference.IterTRAEAnchoredLocalizedConcentration` (`Helpers/IterTRAEConcentration.lean:92`)

```lean
noncomputable def IterTRAEAnchoredLocalizedConcentration : Sort 0 :=
  ∀ B cfloor : ℝ, TRAEBoundedness V B → TRAECommonNullSetBoundedness V B → 0 < cfloor →
  ∃ C > 0, ∀ n : ℕ, 3 ≤ n → ∀ ζ ∈ Set.Ioo (0 : ℝ) 1,
  ∀ r : ℝ, cfloor * Real.sqrt
    ((3 * Real.log (Real.log n) + Real.log (1 / ζ)) / n) ≤ r →
  0 < r →
  (∀ F ∈ ({starClass (momentImageClass V),
      starClass (productObservationClass V), starClass (criticObservationClass V)} :
        Set (Set (𝒲 → ℝ))),
    IsCriticalRadiusBound V.law F n r) →
  ∀ (ρ c b β : ℝ) (δ : ℕ → ℝ) (t : ℕ),
  GridDomain ρ → 0 < c → 2 ≤ b → 0 < β → 1 ≤ t →
  let radius := c * (b * δ n ^ 2) ^ (1 / (qualificationExponent β t + 1))
  ∀ anchor : 𝒲 → Fin 3 → ℝ,
  (anchor = 0 ∨ ∃ j : ℕ, 1 ≤ j ∧ j ≤ t ∧
    ∃ lam ∈ regGrid ρ n,
      (∃ k ≤ terminalIndex ρ n, gridValue ρ k ≤ radius) ∧
      oraclePoint ρ n radius ≤ lam ∧ lam ≤ 2 ∧ 0 < lam ∧
      populationIterate V lam j ∈ V.H ∧
      projCondExpOp V (minNormSolution V - populationIterate V lam j) ∈ V.F ∧
      anchor = iterTraePopulationAnchor V j lam) →
  anchor ∈ traeLossClass V →
  AELocalizedSupremaMeasurableAt V.law (traeLossClass V) anchor (traeClippedLoss B) n →
  ∃ E : Set (Fin n → 𝒲), MeasurableSet E ∧
    1 - ζ ≤ (Measure.pi (fun _ : Fin n => V.law)).real E ∧
    ∀ x ∈ E, ∀ u ∈ traeLossClass V,
      |(n : ℝ)⁻¹ * (∑ i, (traeClippedLoss B (x i) (u (x i)) -
          traeClippedLoss B (x i) (anchor (x i)))) -
        ∫ w, (traeClippedLoss B w (u w) - traeClippedLoss B w (anchor w)) ∂V.law| ≤
      C * (r * (∑ i : Fin 3, inputL2Norm V.law (fun w => u w i - anchor w i)) + r ^ 2)
```

The passage of the proof:

> Apply the localized concentration inequality of \citet[Lemma~9]{bennett2023source} to the product and moment-image classes in \cref{asp:critical_radius}, and apply its bounded Lipschitz version to the squared test functions.

`lem:iter-trae-fixed-center-squared-loss-lemma-9` (bennett2023source, Lemma 9 (lem:concentration)), invoked in the proof of `lem:iter-trae-strong-propagation`:

What Lean assumes for it: `Papers.TsAdaptiveEstimationInference.IterTRAEFixedCenterSquaredLossConcentration` (`Helpers/IterTRAEConcentration.lean:214`)

```lean
noncomputable def IterTRAEFixedCenterSquaredLossConcentration : Sort 0 :=
  ∀ B cfloor : ℝ, TRAEBoundedness V B → TRAECommonNullSetBoundedness V B → 0 < cfloor →
  ∃ C > 0, ∀ n : ℕ, 3 ≤ n → ∀ ζ ∈ Set.Ioo (0 : ℝ) 1,
  ∀ r : ℝ, cfloor * Real.sqrt
    ((Real.log (Real.log n) + Real.log (1 / ζ)) / n) ≤ r → 0 < r →
  ∀ (F : Set (𝒲 → ℝ)) (anchor center : 𝒲 → ℝ),
  IterTRAEScalarSourceApplication V n F anchor center →
  anchor ∈ F →
  (∀ᵐ w ∂V.law, ∀ f ∈ F, |f w| ≤ 2 * max 1 B) →
  (∀ w (u v : Fin 1 → ℝ),
    |iterTraeSourceSquare B center w u - iterTraeSourceSquare B center w v| ≤
      |u 0 - v 0|) →
  expectedRademacher V.law {f ∈ starClass F | eLpNorm f 2 V.law ≤ ENNReal.ofReal r} n ≤
    ENNReal.ofReal (r ^ 2) →
  AELocalizedSupremaMeasurableAt V.law (iterTraeScalarClass F)
    (fun w => fun _ : Fin 1 => anchor w) (iterTraeSourceSquare B center) n →
  ∃ E : Set (Fin n → 𝒲), MeasurableSet E ∧
    1 - ζ ≤ (Measure.pi (fun _ : Fin n => V.law)).real E ∧
    ∀ x ∈ E, ∀ f ∈ F,
      |(n : ℝ)⁻¹ * (∑ i,
          (iterTraeSourceSquare B center (x i) (fun _ => f (x i)) -
           iterTraeSourceSquare B center (x i) (fun _ => anchor (x i)))) -
        ∫ w, (iterTraeSourceSquare B center w (fun _ => f w) -
          iterTraeSourceSquare B center w (fun _ => anchor w)) ∂V.law| ≤
      C * (r * inputL2Norm V.law (fun w => f w - anchor w) + r ^ 2)
```

The passage of the proof:

> For the fixed-center squared-loss comparisons, apply the same localized concentration inequality of \citet[Lemma~9]{bennett2023source} to the bounded clipped losses corresponding to $(h-h_{j-1,\lambda}^*)^2$ at comparator $h_{j,\lambda}^*$ and $(h-h_{k,\lambda}^*)^2$ at comparator $h_{k,\lambda}^*$, for the fixed iteration--grid pairs under consideration.

The original filler named the Lean helper traeLocalizedDeviationBoth rather than a patchable core node; the orchestrator corrects only that routing error. minimal patch (confirmed): corrected statement: For a fixed $B<\infty$, suppose that $\|m(W;f)\|$, $\|h(X)\|$, and $\|f(Z)\|$ are a.s. bounded by $B$, uniformly over $ h\in \mathcal{H} ,f\in \mathcal{F}$. Suppose in addition that, for every integer $n\geqslant1$ and every $r>0$, the localized squared-loss suprema \[ \sup_{\substack{h\in\mathcal H\\ \\|h-h_0\\|\leqslant r}} \left\|(\mathbb E_n-\mathbb E)[h(X)^2-h_0(X)^2]\right\| \quad\text{and}\quad \sup_{\substack{h\in\mathcal H\\ \\|h-h_0\\|\leqslant r}} \left\|\frac1n\sum_{i=1}^n\epsilon_i\{h(X_i)^2-h_0(X_i)^2\}\right\| \] are measurable random variables under the fitting-sample law and its product with the independent Rademacher law, respectively. Regard the functions in $\mathcal H\cdotp\mathcal F$ and $\mathcal F$ as functions of $W$ through $(X,Z)$ and $Z$, respectively, and put \[ \mathcal U=\operatorname{star}(m\circ\mathcal F) \times\operatorname{star}(\mathcal H\cdotp\mathcal F) \times\operatorname{star}(\mathcal F), \qquad \\|u\\|_{L_2(W;\mathbb R^3)}^2 =\mathbb E\!\left[\sum_{j=1}^3u_j(W)^2\right]. \] Define the loss with zero comparator by \[ \operatorname{clip}_B(a)=\max\{-B,\min\{a,B\}\}, \qquad \ell_B(u)=2u_1-2u_2-\operatorname{clip}_B(u_3)^2, \qquad \ell_B(0)=0. \] Suppose also that, for every integer $n\geqslant1$ and every $r>0$, the localized loss suprema \[ \sup_{\substack{u\in\mathcal U\\ \\|u\\|_{L_2(W;\mathbb R^3)}\leqslant r}} \left\|(\mathbb E_n-\mathbb E)[\ell_B(u(W))-\ell_B(0)]\right\| \quad\text{and}\quad \sup_{\substack{u\in\mathcal U\\ \\|u\\|_{L_2(W;\mathbb R^3)}\leqslant r}} \left\|\frac1n\sum_{i=1}^n\epsilon_i\{\ell_B(u(W_i))-\ell_B(0)\}\right\| \] are measurable random variables under the fitting-sample law and its product with the independent Rademacher law, respectively. Whenever the primal and dual roles are exchanged, suppose that these same two localized loss suprema for the exchanged problem are measurable under its fitting-sample law and its product with the independent Rademacher law, respectively. For iterative TRAE, suppose in addition that the following fixed-population-center squared-loss processes are measurable. For every integer $n\geqslant1$, prescribed integer $t\geqslant1$, grid point $\lambda\in\Lambda_n\cap[\lambda_{\mathrm{IT}}^\circ,2]$ for which $\lambda_{\mathrm{IT}}^\circ$ is defined, radius $r>0$, and $j=1,\ldots,t$, put \[ a_{j,\lambda}(h;X) =\{h(X)-h_{j-1,\lambda}^*(X)\}^2 -\{h_{j,\lambda}^*(X)-h_{j-1,\lambda}^*(X)\}^2, \qquad d_{j,\lambda}(h;X)=\{h(X)-h_{j,\lambda}^*(X)\}^2, \] where the population iterates are defined by \eqref{eq:iter_population}. For each $v\in\{a_{j,\lambda},d_{j,\lambda}\}$, suppose that both \[ \sup_{\substack{h\in\mathcal H\\\\|h-h_{j,\lambda}^*\\|\leqslant r}} \left\|(\mathbb E_n-\mathbb E)v(h;X)\right\| \quad\text{and}\quad \sup_{\substack{h\in\mathcal H\\\\|h-h_{j,\lambda}^*\\|\leqslant r}} \left\|\frac1n\sum_{i=1}^n\epsilon_i v(h;X_i)\right\| \] are measurable under the fitting-sample law and its product with the independent Rademacher law, respectively, using the same function representatives as the empirical penalties. The zero-iterate comparison uses $\hat h_{0,\lambda}=h_{0,\lambda}^*=0$. · proof change: The proof as printed proves the repaired statement; no step changes.
- run-level `lem:trae-squared-norm-lemma-14`: [refuted, V1.5] [F2.5 reviewer] [statement-wrong] The carrier assumes class-level squared-loss process measurability absent from the frozen TRAE setting; if this regularity is necessary, it requires an explicit referee-approved condition rather than a carrier-only premise. witness: [statement-wrong] The carrier assumes class-level squared-loss process measurability absent from the frozen TRAE setting; if this regularity is necessary, it requires an explicit referee-approved condition rather than a carrier-only premise. minimal patch (confirmed): proof change: We use the squared-loss measurability condition in Assumption~\ref{asp:critical_radius}. Put $M=\max\{1,B\}$, $\mathcal U=\{h/M:h\in\mathcal H\}$, and $u_0=h_0/M$. Assumption~\ref{asp:closeness_deepiv} gives $u_0\in\mathcal U$, and the boundedness condition gives $\|u(X)\|\leqslant1$ uniformly over $u\in\mathcal U$. Define the globally Lipschitz loss \[ \ell(v)=M^2\bigl(\max\{-1,\min\{v,1\}\}\bigr)^2. \] The clipping map is $1$-Lipschitz, and $\|a^2-b^2\|\leqslant2\|a-b\|$ for $a,b\in[-1,1]$. Hence $\ell$ is $L$-Lipschitz with $L=2M^2$, and $\ell(h(X)/M)=h(X)^2$.  We verify the anchored complexity condition. Set $\mathcal S=\operatorname{star}(\mathcal H-\mathcal H)$. By Assumption~\ref{asp:critical_radius} and \eqref{eq:delta_trae}, there is a deterministic critical radius $d\leqslant\delta_n$ for $\mathcal S$. Since $0\in\mathcal S$, we have $\mathcal S\subset\operatorname{star}(\mathcal S-\mathcal S)$, and the critical-radius definition implies \[ \mathfrak R_n(d;\mathcal S)\leqslant d^2. \] For $s\geqslant d$, star-shapedness shows that $(d/s)g\in\mathcal S$ and $\\|(d/s)g\\|\leqslant d$ whenever $g\in\mathcal S$ and $\\|g\\|\leqslant s$. Applying this scaling inside the Rademacher supremum gives \[ \mathfrak R_n(s;\mathcal S) \leqslant\frac{s}{d}\mathfrak R_n(d;\mathcal S) \leqslant sd. \] Also, $h_0\in\mathcal H$ implies $\operatorname{star}(\mathcal H-h_0)\subset\mathcal S$. Therefore, for every $s\geqslant\delta_n$, \[ \begin{split} \mathfrak R_n\bigl(s;\operatorname{star}(\mathcal U-u_0)\bigr) &=M^{-1}\mathfrak R_n\bigl(Ms;\operatorname{star}(\mathcal H-h_0)\bigr)\\ &\leqslant M^{-1}\mathfrak R_n(Ms;\mathcal S) \leqslant sd\leqslant s\delta_n. \end{split} \] In particular, every $r\geqslant\delta_n$ satisfies the anchored fixed-point inequality for the normalized class.  For $R>0$, define \[ Z_n(R)=\sup_{\substack{u\in\mathcal U\\\\|u-u_0\\|\leqslant R}} \left\|(\mathbb E_n-\mathbb E)[\ell(u(X))-\ell(u_0(X))]\right\|. \] Its empirical and Rademacher suprema are precisely the squared-loss suprema in Assumption~\ref{asp:critical_radius}, with localization radius $MR$, and thus are measurable. The localized concentration inequality used in the proof of \citet[Lemma~14]{foster2023orthogonal}, applied to this measurable loss process and the anchored fixed point just verified, gives universal constants $c_1,c_2>0$ such that, for $R\geqslant r\geqslant\delta_n$ and $v>0$, \[ \mathbb P\{Z_n(R)\geqslant16LRr+v\} \leqslant c_1\exp\left\{-\frac{c_2nv^2}{L^2R^2+2Lv}\right\}. \] Taking $v=LRr$ and using $r\leqslant R$ yields \[ \mathbb P\{Z_n(R)\geqslant17LRr\} \leqslant c_1\exp\{-c_2nr^2/3\}. \]  Choose $r=a\delta_n$ with a fixed sufficiently large $a\geqslant1$. If $r\geqslant2$, set $J=0$. If $r<2$, set $J=\lceil\log(2/r)/\log2\rceil$. For $j=0,\ldots,J$, put $R_j=2^jr$. A union bound shows that the measurable event \[ Z_n(R_j)<17LR_jr,\qquad j=0,\ldots,J, \] has failure probability at most $(J+1)c_1\exp\{-c_2nr^2/3\}$. Since $r\geqslant\delta_n\geqslant n^{-1/2}$, there is a universal constant $C_0$ such that $J+1\leqslant C_0\log(en)$. Moreover, \eqref{eq:delta_trae} gives $n\delta_n^2\geqslant1$ and a fixed positive lower bound for $n\delta_n^2/\{\log\log(en)+\log(1/\eta_n)\}$. Consequently, a fixed sufficiently large $a$ makes this failure probability at most $\eta_n/4$.  On this event, let $u\in\mathcal U$ and write $b=\\|u-u_0\\|$. If $b\leqslant r$, then the loss increment is bounded by $Z_n(r)<17Lr^2$. If $b>r$, boundedness gives $b\leqslant2$, so there is an index $j\in\{1,\ldots,J\}$ with $R_{j-1}<b\leqslant R_j$. For that index, $R_j<2b$, and the loss increment is bounded by $Z_n(R_j)<34Lrb$. Thus, in both cases, \[ \left\|(\mathbb E_n-\mathbb E)[\ell(u(X))-\ell(u_0(X))]\right\| \leqslant34Lr(b+r). \] Substituting $u=h/M$, $L=2M^2$, and $r=a\delta_n$ proves, uniformly over $h\in\mathcal H$, \[ \begin{split} \left\|\{\\|h\\|_{2,n}^2-\\|h_0\\|_{2,n}^2\} -\{\\|h\\|^2-\\|h_0\\|^2\}\right\| &\leqslant68M^2a\delta_n\{\\|h-h_0\\|/M+a\delta_n\}\\ &\leqslant C\{\delta_n\\|h-h_0\\|+\delta_n^2\}, \end{split} \] where $C$ depends only on the fixed boundedness and calibration constants. Allocating $\eta_n/4$ to this comparison at each grid point uses at most $(K_n+1)\eta_n/4=\xi/4$ of the total failure probability. This supplies the squared-norm comparison included in $\mathcal E_n$.
